## Phase 2 — stages

P2.1 trade-off study → H2 (B2 now, B3 follow-on, int8 default). P2.2 spike. P2.3 local backend. P2.4 downloader, Models page, default flip. P2.3b panel fix-ups. P2.5 espeak-ng for unknown words (B3). P2.4b panel fix-ups. P2.6 close-out. P2.7 DoD fix-ups (seam pauses, overlay ×). P2.8 whole-sentence inference. The P2.2–P2.4b notes below are condensed; the full text (edge-case lists, live-run logs) is in `git show 948c68d:PLAN.md`.

## Phase 2 — P2.1 Phonemisation trade-off study

Written 2026-10-13 from web research (URLs inline) plus a timing spike run in the scratchpad (`kspike/`, Python `onnxruntime` 1.31.0 + `kokoro-onnx` 0.6.1, Homebrew espeak-ng 1.52, this M4 Pro 10P+4E). No source file changed; no dependency added.

### Facts that frame every option

- **`ort`** ([pykeio/ort](https://github.com/pykeio/ort)): no 2.0 final; latest `2.0.0-rc.14` (2026-10-12, ONNX Runtime 1.31.0), MIT/Apache-2.0. Every rc broke the API (rc.14: `ort::init().build()` env is mandatory; `SessionBuilder` defaults to `AutoDevicePolicy::MaxEfficiency`, which silently routes to CoreML on macOS and breaks Kokoro's zero-size slices — [#662](https://github.com/pykeio/ort/issues/662); use `PreferCPU`). Pin `=2.0.0-rc.14`. Default `download-binaries` fetches a 9.7 MB archive from pyke's CDN at build time → static `libonnxruntime.a` (83 MB, CoreML built in); **+19 MB on a stripped arm64 release binary** (measured by the research agent; Mouthy.app is 9.6 MB today). Only `aarch64-apple-darwin` is prebuilt — no x86_64/universal without a source build or `load-dynamic` (`ORT_DYLIB_PATH`, Microsoft dylib 44.8 MB). [Linking docs](https://ort.pyke.io/setup/linking).
- **CoreML/Metal:** ONNX Runtime has no Metal/MPS EP; WebGPU EP (Dawn→Metal) is marked experimental. The CoreML EP accepts only ~1050/2478 Kokoro nodes (STFT, LSTM, zero-size slices, dynamic sequence length) — [kokoro-onnx #16](https://github.com/thewh1teagle/kokoro-onnx/issues/16), [#56](https://github.com/thewh1teagle/kokoro-onnx/issues/56). **Measured here:** CoreML EP was equal or slower than CPU and added 3–4 s session-load time. Treat Phase 2 as CPU-only; CoreML gains need a native Core ML conversion (out of scope).
- **Model files** (Apache-2.0): [onnx-community/Kokoro-82M-v1.0-ONNX](https://huggingface.co/onnx-community/Kokoro-82M-v1.0-ONNX) `onnx/model.onnx` 325,532,232 B (fp32), `model_fp16.onnx` 163,234,740, `model_quantized.onnx` 92,361,116 (int8); inputs `input_ids` i64 [1,n], `style` f32 [1,256], `speed` f32 [1]; output `waveform` 24 kHz; ≤510 tokens. Voices: `voices/<id>.bin`, raw little-endian f32, 522,240 B each, reshape (510,1,256), row = token count — **no npz reader needed**. sha256 for P2.3 = `lfs.oid` from `GET https://huggingface.co/api/models/onnx-community/Kokoro-82M-v1.0-ONNX/tree/main/onnx` (verified). `bm_lewis` exists (hexgrad grade D+; `bf_emma` B- is the best British voice). No Kokoro English release after v1.0 (Jan 2025).
- **espeak-ng is GPL-3.0-or-later.** FSF reads static, dynamic and dlopen linking all as a combined work ([GPL FAQ](https://www.gnu.org/licenses/gpl-faq.en.html#GPLStaticVsDynamic)); a subprocess exchanging plain text over argv/stdout is "mere aggregation". Every Rust binding (`espeak-rs`/`espeak-rs-sys` 0.2.0 vendors + statically compiles espeak-ng via cmake; `espeakng-sys` 0.3.0 links a system lib with no pkg-config) is MIT-labelled but inherits GPL. Precedent: Piper moved to `piper1-gpl` once it embedded espeak-ng; sherpa-onnx is removing espeak-ng for 2.0 as "incompatible with Apache-2.0" ([#3731](https://github.com/k2-fsa/sherpa-onnx/issues/3731)). Homebrew `espeak-ng` 1.52.0 is installed on this machine (`/opt/homebrew/lib/libespeak-ng.dylib`, data `/opt/homebrew/share/espeak-ng-data`).
- **Pure-Rust G2P exists and is MIT:** [`misaki-rs`](https://github.com/MicheleYin/misaki-rs) 0.6.0 (2026-09-02, 77k downloads) ports hexgrad's misaki (Apache-2.0): embedded US+GB gold/silver lexicons (~197k GB forms), perceptron POS tagger, num2words; `default-features = false` drops `espeak-rs`, and OOV words are then spelled letter-by-letter. [`loqui-g2p`](https://github.com/Awakened-Labs/loqui) 0.3.0 (2026-10-04, 1 contributor, 273 downloads) is the same idea plus an embedded pure-Rust neural OOV fallback and `Dialect::for_kokoro_voice("bm_…")`. Both crates are ~9 MB (lexicons).
- **Rust Kokoro crates** all wrap `ort` and differ only in G2P: Kokoros (git-only, Apache-2.0, `espeak-rs` static GPL, 16 deps incl. tokio/reqwest/mp3lame, unpinned `ort` that drifted to rc.14 and broke, [#121](https://github.com/lucasjinreal/Kokoros/issues/121)); kokorox (now GPL-3.0); kokoro-tts 0.3.3 (US dict only, hard-codes CUDA EP); kokoro-en 0.1.5 (misaki-rs + ort coreml, 12 deps, GB via a global env var); loqui-kokoro 0.3.0 (4 deps, `ort =rc.13`, no streaming, returns whole `Vec<f32>`); kokoro-micro 1.3.0 (pure-Rust lexicon, open macOS teardown crash). None pins rc.14; most pull tokio.

### Comparison

| Option                                                              | Binary size                                                  | Licence                                                                                    | Maintenance risk                                                                                                                             | macOS build                             | CoreML/Metal                                     | Phoneme quality (GB)                                                 | First audio, 5-word sentence (M4 Pro)     | New Cargo deps                                                                                         |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------ | -------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| **A1** `kokoros` git dep                                            | +19 MB ORT, +~3 MB espeak-ng + data dir that must be bundled | app becomes GPL-3 in practice (static espeak-ng)                                           | high: no crates.io release, unpinned `ort`, 45 open issues, path-baked data dir ([#131](https://github.com/lucasjinreal/Kokoros/issues/131)) | cmake + clang for espeak-ng; arm64 only | none                                             | good (espeak `en-gb`, must pass lang manually)                       | ~300–400 ms                               | 16+ (tokio, reqwest dup, hound, mp3lame, opus, ogg, uuid, …)                                           |
| **A2** fork/vendor `loqui-kokoro` or `kokoro-en`                    | +19 MB ORT, +9 MB lexicon                                    | MIT                                                                                        | medium: we own ~1k lines; inherit their `ort` pin churn                                                                                      | pure Rust                               | none (kokoro-en has CoreML plumbing, no benefit) | good (lexicon) / OOV spelled or neural                               | ~300–400 ms                               | 6–12                                                                                                   |
| **B1** `ort` + espeak-ng dynamic link (libloading → Homebrew dylib) | +19 MB                                                       | contested: FSF says combined work even via dlopen; user-supplied lib is a minority defence | medium: hand-rolled FFI to 5 functions + IPA→Kokoro remap; user must `brew install espeak-ng`                                                | pure Rust + runtime dlopen              | none                                             | best (espeak `en-gb` everywhere)                                     | ~300–400 ms (+0.5 s one-off espeak init)  | 2 (`ort`, `libloading` — already in lock)                                                              |
| **B2** `ort` + `misaki-rs` (no espeak)                              | +19 MB, +9 MB lexicon                                        | MIT clean                                                                                  | low: 2 pinned crates, small adapter we own                                                                                                   | pure Rust, `cargo build` only           | none                                             | good for prose; OOV (names, brands, jargon) spelled letter-by-letter | ~300–400 ms                               | 2 direct (`ort`, `misaki-rs`) → ~6 transitive (fancy-regex, language-tokenizer, num2words, ort-sys, …) |
| **B3** B2 + optional `espeak-ng` **subprocess** for OOV words only  | as B2                                                        | MIT clean (arms-length, FSF-sanctioned)                                                    | low+: ~60 lines, off by default, auto-detects `/opt/homebrew/bin/espeak-ng`                                                                  | as B2                                   | none                                             | B2, rising to B1 when espeak-ng is installed                         | as B2 (+~30 ms spawn, OOV sentences only) | as B2                                                                                                  |

Latency column is measured inference only; see the next block. Streaming/sentence API: none of the crates adds anything over our Phase 1 splitter, so A1/A2 buy nothing there.

### Latency vs the ~300 ms target (measured on this M4 Pro, CPU EP, warm session)

| Model                               | 3 words / 18 ph | 5 words / 33 ph | 14 words / 81 ph | 28 words / 190 ph |
| ----------------------------------- | --------------- | --------------- | ---------------- | ----------------- |
| fp32, 8 intra-op threads            | 212 ms          | 292 ms          | 550 ms           | 1,291 ms          |
| fp32, 4 threads                     | —               | 484 ms          | —                | —                 |
| int8 (`model_quantized`), 8 threads | 123 ms          | 177 ms          | 331 ms           | 754 ms            |

Session load: fp32 0.58 s, int8 0.41 s (do once at model select, not per hotkey). G2P: Homebrew espeak-ng in-process ≈0 ms after a 0.5 s first-call init (CLI spawn 30 ms wall); `misaki-rs` lexicon lookup is unmeasured but is hash lookups, expect <2 ms. Playback start via rodio after `append` is tens of ms. Phase 1 capture (⌘C + clipboard poll) adds ~50–200 ms before synthesis begins. **Conclusion:** a short first sentence meets ~300 ms of synthesis on an M4 Pro at fp32; a typical 12–15-word sentence does not (550 ms+), and an M1 (4 P-cores) will be roughly 2× slower. The reliable route to the target is independent of the option chosen: synthesise a short first chunk (first clause / ~8 words) and the rest sentence-by-sentence, and default to int8 or fp16. Caveat: published figures say int8 is 2–3× _slower_ than fp32 on Apple CPUs (older ORT); ORT 1.31 here shows the opposite, so the spike must re-measure through the `ort` crate.

### Recommendation

**B2 now, with B3 as a small follow-on stage**: `ort = "=2.0.0-rc.14"` (features `download-binaries`, no `coreml`, `PreferCPU`) + `misaki-rs` with `default-features = false`, adapter in `tts/local.rs`, HTTP backend kept as `TtsBackend::Http`. It is the only option that keeps Mouthy MIT without argument, builds with plain `cargo build`, adds two direct dependencies we pin ourselves, and gives automatic British phonemes from the `b*` voice prefix. A1 is rejected on licence and hygiene; A2 saves nothing over B2 because the Kokoro part is ~150 lines of `ort` calls. B1 is rejected: it carries the licence risk for only OOV words, and B3 recovers the same quality through a subprocess when the user has Homebrew espeak-ng.
**Biggest risk:** OOV handling. Spelling "Kubernetes" or a surname letter-by-letter is jarring; `misaki-rs` was corrected "using eSpeak" (derived data, licence note defers to misaki Apache-2.0) and we have no coverage figure for GB prose. Mitigations in order: B3 subprocess, or swap to `loqui-g2p` (neural fallback, same shape of adapter) if a sample text shows >1 OOV per paragraph.
**1-day spike must prove:** (1) `ort` rc.14 on arm64 runs `model.onnx` and `model_quantized.onnx` with `input_ids`/`style`/`speed` from a raw `bm_lewis.bin` row and matches the Python audio (int8 timing confirmed or refuted); (2) `misaki-rs` `G2P::new(true)` output on 20 real sentences (news, code docs, names) token-maps into Kokoro's vocab with no `❓`, and OOV rate is acceptable; (3) release-binary size delta is ~19 MB and `tauri build` keeps a working bundle.

### P2.2 Implementation Notes (spike, 2026-10-13)

- Spike `examples/kokoro_spike.rs` (deleted in P2.3b; it lives at `e561e48`); all three proofs passed. Model files in the scratchpad `kspike/hf/`, never committed.
- **Timings** (M4 Pro, CPU `PreferCPU`, `bm_lewis`, warm median, ±10%): int8 for 3 / 5 / 14 / 28 words ≈ 150 / 210 / 370 / 830 ms; fp32 270 / 384 / 707 / 1,563 ms; session load int8 ~290–370 ms, fp32 ~460 ms. int8 is ~1.9× faster than fp32 through `ort` (Python agrees), hence the int8 default. `ort` output is bit-identical to Python onnxruntime 1.31.
- **Memory:** `G2P::new(EnglishGB)` 70–150 ms and +144 MB RSS (parses ~15 MB of embedded JSON); int8 session +150 MB; fp32 +410 MB.
- **Size:** +52 MB installed, +15 MB zipped, not ~19 MB: misaki-rs `include_str!`s ~35.5 MB of US+GB lexicon and tagger JSON; ONNX Runtime is the other ~19 MB (→ H9).
- **misaki-rs on 21 sentences / 300 words:** 13 unknown words (4.3%, code terms and British names), spelled letter by letter; `£` gave `❓`, `$` dropped, decimals, versions, years and times misread (→ P2.3 `expand_numbers`). Its lexicons were regenerated with eSpeak: 58% of GB gold entries match hexgrad's, and up to ~8% of running words get non-RP forms (`path` pˈaθ, `one` wˈɒn, `default` dɪfˈɒlt); US 41% (→ H10). `G2P::with_fallback` calls a fallback once per unknown word (P2.5 uses it).
- **Vocab:** onnx-community `tokenizer.json`, 114 symbols plus pad 0; input `[0, ids…, 0]`, at most 510 ids; style = row `ids.len()` of the voice `.bin` (510 × 256 little-endian f32). misaki-rs output needs U+200D ties mapped to Kokoro's single symbols (misaki's `espeak.py` table), spacing fixed, and out-of-vocab symbols dropped; all in `tts::local`.
- **`ort` rc.14 gotchas:** features `default-features = false, ["std", "download-binaries", "tls-native", "api-31"]`. One `Environment` per process (a second `build()` errors); say no to telemetry explicitly. The default `MaxEfficiency` policy routes to CoreML (3.2 s load, ~10% slower, a slightly different waveform), so use `PreferCPU`. `Session::run` takes `&mut self`. `RunOptions::terminate` from another thread interrupts a run. Release LTO builds drop std's weak `___isPlatformVersionAtLeast`, so clang's runtime must be linked (`build.rs`, `-fapple-link-rtlib` since P2.3b). The prebuilt objects target macOS 13.4 and always pull in CoreML, Foundation and libc++. No `x86_64-apple-darwin` archive: Intel and universal builds fail at link. The ~9.7 MB archive comes from `cdn.pyke.io` and is cached in `~/Library/Caches/ort.pyke.io`; an offline fresh clone without that cache fails unless `ORT_LIB_PATH` points at a copy (untried). misaki-rs 0.6: `G2P::new(Language::EnglishGB)`.
- Verification: `cargo test` 96 pass / 1 ignored; release build and bundle linked.

### P2.3 Implementation Notes (local backend, 2026-10-13)

Commits `4dc9cde` (backend), `ecd94be` (text), `00b495b` (settings UI), `777d027` (macOS 13.4).

- **Backend (`tts/local.rs`):** `ModelFiles` names `models/kokoro-v1.0/{int8,fp32}.onnx` and `voices/<voice>.bin` (names limited to `[A-Za-z0-9_-]`). `LOADED` holds session, voice table and G2P and reloads only what changed (a `FileStamp` of path, size and mtime, plus the dialect); a poisoned lock is cleared and the model reloaded. ORT logs go to `log` target `ort`. Sessions: `PreferCPU`, intra-op spinning off, default thread count. Text with no phonemes gives no samples, so CJK-only text ends as "Nothing selected".
- **Stop:** a per-session `tts-interrupt` thread polls the stop flag every 20 ms and calls `RunOptions::terminate()`; anything returned after a stop counts as stopped, not failed.
- **Seam (`tts/mod.rs`):** `Backend { Http, Local }` is chosen on the main thread before the capture; `Backend::open` on the synth thread gives a `Synth`, so `block_on` stays in `actions.rs`.
- **Missing files:** checked on the main thread before ⌘C → `OverlayView::ModelMissing` ("Offline voice files are missing", 6 s).
- **Preload:** `actions::preload_local_voice` spawns `tts-preload` at launch and on backend, model and voice changes; it reads the settings only after taking the lock, so the last change wins.
- **Text:** `split_first_clause` cuts a first sentence of more than 8 words at its first `,;:` or dash when both sides are ≥ 20 chars. `expand_numbers` (Local only): currency, decimals, versions, years by num2words' `to_year` rule, decades, ordinals, times, percent, a leading minus, and "to" between two numbers of one kind. Plain integers stay digits.
- **Logs:** `misaki_rs` capped at Warn (it logs selected text at debug). `First audio X ms after the capture` at info.
- **Measured** (M4 Pro, int8, release LTO off): first audio 205–241 ms for a 5-word sentence, 237–260 ms for a 6-word first clause, 318–341 ms for a 10-word clause, 458–502 ms for 14 + 4 words with no clause break; fp32 whole 20-word sentence 1,188 ms (debug). Background load: int8 350 ms, fp32 476 ms, British lexicon 99 ms (release) / ~700 ms (debug). Stop: playback out 3–12 ms, inference interrupted 3–18 ms after; a new request 80 ms later had first audio in 321 ms. Quit mid-inference: clean exit 3 of 3.
- **Gotcha:** the ~200–300 ms target holds only when the first clause has about 6 words or fewer; the splitter never cuts inside a clause.
- Seen once in 20+ runs: `audio stream error: A buffer underrun or overrun occurred.` at the start of the very first debug local run.
- Verification: `cargo test` 161 pass / 2 ignored; bundle 63,176 KB.

### P2.4 Implementation Notes (downloader + Models page, 2026-10-14)

- **Catalog (`tts/catalog.rs`):** int8 ← `onnx/model_quantized.onnx` 92,361,116 B; fp32 ← `onnx/model.onnx` 325,532,232 B (fp16 dropped in P2.4b); 28 English voices (`bf_`, `bm_`, `af_`, `am_`; 522,240 B each; the other 26 v1.0 voices need other languages' phonemes). sha256 = HF `lfs.oid`; URLs pinned to revision `1939ad2a8e416c0acfeecc08a694d14ef25f2231` so the hashes always describe what the URL serves. Paths come from `tts::local`.
- **Downloader (`tts/download.rs`):** Handy's resumable downloader trimmed: bytes to `<file>.part`, resume with `Range` (a 206 must start at our offset, a 200 restarts, a 416 or wrong offset discards), cut-off past the expected size, `sync_all`, sha256 on `spawn_blocking`, then `rename`. Connect timeout 15 s, stall 60 s, no overall timeout; system proxy honoured. reqwest's `stream` feature is not needed.
- **Commands (`commands/models.rs`):** `list_model_files`, `download_model_file`, `cancel_model_download` (keeps the `.part`), `delete_model_file` (refused while that file downloads), `open_models_dir`; events `model-download-progress` (≤ 10/s) and `model-files-changed`; a preload after each download and delete.
- **UI:** Models page (status line with one Download for the missing files, model rows, voice rows, folder and licence); two-click Delete (the dialog plugin went in R5); the overlay's model-missing Open Settings sends `show-section` `"models"`.
- **Default flip:** fresh stores get `local`; a store without `tts_backend` gets `http` written before parse (`keep_server_backend_for_older_stores`). Removed 2026-10-21: every store without a readable `tts_backend` loads as `local` (see Current State).
- **Live:** cancelled at ~30 MB, resumed from the same byte (HF's 302 to its CDN keeps `Range`), verified in 4.6 s in debug (~3 s of it the sha256); cancel, quit and SIGKILL all resume. First audio on the fresh files 262–299 ms. Delete mid-speech: speech carries on, the model unloads after the session.
- Verification: `cargo test` 182 pass / 2 ignored; bundle 63,304 KB, zipped 19.7 MB.

### P2.3b Implementation Notes (fix-ups, 2026-10-14)

Commits `58dedce` … `7daf316` (10, all signed).

- **`plain_text` (Local only):** `’ ‘ ʼ` → `'`; soft hyphen, ZWSP and BOM dropped; NBSP → space; Latin-1 and common Latin Extended-A letters and ligatures folded to ASCII (misaki-rs's lexicons hold no non-ASCII key).
- **First-clause split:** a dash whose neighbouring words both contain a digit is a range, not a break; the split runs for Local only, so the HTTP path is the Phase 1 DoD's.
- **`build.rs`:** `cargo:rustc-link-arg=-fapple-link-rtlib`.
- **Memory:** `PIECE_TOKENS = 150` (was one 510-id piece). Steady RSS after long sentences 0.56–0.74 GB instead of 1.03–1.27 GB, for 5–15% more inference time. Release app: 348 MB after the load; 666–682 MB RSS and 719 MB `footprint` after three long sessions. Between sessions RSS fell to ~104 MB but `footprint`, the figure Activity Monitor shows, did not (→ H11).
- **First audio with the cap:** 5 words 216–236 ms; 14 words with no clause break 386–429 ms; 300 characters with no clause break (2 pieces) 1.41–1.53 s.
- **Lows:** three or more dash-joined numbers stay as written (dates, phone and card numbers); `7.30pm` is a time; a spaced em or en dash becomes ", " on Local; `load_session` refuses a model without `waveform`; `open` returns early if the session was stopped while waiting.
- **Other:** the spike's corpus is `tts::local::tests::corpus_report` (`#[ignore]`); `autostart.rs` always uses `SMAppService` on macOS (`remove_plugin_launch_agent` stays for plists from Phase 1's 10.15 floor); Rust 1.92 minimum.
- Verification: `cargo test` 176 pass / 3 ignored; bundle 63,304 KB.

### P2.5 Implementation Notes (espeak-ng for unknown words, 2026-10-14)

Commits `7d5a1a8` (backend), `e34a075` (toggle), `2eeb81b` (docs).

- **Setting and detection:** `tts_espeak_fallback` (false); `find_espeak_ng` command; `tts::espeak::find` checks `/opt/homebrew/bin`, `/usr/local/bin`, then `PATH` on every request, so installing or removing it needs no restart.
- **Invocation:** `<path> -q --ipa=3 -v en-gb|en-us -- <word>` with no shell; only ASCII letters and apostrophes up to 48 bytes are sent; killed after 500 ms. A failed, slow or empty call gives misaki's spelled form (the fallback never returns `Err`). Successes are cached per G2P (cleared at 10,000). No log line names the word.
- **Mapping (`to_kokoro`):** follows hexgrad's `espeak.py` (US flap `T`, `ɜɹ`, `əɹ`, `ɐ`→`ə`), except that dialect ties run before `e`→A; lexicon words keep misaki-rs's eSpeak forms (`ɾ`, `ɚ`, `ʔ`), part of H10.
- **UI:** General → Speech, Local only, with a found/not-installed line.
- **Measured:** 17–32 ms per uncached word, 20–52 ms through the fallback; all 13 corpus words read (eSpeak's guess for Cholmondeley is wrong); "Siobhan from Keighley runs Kubernetes." is 7.3 s of audio spelled, 3.8 s with espeak-ng.
- Verification: `cargo test` 188 pass / 4 ignored.

### P2.4b Implementation Notes (panel fix-ups, 2026-10-14)

Commits `c64d39b`, `b66db85`, `91b5e01`, `d31567a`, `51452a7` (all signed).

- **Models page truth:** `ModelFileList` carries `missing_model`, `missing_voice` (the same checks as the speak path) and `load_error` (`tts::local::PRELOAD_ERROR`, its own lock because only the main thread may lock `TtsState`). `local-voice-status` is gone; `model-files-changed` follows every preload. Non-catalog voices get the `unlistedVoice` line.
- **`plain_text`:** combining accents after an ASCII letter dropped; U+2212 → `-`; `“ ” „` → `"`.
- **fp16 dropped:** no faster than fp32 on CPU. A stored `"fp16"` fails the parse and salvage gives `int8`.
- **Backend salvage:** a missing or unreadable `tts_backend` becomes `http`; frontend fallbacks are `"local"`; `App.tsx` uses `Object.hasOwn` (tsconfig `lib` ES2022).
- **Download run counter removed:** only a file's own task removes its entry, and a second download of that file is refused meanwhile.
- **Not shown on the page:** a load failure from the speak path (not a preload).
- Verification: `cargo test` 194 pass / 4 ignored.

### P2.6 Implementation Notes (close-out, 2026-10-14)

Commits `d70eef3` (DoD checklist), `4d6c485` (README, CHANGELOG, AGENTS.md), `88e272e` (these notes), `e55faf9` (CONTRIBUTING's CHANGELOG pointer). All signed.

- **DoD checklist (`docs/dod-checklist.md`):** 22 steps on the built app, starting from a fresh install (app data moved aside): model-missing notice → Models page → download with one cancel/resume → Wi-Fi off for steps 4–17 (Safari, VS Code, tray, held hotkey on both backends, nothing selected, a 329-character sentence with no comma to listen for 150-token seams, curly quotes/accents/numbers, overlay, memory in Activity Monitor, relaunch preload, Bluetooth) → Wi-Fi on (second voice, espeak-ng, HTTP and back, delete while speaking, login item). Footer lists what to report, including the first-audio log lines and the H9/H10/H11 answers.
- **README:** offline-first description and status, requirements (Apple Silicon, macOS 13.4, Rust 1.92, 93 MB download), first run = Accessibility then the in-app download, a separate "Using a speech server instead" section, ORT archive cache note. No fp16 mention remained.
- **CHANGELOG:** `0.2.0 (unreleased)` holds the offline release (Added: offline speech, number reading, espeak-ng, Models page; Changed: default, first clause, macOS 13.4, build needs). The fork's Phase 1 entries became `0.1.0 (not released)`, with its own 9.6 MB size. No version bump in Cargo, tauri.conf or package.json (Phase 3).
- **AGENTS.md:** prerequisites (arm64, 13.4, Rust 1.92, ORT download, model files never committed), `audio_toolkit/`, and the Local-backend invariants (`ENVIRONMENT`, `LOADED`, `PRELOAD_ERROR`, `PIECE_TOKENS`, `PreferCPU`, `-fapple-link-rtlib`) and download invariants (`Abortable`, `.part`, `ModelDownloads` never held across an await, pinned revision).
- **PLAN.md:** P2.2–P2.4b notes condensed (full text at `948c68d`); P2.1's unused A2 stage branch removed; Phase 3 candidates collected.

- **Bundle:** `Mouthy.app` 63,336 KB; binary 64,722,960 B; zipped (`ditto -c -k`) 19,756,178 B; arm64 only, `minos` 13.4, `LSMinimumSystemVersion` 13.4, ad-hoc signed with the hardened runtime, `com.azizi.mouthy`, version 0.1.0. Not launched (it would prompt for Accessibility).
- **ONNX Runtime archive:** came from the cache. Both `ort-sys` builds linked from `~/Library/Caches/ort.pyke.io/dfbin/aarch64-apple-darwin/4033efca…`, whose file time (2026-10-13 21:22) did not change. Fetching the same archive over the network separately (`cdn.pyke.io/…/aarch64-apple-darwin+coreml.tar.lzma2`) took 0.42 s for 9,687,276 B, sha256 matching `ort-sys`'s `dist.tsv`. `ort-sys` prints `[WARN] can't do xcframework linking for target 'aarch64-apple-darwin'` from its build script; cargo hides it for registry crates and the link is fine.
- No failure, so no code change. After the docs commits, the fresh clone pulled `4d6c485` and `bun run format:check` and `bun run lint` passed again.

### Stage P2.7 — DoD fix-ups: seam pauses, overlay × (2026-10-14) `[fix]`

From the user's DoD run: step 11's 329-character sentence had two very noticeable pauses; × on a notice brought Settings to the front when it was open. Commits `7e9dcba` (seam trim), `17d27ca` (overlay), `02aec7c` (docs), then after review `9e3994e` (pause rule), `3e9831e` (warning text) and the docs commit. `7e9dcba`, `9e3994e` and `3e9831e` are **unsigned** (1Password locked or not answering).

**Seam pauses — measured** (temporary probe on the real int8 model, `bm_lewis`, debug; silence = |sample| ≤ 0.003, about −50 dBFS; edges move ≤ 25 ms at 0.001, so the padding is clean near-zero):

| Piece (1×)                        | Leading silence | Trailing silence |
| --------------------------------- | --------------- | ---------------- |
| 64 ids ending in `,`              | 399 ms          | 591 ms           |
| same ending in `.`                | 394 ms          | 591 ms           |
| same with no punctuation          | 397 ms          | 632 ms           |
| same ending in `?`                | 403 ms          | 614 ms           |
| "Still working." / no full stop   | 365 ms          | 463 / 478 ms     |
| step 11 pieces (144, 149, 30 ids) | 399–405 ms      | 827, 732, 513 ms |

- Kokoro pads every inference by ~0.4 s before and 0.5–0.8 s after (longer pieces get more), **whatever the last punctuation**: a cut at a comma gets no extra pause from the model, just the same padding.
- Its own pauses inside one inference: comma 380 ms, semicolon 405 ms, full stop 470 ms at 1×; 540 / 655 ms at 0.75×, 250 / 305 ms at 1.5×, 190 / 230 ms at 2× (≈ 1/speed). Padding at 1.5×: ~323 ms lead, ~410 ms trail; at 2×: ~330 / ~335 ms.
- Step 11: `split_sentences` gives two chunks (297 + 31 characters; `split_long` cuts at the last space before 300). `split_first_clause` does not cut it (no clause break). The first chunk is 293 ids, so `split_tokens` makes two pieces (144 + 149, cut at a space). Two seams, both mid-phrase: 827 + 405 ≈ 1.23 s and 732 + 400 ≈ 1.13 s of silence. Those were the two pauses.
- **Fix (`tts::local`):** `trim_silence` keeps at most `LEAD_MS` 50 ms before each piece's first sample above `SILENCE` (0.003) and, after its last, `PAUSE_END_MS` 400 ms or `PHRASE_END_MS` 100 ms, both divided by the speed. **Pause rule (from review):** `split_sentences` returns `text::Chunk { text, ends_sentence }`; `ends_sentence` is false only for a `split_long` length cut, and `split_first_clause`'s head is true while its tail keeps the sentence's flag. The last piece of a chunk that ends a sentence (punctuation, a line break such as a heading or bullet, the end of the text, or a first-clause cut) keeps `PAUSE_END_MS` whatever its punctuation, because misaki drops `…`, `—`, quotes and brackets and a line break ends a sentence with none. Every other piece (inside a chunk from `split_tokens`, or the end of a `split_long` cut) keeps `PAUSE_END_MS` only if it ends in a `PAUSE_IDS` token, else `PHRASE_END_MS`. `split_long` stays on the Local path: `LocalSynth::synthesize` returns a chunk's pieces together, so without it a long run-on sentence would wait for every piece before the first audio. The HTTP path ignores the flag. With the next piece's 50 ms lead a pause is ~450 ms, between Kokoro's comma (380) and full stop (470), which the review judged indistinguishable. The first version keyed three tiers off the last token only, so lines without a full stop got a 150 ms breath. Every piece and so every sentence is trimmed, including the first piece's lead-in; the HTTP backend is not touched. All silence gives no samples.
- **After** (same probe through `Loaded::synthesize`, first version): step 11's two seams were 145 ms each and its audio 19.5 s instead of 22.0 s; a paragraph's sentence seams 465 ms (were ~1.0 s). Under the pause rule both of step 11's seams (`split_tokens` inside the first chunk, and the `split_long` cut "…done | with the money…") stay ~150 ms breaths; sentence seams are ~450 ms.
- **First audio:** the `First audio … after the capture` log line is unchanged (it times the first append), but the audible start is ~350 ms earlier, since the 0.4 s lead-in is gone. The ignored `local_voice_speaks_and_stops` now asserts that the first sound is within 100 ms of the start (it was).
- **Deviation:** the packet suggested per-window detection; a per-sample threshold is used because the measured padding never rises above 0.001, so windows would add nothing. The trailing cap depends on where the piece ends (rather than one 60–100 ms cap) so that comma and sentence seams keep their pauses.

**Overlay × — root cause.** tao creates the overlay as an ordinary `NSWindow`; tauri-nspanel then swaps its class to an `NSPanel` subclass and sets the `NonactivatingPanel` style mask. AppKit sets a window's "prevents activation" state only when it is created with that mask. A Swift probe on this Mac (macOS 26.6.2): a panel created with the mask reads `_preventsActivation` = true; a window converted the tauri-nspanel way reads false (`_isNonactivatingPanel` is true for both). So any click on the overlay activated Mouthy, and activation brought its open Settings window forward; with Settings closed there was nothing to raise. It applied to every overlay view (model-missing, error, speaking), not to the hide path: `hide_overlay` is a plain `orderOut:`, and the panel never becomes key (`can_become_key_window: false`).

- **Fix (`overlay::prevent_activation`):** after the panel is built, call `_setPreventsActivation: YES` (private AppKit; guarded by `respondsToSelector:`, a warning is logged if a macOS release drops it). Through the existing `objc2` dependency; no new dependency. The probe shows the flag survives show, resize, `orderOut:` and a later `setStyleMask:`. In the real debug app a temporary log line read `_preventsActivation=true` right after launch.
- Open Settings still raises Settings: `show_main_window` activates the app itself.

**Review verdicts:** both fixes accepted. The private `_setPreventsActivation:` is accepted: there is no public route, since tauri-nspanel converts the window after tao creates it; the call is guarded; it would only matter for the App Store. The adversary's Swift probe showed clicks do reach a converted non-activating panel. The pause rule above replaced the punctuation-only rule (a must-fix), and the tiers went from three to two.

**Checklist:** step 11 now expects speech a little over a second after the press (the log line still says ~1.5 s), two short breaths and no full pause; step 20's × expects Settings to stay behind Safari; step 21 adds × on the model-missing notice (the user's case).

**Verified:** `cargo test` 199 pass / 4 ignored after review (5 trim and pause tests); the ignored live test against the real model; `cargo clippy --all-targets` clean; `cargo build` 0 compiler warnings; `bun run lint`, `check:translations`, `format:check`, `bun run build` all pass; `bindings.ts` unchanged. `bun run tauri build --bundles app` rebuilt `Mouthy.app` (63,336 KB, binary 64,722,960 B: the same as P2.6 after page alignment; it contains `_setPreventsActivation:`). Not launched.

**Not verified:** how the trimmed seams sound (nobody has listened); a real click on × (this shell cannot post mouse events: `AXIsProcessTrusted` false), including that the click still reaches the button when Mouthy stays inactive (it did before, while AppKit already treated the window as a non-activating panel, so it should); the hotkey loop live (⌘C capture is blocked in the agent shell). The user re-tests steps 4, 11, 15, 17, 20 and 21.

### Stage P2.8 — whole-sentence inference (2026-10-14) `[fix]`

From the user's step 11 re-test after P2.7: pauses were comma-length, but the sentence sounded like several stuck together. Each piece of at most 150 ids was its own Kokoro inference, and every inference ends with sentence-final intonation; trimming cannot change that. Commits `834de06` (memory pattern off), `a528963` (whole sentences), `fe29b74` (README, CHANGELOG, AGENTS.md, checklist) and the docs commit; all signed.

**Measured** with a temporary ignored test in a scratchpad copy (never committed): the real int8 model and `bm_lewis` through the app's `normalize` → `split_sentences` → `split_first_clause` → `text_to_ids` → `split_tokens` → `run`, one process per row after a short warm-up; M4 Pro, debug build (ORT is prebuilt, so its speed is unchanged). Memory is `phys_footprint` from `task_info`, the figure Activity Monitor shows (it matched `/usr/bin/time -l`). Texts: step 11 (329 characters, no comma), a 510-character run-on with no comma, a 679-character paragraph of five sentences; "sustained" reads paragraph, step 11, run-on, paragraph, twice. Times are first audio (the first chunk's inference).

| Cap (ids)   | `split_long` cut | Step 11                                    | Run-on                                | Paragraph           | Sustained                |
| ----------- | ---------------- | ------------------------------------------ | ------------------------------------- | ------------------- | ------------------------ |
| 150 (P2.3b) | 300              | 494 MB, 1.26–1.31 s                        | 508 MB, 1.16–1.28 s                   | 473 MB, 0.50–0.55 s | 670–680 MB               |
| 300         | 300              | 721 MB, 1.28 s                             | 681 MB, 1.17 s                        | 506 MB              | —                        |
| 510         | 300              | 725 MB, 1.26 s (2 chunks)                  | 682 MB, 1.16 s                        | 495 MB              | 1,077 MB                 |
| 510         | none             | 728 MB, 1.34–1.42 s (1 inference, 325 ids) | 1,034–1,065 MB, 2.03–2.06 s (493 ids) | 496 MB              | 1,266–1,296 MB (470 cut) |

A full 509-id piece peaks at 1,033–1,042 MB, the ceiling at a 510 cap.

ORT memory options, 510 cap, no length cut (sustained at a 470 cut); steady footprint, peak in brackets where higher:

| Option                                                             | Run-on                   | Step 11          | Sustained                                      | Inference time |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------ | ------------------------ | ---------------- | ---------------------------------------------- | -------------- |
| Defaults                                                           | 1,034–1,065 MB           | 724–728 MB       | 1,266–1,296 MB                                 | —              |
| Arena shrinkage (`memory.enable_memory_arena_shrinkage` = `cpu:0`) | 1,074 MB                 | 762 MB           | —                                              | +3–6%          |
| Memory pattern off (`with_memory_pattern(false)`)                  | 1,033 MB                 | 724 MB           | 998–1,009 MB                                   | no change      |
| Shrinkage + pattern off                                            | 652–793 MB (1,030–1,068) | 773 MB           | 582 / 992 MB (992–1,094)                       | +2–5%          |
| CPU arena off                                                      | 887 MB (1,538)           | 1,016 MB (1,110) | —                                              | +11–17%        |
| Initializers outside the arena (± shrinkage, pattern)              | 1,036–1,059 MB           | 724–769 MB       | —                                              | no change      |
| `kSameAsRequested` arena (raw `OrtApi` env allocator)              | 1,353 MB                 | —                | 2,083 MB; + shrink, pattern off 651 MB (1,614) | +0–5%          |

- **Shrinkage** does nothing alone and, with the pattern off, frees 250–400 MB only sometimes (582 and 992 MB on identical sustained runs): ORT frees only arena regions with nothing in use, and the run's output is still alive when it shrinks. Peak unchanged. Not used.
- **Memory pattern off** keeps the sustained figure at the single-run peak of the longest piece (it also takes P2.3b's 150 cap from 680 to 489 MB), at no measurable time cost; step 11's audio is bit-identical with it on, off, and off with shrinkage. Used.
- **Arena off** and **`kSameAsRequested`** are worse; the latter would also need raw FFI.

**Shipped** (510 cap, pattern off, Local cut at 450; through `open` and `Loaded::synthesize`):

| Text      | Chunks (characters) | First audio | Footprint after          |
| --------- | ------------------- | ----------- | ------------------------ |
| Step 11   | 1 (329)             | 1.29–1.36 s | 724–768 MB               |
| Run-on    | 2 (444 + 65)        | 1.64–1.71 s | 957–984 MB               |
| Paragraph | 5                   | 0.47–0.49 s | 510 MB                   |
| Sustained |                     |             | 984 MB (RSS 662 MB idle) |

The app process adds ~50–85 MB (P2.3b: 348 MB after the load against ~265 MB here), so estimated ~0.6 GB for ordinary prose, ~0.8 GB after step 11, up to ~1.1 GB after a 450-character sentence.

**Decision:** peak ≤ ~1.04 GB and steady = peak with the pattern off, so the ≤ ~1 GB branch:

- `PIECE_TOKENS` = 510, Kokoro's limit; `split_tokens` stays as the safety for a chunk over 510 ids. P2.7's trim and pause rules are unchanged; the interior rule now applies only past 510 ids.
- `build_session` sets `with_memory_pattern(false)`.
- `split_sentences(text, max_chars)` with `Backend::max_chunk_chars`: Local 450 (`tts::local::MAX_CHUNK_CHARS`), HTTP 300 (`tts::http::MAX_CHUNK_CHARS`), so HTTP requests are unchanged. Ids per character: prose 0.96–1.04 (the corpus plus the three texts), numbers, acronyms and spelled-out names 1.2–2.3. 450 plus a merged short tail (≤ 470 characters) stays under 510 ids for prose; denser text is cut by `split_tokens`.
- Tests: `a_long_sentence_is_read_in_one_inference` (step 11 is one chunk and one piece; 470 characters of prose fit 510 ids), `the_limit_decides_whether_a_long_sentence_is_cut`; the `split_tokens` tests scale with `PIECE_TOKENS`.

**First clause (packet item 4):** `split_first_clause` gives a long first sentence's head its own inference, so in principle the head ends with sentence-final intonation too. Whole first sentences: the 300-character comma-rich test sentence 1,333 ms against 341–349 ms with the cut; step 11 (no clause break, already whole) 1.29–1.36 s; a 444-character first chunk 1.64–1.71 s. Whole first sentences stay within ~1.5 s only up to ~330 characters on this M4 Pro (an M1 is about 2× slower) and cost ~1 s for a typical long first sentence, so the cut stays; the user decides.

**Edge cases**

- Empty or unspeakable text: no chunks, as before.
- A chunk over 510 ids (numbers, acronyms, names spelled out): `split_tokens` cuts at the last pause, else space, with P2.7's interior pause rule.
- A sentence over 450 characters: `split_long` cuts at its last clause break, else space, before 450; `ends_sentence` false as in P2.7.
- HTTP: the 300 cut and everything else as before (the text tests run at 300).
- Stop during a long inference (~2.4 s at 510 ids): `RunOptions::terminate` still ends it; the live test stopped one ~460-id inference 13 ms after the flag.
- A short first clause followed by a long tail: the tail (up to ~470 characters, ~1.8 s here) synthesises while the head (≥ 20 characters plus its 400 ms pause) plays; on a Mac twice as slow there may be a gap of a second or two there (up to ~0.7 s with the 300 cut). Not measured.
- Memory is never given back while the app runs (as before, H11); the ceiling is ~1.04 GB plus the app.

**Failure and reversibility:** no stored state or migration; reverting `a528963` (and `834de06`) restores the old behaviour.

**Verified:** `cargo test` 202 pass / 4 ignored; the ignored live test against the real model; `cargo clippy --all-targets` clean; `cargo build` 0 compiler warnings; `bun run lint`, `check:translations`, `format:check`, `bun run build` pass; `bun run tauri build --bundles app` rebuilt `Mouthy.app` (63,336 KB, binary 64,722,960 B). Not launched.

**Not verified:** how it sounds (nobody has listened); memory in the real app (the agent shell cannot capture a selection, so the app figures are estimates); first audio and the start-of-speech gap on a slower Mac; the hotkey loop. The user re-tests steps 4, 11, 15, 17, 20 and 21.
