### Stage P3.0 — reflow hard-wrapped text before sentence splitting (2026-10-21) `[feat]`

From the user: text copied from programs that lay out their own lines (Claude Code, nvim, man pages) and from email and PDFs has a newline at every wrapped line, and `split_sentences` ends a sentence at every line break (on purpose, so headings and bullets read as sentences). Each wrapped line got a full pause and its own falling intonation.

**Where:** `text::reflow(&str) -> String` is the first step of `normalize`, before `strip_line_markers` (which would remove the `#`, `-` and `>` it needs). `actions.rs` calls `normalize` once for every request, so Local, HTTP and the HTTP path's plain `normalize` + `split_sentences` output all get it. `split_sentences` is unchanged, so its line-break tests still hold; `normalize` drops the blank lines `reflow` leaves.

**Rules**, in order:

1. CRLF and lone CR become `\n`. A line that is empty or holds only whitespace, box-drawing characters (U+2500–U+257F), `⏺`/`⎿` or a byte order mark is a paragraph break.
2. Fenced code (` ``` ` or `~~~`, also behind `⏺`, `⎿` or a quote gutter) is copied verbatim to its closing fence; one left open inside a quote gets its closing fence written, so a second pass reads it the same way.
3. Two or more consecutive lines sharing a gutter (`│ ┃ ╎ >`, but not `>>>`) lose it, one level at a time (nested `> >` too), and the text inside is split and reflowed again on its own. One quoted line in prose keeps its `>` and is never joined; a line indented to its text, as Claude Code shows a wrapped prompt, continues it.
4. Leading `⏺`/`⎿` and a byte order mark count as indent.
5. A break always stays when the next line starts like structure (heading `#`, `- * •` plus space, `1.`/`2)` plus space, `>`, `$ `, `>>>`, `... `, `|`, box-drawing, a fence); when the line is a heading, prompt, table or box row (a row: a tab between words, `│` or `|`); after a list item or quote unless the next line is indented to its text (its hanging indent); when the indent changes by two or more columns (tabs to multiples of 8), unless the next line sits at the line's hanging column, which is also the word after a 2+ space gap as in man's `-A      Include …`; after `.!?…:;` (closing quotes and brackets, `*`, `_` and backticks allowed after), except after an abbreviation from `ABBREVIATIONS`; after a markdown hard break (two trailing spaces); in a paragraph whose widest line is under 40 columns (`MIN_WRAP_CHARS`); and in a paragraph that is code (a line ending in `{` or `:` followed by a deeper plain line) or chat (two or more lines starting `name:` or `name —`).
6. Otherwise the fit test decides, as greedy wrapping does: a line moves a word down only when it does not fit, and fills every line but a paragraph's last. The break stays when the next line's first word would have fitted on the line (line width + 1 + word ≤ the paragraph's widest line), or when the next line itself left room for the word after it. Widths count columns with the indent.
7. A next line starting in lower case or with a capitalised word (a name, "I") then joins with a space. One whose first word has a digit (a number, a hash such as `a1b2c3d`, a code such as `E0308` or `ABC-123`) or is in capitals of two or more letters (a log level such as `WARN`) joins only when the line or the line after the next starts in lower case, so a git log, compiler errors or a log stays apart. A word with a digit does not count as lower case. (Until P3.1 capitals needed that lower-case neighbour too; the user chose the fit test alone, see Stage P3.1.)
8. A join after a letter and `-` adds no space and keeps the hyphen ("well-known", "Anglo-Saxon"; "neigh-bouring" still reads as two parts, as it did).
9. The text's first line may be the tail of a line the selection began in: when it has no indent and the next line has one, the indent change does not count, and a lower-case next line joins whatever the widths.
10. Kept lines lose their indent and are separated by a blank line, so a second pass finds nothing to join.

**Thresholds:** only `MIN_WRAP_CHARS` (40); the fit test needs none. A first version used two width shares (a line at least 70% of the widest joins, or 50% before a lower-case line); review replaced them with the fit test, which every genuine wrap in the corpus passes (greedy wrapping guarantees it). The fit test alone still joins after a paragraph's widest line, which is why rules 6 and 7 add the next line's own fit and the lower-case neighbour: without them two unmarked headings over a paragraph (48 + 1 + 6 > 54), "Order number: 4821-7734" under a long line, an unmarked list and a web heading over "Install" were joined. Since P3.1 the neighbour applies to numbers and codes only, so the unmarked list and the web heading join (the user's decision). The corpus, in a scratchpad: Claude Code replies laid out by a greedy wrapper at 60, 80 and 120 columns (`⏺`, `⎿`, `>` prompt, bullets and numbered steps with hanging indents), `man ls | col -b` at 80, an upstream Handy commit body (72), a wrapped email with a quoted reply, a markdown document, a Safari paragraph, short bullets, stacked headings, an unmarked list, a web heading over "Install", Rust, Python, JS and JSON, a `cargo test` transcript, nested quotes, a box, a tree and CRLF; plus the review's 37 cases (tables, chat, git log, subtitles, an address with hard breaks, verse, logs, Python prompts, CJK, quoted fences).

| Sample                                                                    | Lines → kept                | Outcome                                                                                                                 |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Claude Code reply, 60 / 80 / 120 columns                                  | 24 / 22 / 16 → 13 / 13 / 13 | paragraphs and steps whole; tool output and headings apart; at 80 a bullet ending "Claude / Code, man pages…" stays two |
| `man ls`                                                                  | 37 → 17                     | each paragraph and option one line; NAME, SYNOPSIS, `-D format` apart                                                   |
| Commit body, email, CRLF                                                  | 10 → 4, 12 → 7, 3 → 2       | paragraphs whole; greeting, attribution, signature apart; the `>` quote reflowed                                        |
| Markdown                                                                  | 17 → 12                     | headings, bullets (one hanging), quote whole; fenced code verbatim                                                      |
| Safari, short bullets, stacked headings, unmarked list, web heading, tree | unchanged                   | since P3.1 the unmarked list and the web heading join                                                                   |
| Rust, Python, JS, JSON, shell transcript, tables, chat, git log           | unchanged                   |                                                                                                                         |

**Deviations from the packet:** joins follow the fit test rather than "lower case, or 70% filled"; capitals needed a lower-case neighbour (dropped in P3.1); gutters are stripped per run of two or more lines rather than only when every line has one, so an email's attribution line no longer blocks its quote; `⏺`/`⎿` count as indent on any line, since only first lines carry them; a list item joins its hanging indent (the packet's "keep after a list line" would have split every wrapped Claude Code bullet), while a line at the item's own indent stays apart; the hyphen is kept, because misaki-rs spells an unknown word such as "wellknown" letter by letter; fenced code, the code and chat checks, table rows, hard breaks and the first-line rule are additions. From review: no lettered markers (`a.` also matched "A. Smith"), no `% `/`❯` prompts, no `+ – —` bullets.

**Edge cases**

- Empty, whitespace-only, box-only input: "". A single line: unchanged apart from its indent, trailing whitespace, a byte order mark and a leading `⏺`/`⎿`.
- A selection starting at a column-0 heading over indented text (man's NAME) joins a lower-case next line: "NAME ls – list directory contents".
- `> > > …` thousands deep, or 5,000 wrapped lines: no recursion (a work stack of pieces).
- Fuzz (scratchpad): the review's generator (stacked prefixes, fences, BOM, CRLF), 2 million texts on each of 3 seeds plus 2 million without fences, and ours, 200,000 on each of 3 seeds: no panic, idempotent, the sequence of alphanumeric words unchanged. It found three bugs, all fixed: `⎿  ⏺` stripped one glyph per pass; a fence behind `⏺`, `⎿` or a gutter was missed, so its closing fence opened one to the end; a byte order mark mid-text survived one pass.

**Known limits:** a wrap right after `:` or `;` keeps a full break (packet rule; it protects code). Lines of a similar width join whatever their first letter (since P3.1): an unmarked list, a heading over a line of similar width, a signature under a long line, verse with a capital on every line. Lines that start in lower case did already: a heading over "macOS requirements…", shell commands without a prompt, JS imports without semicolons, npm warnings, a verse whose line is as wide as its widest. A mid-word wrap without a hyphen (nvim without `linebreak`) joins as two words. An email quoted three levels as `>>>` reads as prompt lines (kept, not reflowed). A terminal that copies trailing spaces would mark every line a hard break and keep it. CJK and right-to-left text never joins (no lower case). A mixed-depth quote with no blank line keeps one level per line apart. `split_sentences` still merges fragments under 20 characters into a neighbour, as before. **First audio:** a wrapped first sentence used to start after its first line; it is now one sentence, which `split_first_clause` cuts at its first clause break, but one with none is a single inference, about 1.3 s at 330 characters on the M4 Pro (P2.8). A word-count fallback cut in `split_first_clause` would restore early audio; deferred, not built, since a cut adds an intonation seam the user dislikes.

**Failure and reversibility:** a pure function on the copied text; no stored state, settings or migration. Reverting the feat commit restores the old reading.

**Verified:** `cargo test` 222 pass / 4 ignored (20 new: one per rule, the corpus, the review's cases, idempotence, deep nesting, `normalize` end to end; each rule fails at least one test when removed); `cargo clippy --all-targets` clean; `cargo build` 0 compiler warnings; `bun run lint`, `check:translations`, `format:check`, `bun run build` pass. `bindings.ts` unchanged (no commands or types touched); the bindings launch handed off to a running release Mouthy.app, so it did not run.

**Not verified:** how it sounds (nobody has listened); a real terminal copy of a Claude Code reply (the corpus layout was generated to match it, not copied); Shift-drag in Zed and Option-drag in iTerm2 (the README note comes from the user and terminal documentation).

**Candidates collected from Deferred items and open asks:**

- Release: ship LICENSE inside the bundle; ONNX Runtime third-party notices (Eigen MPL-2.0 with a source pointer, protobuf, abseil), misaki/misaki-rs and Snowball notices; credit ONNX Runtime and misaki on About.
- Dependencies: move `ort` to 2.0 final when it ships.
- Answers to H9 (lexicons loaded from the models folder via a misaki-rs fork), H10 (hexgrad's lexicons through our own lookup) and H11 (unload after idle).
- Intonation vs latency: `split_first_clause` gives a long first sentence's head its own inference (P2.8 notes); reading it whole would cost ~1 s of first audio.
- Text: fold overrides for words whose accent matters (résumé, rosé, exposé, saké via misaki `[word](/ph/)` markers); spaced ranges ("10 – 12") and mixed ones ("10-20%", "£5-10", "5-6pm") read with "to"; `7.30-9.30pm`; two-number phone chains; a spaced hyphen or unspaced em dash as a pause. From P3.1: shouted function words spelled by misaki's NNP rule (a `[word](+2)` override).
- Models page: "In use" badge while on HTTP; Download button showing the remaining size; `sync_all` on the full-size-partial path; Time Machine exclusion for `models/`; App Nap during hidden downloads (unverified).
- Pre-existing: when handy-keys cannot start (no Accessibility), `init_shortcuts` reports success and the hotkey is silently dead instead of falling back to Tauri.
- H4: an in-app warning about the ⌥⎋ clash with Spoken Content, if wanted.
- **"Speak clipboard" binding:** a second hotkey that reads the clipboard without touching the selection, default ⌥⇧⎋ (user, 2026-10-21). Programs that turn on terminal mouse reporting (Claude Code, nvim, htop) never produce a terminal selection, so speak-selection finds nothing; the user copies inside the program (nvim yank to `+`) and presses this instead. It also reads "what I just copied". A separate binding, not a silent fallback, so a mis-selection never reads stale clipboard text. Reuse the bindings model (`shortcut/mod.rs` default bindings, both backends, `check:translations` for new labels). At implementation, confirm ⌥⇧⎋ is not a macOS system shortcut (⌥⌘⎋ is Force Quit, ⌥⎋ is Spoken Content) and that terminals pass it through harmlessly.

### Stage P3.1 — initialisms, capital-start wraps, dotted names (2026-10-24) `[fix]`

From the user, listening to the local engine (`af_alloy`, espeak-ng on): "ID", other capitals, "macOS", "GitHub" and "HTMLParser" read as one word; a wrapped line starting with a capital read as a new sentence; "Node.js" split into "Node" and "js". Commits `87785af` (initialisms, camel case), `46faac5` (dots), `60fe840` (capital wraps), `55e1f1b` (camel guard), `28e4a59` (TODO, FIXME) and the docs commit; all signed.

**What misaki-rs 0.6 did** (scratchpad probe on both lexicons):

- "ID" → `ˈɪd`. The gold lexicon has `ID` as `{DEFAULT: ˈɪd, NOUN: None}`; in Python misaki a `None` for the noun tag sends a noun to `get_NNP` (letters), but misaki-rs's `resolve_phonemes` falls back to DEFAULT, so all 65 (GB) / 67 (US) such entries, ID, IT, US, OS, AM, ETA and LED among them, always read as the word; `INITIALISMS_THAT_SPELL_WORDS` spells 16 of them. Unknown capitals (API, URL, CPU) were already spelled, as one NNP word (`ˌApˌiːˈI`) or letter by letter; espeak-ng, when on, says some as words (AWS "awz", CI "sigh", QA "kah", IDE "ide").
- "macOS" → `mˈɑːkəʊz` (a silver entry); "GitHub", "HTMLParser" and "iPhone" spelled letter by letter without espeak-ng. "iOS" is a gold entry and was right.
- "Node.js": the tokeniser makes every `.` its own token, which Kokoro gets as a full stop; `U.S.` and `e.g.` got one after each letter. `split_sentences` already needed whitespace after a stop (now pinned by a test), so defect 3 was in the phonemiser, not the splitter.
- Spaced capitals give letter names (`I D` → `ˌI dˈiː`), but the tagger reads a lone "A" as the article: "I use A P I keys" → `ɐ pˈiː ˌI`.

**Where:** `text::spell_out(text, &impl text::Lexicon)` runs in `tts::local::reading` after `plain_text` and `expand_numbers`, so for the Local backend only, like `expand_numbers`; the HTTP path is unchanged (Kokoro-FastAPI runs Python misaki with spaCy tags, and an OpenAI-compatible server reads "API" itself). After `expand_numbers`, versions, decimals and times are words ("version two point zero", "seven AM"), so no digit-dot-digit reaches the dot rule and `a.m.` after a number is already "AM". `text::Lexicon` is implemented for misaki's `Lexicon`: `has` asks the gold table; `spell` builds misaki's NNP form from the gold letter names (every stress secondary, the last primary, `append_s` for a plural) and returns a `[ID](/ˌIdˈiː/)` phoneme override, which the tagger cannot misread.

**Rules**, on each whitespace-delimited word:

1. Dotted initials: two or more groups of one letter, or a capital and a small letter ("Ph", "Sc"), each followed by a stop and not by a letter or digit (`U.S.`, `e.g.`, `i.e.`, `a.k.a.`, `Ph.D.`, `U.S.A.-made`), are spelled. Their last stop stays when no letter or digit follows in the chunk, and, for capital initials, before a capitalised word ("in the U.S. Analysts expect"); "e.g." and "i.e." never keep it. An "'s" joins the spelling (`U.S.'s` → `jˌuːˈɛsɪz`); any other apostrophe ending (`ID'd`) leaves the word as written, because misaki reads it as one token and drops an override that covers only the letters.
2. A `.` between letters or digits, unless both are digits, is " dot " (`Node.js`, `file2.txt`, `3.x`, `ort.pyke.io`); one starting a word, or after `( [ { " ' /`, before a letter is "dot " (`.env`, `.NET`). Other stops stay.
3. All capitals, two or more letters, with an optional `s` or `'s`: first `SAID_AS_WORDS`, respelt also in lower case (AJAX, CORS, CUDA, FIXME "fix me", JSON "jason", README "read me", TODO "to do", TOML "tom el", UNIX, YAML "yam el"). Then `INITIALISMS_THAT_SPELL_WORDS` (AD, AM, BA, BI, COO, ER, ETA, ID, IDE, IT, LED, MA, OS, PA, SEC, US) are spelled unless the word next to them across a space is a capitals word the lexicon knows that is not on that list ("DO IT NOW", "LET US KNOW"); "I" or "A" counts only with such a word beyond it ("I AM HERE"), so "the ID I sent", "in the US I learned" and "your US ID card" are spelled. Other capitals the gold lexicon has as written, in lower case or capitalised, stay (NASA, NATO, GIF, RAM, ROM, LAN, WAN, GUI, PIN, SIM, SQL, ASAP, CRUD, MIDI, JPEG; NOTE, MUST). The rest are spelled when they have 2–4 letters or no vowel (`HTTPS`); longer ones with a vowel stay (UNESCO, SIGKILL).
4. Camel case is cut at lower→upper and before the last capital of a run followed by two small letters (`Git Hub`, `mac OS`, `HTML Parser`, `i Phone`, `O Auth`, `Fast API`); rule 3 then runs on each part. The word stays whole when the gold lexicon has it (iOS, YouTube, McDonald, SaaS, LaTeX, IoT: 57 GB / 61 US camel entries), for `Mc` names, and when a part is neither a word the lexicon knows, a single letter nor capitals (`PostgreSQL`, `XMLHttpRequest`, `PyTorch`): espeak-ng reads those whole, and misaki would spell the fragment.
5. Single letters ("a", "I", "B", `3D`, `H2O`) never change.
6. Square brackets reach misaki as parentheses (`plain_text`): it reads `[text](…)` as a pronunciation, so an earlier `[` in the text would pair with an override and swallow the words between ("[TODO: fix the API before release]" read only "API before release"). misaki reads both as a space; `normalize` has already turned markdown links into their text.

**The packet's allow-list:** NASA, NATO, GIF, RAM, ROM, LAN, WAN, GUI, PIN, SIM, SCUBA, LASER, RADAR, CAPTCHA and ASAP are gold entries (upper or lower case), so the lexicon keeps them words; only JSON and YAML were missing.

**Verified phoneme outputs** (`reading` → Kokoro symbols; `initialisms_read_as_letter_names`, `a_stop_inside_a_name_is_no_sentence_end` and the live test):

| Text              | GB (`bm_lewis`)                 | US (`af_alloy`)                |
| ----------------- | ------------------------------- | ------------------------------ |
| Your ID           | `jˌɔː ˌIdˈiː`                   | `jˌʊɹ ˌIdˈi`                   |
| Call the API URL. | `kˈɔːl ði ˌApˌiːˈI jˌuːˌɑːˈɛl.` | `kˈɔl ði ˌApˌiˈI jˌuˌɑɹˈɛl.`   |
| I use API keys    | `ˌI jˈuːs ˌApˌiːˈI kˈiːz`       | `ˌI jˈus ˌApˌiˈI kˈiz`         |
| Install macOS.    | `ɪnstˈɔːl mˈak ˌQˈɛs.`          | `ɪnstˈɔl mˈæk ˌOˈɛs.`          |
| Node.js is fast.  | `nˈQd dˈɒt ʤˌAˈɛs ɪz fˈast.`    | `nˈOd dˈɑt ʤˌAˈɛs ɪz fˈæst.`   |
| NASA launched it. | `nˈasə lˈɔːnʧt ɪt.`             | `nˈæsə lˈɑnʧt ɪɾ.`             |
| Update to iOS 17. | `ˈʌpdAt tʊ IQˈɛs sˈɛvəntˌiːn.`  | `ˈʌpdAt tʊ ˌIOˈɛs sˈɛvəntˌin.` |
| the U.S. team     | `ði jˌuːˈɛs tˈiːm`              |                                |
| at 7 a.m.         | `at sˈɛvən ˌAˈɛm.`              |                                |

**Live model** (ignored `local_voice_reads_initialisms`, int8, both voices): every sample synthesises, and "URL" spelled lasts 1.13 s (GB) / 1.24 s (US) against 0.94 / 0.75 s for the word "earl", which the test asserts. In scratchpad runs, "ID" spelled took 1.12 / 1.07 s against 0.92 / 0.69 s for the word `ˈɪd`, "the US" 1.30 / 1.57 s against "the us" 1.18 / 1.22 s, and spaced "I D" 1.14 / 1.24 s, about the same as the override. No speech recogniser is installed here, so the audio was measured, not heard.

**Capital-start wraps (P3.0 rule 7):** a line starting with a capitalised word now joins on the fit test alone, as the user asked. The lower-case neighbour stays for a first word with a digit or in capitals: without it a `git log --oneline`, Claude Code's tool output of commit lines, compiler errors (`E0308 …`), tickets (`ABC-123 …`) and log levels (`INFO …`, `WARN …`) joined into one sentence. A sentence wrapped before a number ("released in / 2024 and …") therefore still stays apart when no neighbour starts in lower case, as on main. A capital after a wrapped hyphen joins too ("Anglo-Saxon"). The unmarked list and the web heading over "Install" now join; their test was adjusted and a test of a sentence wrapped before "Visual Studio Code" and "Claude Code" added. The P3.0 corpus, idempotence and the review cases are unchanged.

**Deviations from the packet:**

- Capitals the lexicon knows as an English word are not spelled; spelling every 2–4 capital token would spell NOTE, MUST, DO, NOT and every shouted word. The initialisms that collide with words are an explicit list instead.
- Letters reach misaki as a phoneme override in its own NNP stress, not as spaced capitals (the "A" problem above); the letter names come from the gold lexicon for the voice's dialect.
- `iOS` stays whole (gold `ˌIOˈɛs`), the same reading as the packet's `i OS`.
- No lower-case spelling rule: misaki already spells `js`, `css`, `npm`, `cd`, `ls`, `md`, `rs` and `txt` (gold or silver entries, or letter by letter), and espeak-ng does too, so the packet's condition ("if poor") was not met.
- Additions found in the sweep: dotted initials, README, TODO and FIXME, the camel guard, and digits keeping the P3.0 neighbour rule.
- `split_sentences` needed no change; the first commit's packet name ("sentence end needs a space after the stop") did not fit, so the dot fix carries the pinning tests.

**Edge cases**

- Empty or whitespace-only text, non-ASCII letters: unchanged; the step only reads ASCII letters and stops.
- Brackets: `[ID]` gives `[[ID](/…/)]`, which misaki reads as the letters; markdown links are already their text after `normalize`.
- Plural and possessive: `IDs`, `ID's`, `APIs` take misaki's `append_s` (`ˌIdˈiːz`).
- Letters next to digits are spelled alone (`MP3` → `ˌɛmpˈiː θɹˈiː`, `ID3`, `EC2`); `3D`, `H2O`, `x86`, `COVID-19` and `v2.0` (expanded first) are unchanged.
- A letter with no gold name would give spaced capitals; a test checks A–Z in both lexicons.

**Known limits:** misaki spells a shouted function word it tags as a proper noun ("THIS IS NOT A DRILL" → T-H-I-S … N-O-T; "ABOUT US", "FOR YOUR EYES ONLY"), as before and as Python misaki does; a `[word](+2)` stress override would keep them words. A title or abbreviation stop ("Dr. Smith", "etc.", "vs.") reached Kokoro as a full stop; fixed in the follow-up below. A missing space after a full stop ("end.Next") reads "dot". An initialism that spells a word reads as the word in shouted text ("ENTER YOUR ID") and is spelled in mixed text ("Contact US"). LoRA reads "lo R A" (espeak-ng said "low rah"). Roman numerals are letters ("World War I I"), as before. Units such as `mAh` read as letters and a word. Unknown capitals of 5+ letters with a vowel (README aside) are left to misaki, which mostly spells them. The other `NOUN: None` entries still read as the word, as on main: function words (IN, IS, OR, HE, ME) and LA, DA, EST, SAT, MOT, GI, TA, SI, DOE, RIP and ISO ("the DA" → `dˈə`, "EST" → `ˈɛst`). Shouted plurals and past tenses ("BUGS", "DIED", "PETS WELCOME") are spelled by misaki's proper-noun rule, as on main. Capital initials before a capitalised word now pause even mid-sentence ("the U.S. Senate").

**Review fixes (adversary, 2026-10-24):** brackets as parentheses (rule 6; it was a blocker: `[TODO: fix the API …]` lost every word before the override); "I", "A" and the initialisms on the list no longer count as shouted neighbours; codes and capitals keep the P3.0 neighbour rule; `'s` and other apostrophe endings after initials; the stop after capital initials before a capitalised word; the last spoken word found once, not per word (102,000 words in 18 ms in release). The reviewer's probe sets (58 sentences, 10 wrap cases) and a 20,000-text fuzz of the atoms above ran without a panic.

**Failure and reversibility:** pure functions on the text of one request; no stored state, settings or migration. Reverting the commits restores the old reading.

**Verified:** `cargo test` 241 pass / 5 ignored (22 new: spelling, shouted neighbours, camel case, dots, initials, brackets, the capital, number and code wraps, and six against the real lexicons); the ignored live tests `local_voice_speaks_and_stops` and `local_voice_reads_initialisms` against the real int8 model with `bm_lewis` and `af_alloy`; `cargo clippy --all-targets` clean; `cargo build` 0 compiler warnings; `bun run lint`, `check:translations`, `format:check` and `bun run build` pass. `bindings.ts` unchanged (no commands or types touched).

**Not verified:** how it sounds (no recogniser here; nobody has listened); the app itself (not launched); the scratchpad corpus of P3.0 (80- and 120-column replies, review cases outside the tests) was not re-run, only the cases kept as tests.

**Follow-up: titles and abbreviations (2026-10-24, `fix(text): titles and common abbreviations lose their stop`).** The user heard a pause after "Mr.", "Mrs." and "Dr.": `split_sentences` did not split there, but misaki-rs still read the stop as a full stop. `text::expand_abbreviations` runs in `reading` after `plain_text`, before `expand_numbers` (so "No." still sees its digit), Local only. One table, `text::ABBREVIATIONS`, drives it and `split_sentences` (case-insensitive there; `INITIALS_AND_MONTHS` holds the rest of the old list), so the two stay in step. Expansion is case-sensitive, by place:

- Titles before a capitalised word, or "and"/"or"/"&" ("Mr. and Mrs. Smith"): Mr Mister, Mrs Missus, Ms, Dr Doctor, Prof, St Saint, Mt Mount, Sgt, Lt, Col, Revd also without the stop when a capital then a small letter follows ("Dr Who", not "Col A"); Capt, Gen, Rev, Hon only with it ("Gen Z", "Rev. 2" stay). The stop goes. misaki spells "Ms" and "Miz", so "Ms" is the override `[Ms](/mˈɪz/)`. Without the stop misaki already read Mr, Mrs, Dr and St as the words.
- Closing: St Street (when no name follows: "Baker St. is long"), Jr, Sr, etc "et cetera". The stop stays at the end of the text or before a capitalised word ("etc. Then we left"), else goes ("etc., and more").
- Inline: vs "versus" (misaki said "vees" in US), approx; the stop stays only at the end of the text.
- "No." before a digit ("No. 5", "No.5") is "number"; `split_sentences` splits after "no." otherwise, as before.

**Verified:** `cargo test` 247 pass / 5 ignored (4 new tests, splitter cases added); ignored live `local_voice_reads_initialisms` (int8, `bm_lewis`, `af_alloy`): "Dr. Smith met Mr. Jones at 7.30pm." reads `dˈɒktə smˈɪθ mˈɛt mˈɪstə ʤˈQnz at sˈɛvən θˈɜːti pˌiːˈɛm.` (GB) and `dˈɑktɚ smˈɪθ mˈɛt mˈɪstɚ ʤˈOnz æɾ sˈɛvən θˈɜɾi pˌiˈɛm.` (US), one stop; `cargo clippy --all-targets` clean; `format:check` passes. The text fed to misaki is "Doctor Smith met Mister Jones at seven thirty PM." ("PM" is a lexicon word, read `pˌiːˈɛm`).

**Known limits:** "St." between two capitalised words is Saint ("Mount St. Helens", but also "Baker St. Then we left" → "Baker Saint Then"). A closing word keeps its stop before any capital ("King Jr. Day" pauses). Months ("Jan. 5") still reach misaki with their stop. "Dr." for Drive is Doctor before a name. Shouted forms ("DR. SMITH") are untouched.

### Phase 3 research notes (2026-10-25; verified against the repo unless marked external)

- **Licences:** `misaki-rs` 0.6.0 is **MIT** (Michele Yin; registry `LICENSE`, Cargo.toml `license = "MIT"`), lexicons and tagger weights embedded (`src/data.rs:6-21`, `src/g2p.rs:116-118`); its README defers data licensing to hexgrad's misaki (Apache-2.0, external). Stemmer chain per `cargo tree`: `misaki-rs → language-tokenizer 0.1.0 (WTFPL) → waken_snowball 0.1.0 (BSD-3-Clause)`, so a Snowball port is linked and its notice must ship. `ort`/`ort-sys` rc.14: MIT OR Apache-2.0. ONNX Runtime: MIT plus `ThirdPartyNotices.txt` in microsoft/onnxruntime (external); pyke's cached archive (`~/Library/Caches/ort.pyke.io`) carries no licence files, so P3.3 fetches them at the onnxruntime tag `ort-sys` rc.14 links (version in its build script). `handy-keys` 0.3.4: MIT. Kokoro-82M: Apache-2.0 (HF model card, external). espeak-ng (GPL-3) is never linked or bundled: no notice.
- **Blend syntax** (verified in `~/Developer/Kokoro-FastAPI` @ `6179791`, 2026-10-11): `openai_compatible.py:58-59` accepts items `name` or `name(weight)` joined by `+` or `-`; `tts_service.py:211-245` defaults a weight to 1, divides by the total (`VOICE_WEIGHT_NORMALIZATION`, default on) and sums the weighted tensors; the language follows the first letter of the string (`docs/inno-tune.md:57`). Mouthy accepts `+` only and passes HTTP strings through unparsed.
- **Voice file layout:** 522,240 B = 510 rows × 256 f32 (`catalog.rs:31-32`, `local.rs:37`); `Voice::load`/`parse_style_rows`/`style_row` (`local.rs:608-640`) pick row = token count of the piece (`:468`). Averaging whole tables then slicing equals slicing then averaging (linear), so a blend is a per-element weighted average. `Dialect::for_voice` (`local.rs:113-128`) keys the lexicon off the first character.
- **Session plumbing:** `speak_selection_on_main` (`actions.rs:148-221`) = toggle check → `speech_backend` (`:274-292`) → `capture_selection` (`:177`) → normalise/split → `start_speech` (`:348-375`, starts the two threads only) → store the `Session`, overlay, tray (`:205-220`). `local_model_files` `:294-303`; `preload_local_voice` `:308-338`; `SessionGuard` `:470-495` ends a session from the playback thread. `ensure_loaded` (`local.rs:353-373`) → `LocalSynth::refresh` (`:428-440`) reloads only a changed voice file (522 KB), never the model. `Session::new` sets `released_since_start = false`.
- **Hotkeys:** both backends register every default binding (`shortcut/tauri_impl.rs:20`, `handy_keys.rs:431`, `shortcut/mod.rs:377-400`; order is `HashMap` order); `sync_bindings_with_defaults` (`settings.rs:414-440`, tests `:613-626`) adds a new default to stored maps; `handle_shortcut_event` (`shortcut/handler.rs:20-38`) dispatches by id to `ACTION_MAP` (`actions.rs:606-613`, test `:630`); `change_binding` (`shortcut/mod.rs:86`) has no cross-binding collision check. Settings UI: `GeneralSettings.tsx:24`; labels `settings.general.shortcut.bindings.<id>.{name,description}` (`HandyKeysShortcutInput.tsx:309-313`). Clipboard text is read inside `clipboard.rs:165-290` (`stringForType` on `NSPasteboardTypeString`, ~`:287`), only within `capture`. CLI flags are forwarded through the single-instance matcher (`cli.rs`, `lib.rs:374-380`).
- **Voice plumbing:** `tts_voice: String` (`settings.rs:192-193`); `change_tts_voice_setting` (`shortcut/mod.rs:667-673`) trims, stores, preloads; `voice_path`/`is_voice_name` (`local.rs:209-222`, allows `[A-Za-z0-9_-]`) are used by `catalog.rs:106` and `installed_voices` (`local.rs:225-237`); `list_model_files` (`commands/models.rs:85-127`) reports one `missing_voice`; `ModelsSettings.tsx:24-31` marks the active voice by id equality, `:164` "Use" overwrites `tts_voice`. Generated commands return `{status:"error"}` and never throw (`bindings.ts:155-161`); `updateSetting` rolls back only on a throw (`settingsStore.ts:157-172`) and the `tts_voice` updater (`:71`) ignores the result.
- **Icons:** `src-tauri/icons/` is Tauri's default set (placeholders, 12 Oct); `tauri.conf.json:24-30` lists five files; tray PNGs in `src-tauri/resources/` selected in `tray.rs:180-200`, loaded via `BaseDirectory::Resource` (`lib.rs:168-178`); `bundle.resources` = `resources/**/*`. The frontend already uses `MouthyWordmark.tsx`. `tauri icon <png>` (external): square PNG with transparency → icns, ico, png set, plus `icons/android` and `icons/ios`.
- **Release facts:** app-data `~/Library/Application Support/com.azizi.mouthy`, `~/Library/Logs/com.azizi.mouthy`, `~/Library/WebKit/com.azizi.mouthy`, `~/Library/Caches/com.azizi.mouthy`, `~/Library/Preferences/com.azizi.mouthy.plist` all exist on this Mac. `ort` 2.0 final has not shipped (newest `2.0.0-rc.15`, 2026-10-19): the rc.14 pin stays. Apple Developer Program: USD 99/year (external).
- **Rejected:** a separate blend settings field (one string, one parser, what the server speaks); the server's `/v1/audio/voices/combine` (needs `ALLOW_LOCAL_VOICE_SAVING`); auto-update on ad-hoc builds (A10); a silent clipboard fallback for speak-selection (user, 2026-10-21); localising the preview sentence; a TS mirror of the voice parser (the UI formats the string, the backend validates).

Common rules for every Phase 3 stage: one branch + PR per stage, squash-merged; conventional commit; `CHANGELOG.md` `## Unreleased` block on top (the first stage creates it); `cd src-tauri && cargo test && cargo clippy --all-targets`, `bun run lint`, `bun run format:check`, `bun run check:translations`, `bun run build` before the PR; `src/bindings.ts` regenerated (`cd src-tauri && perl -e 'alarm 20; exec @ARGV' ./target/debug/mouthy --start-hidden`) and committed when a command or exported type changes; if 1Password SSH signing locks, stop and report; never touch the user's Kokoro-FastAPI; restore the dev settings store byte for byte (P3.2 changes it once: record the new sha256 in Current State).

### Stage P3.2 — "Speak clipboard" binding, default ⌥⇧⎋ `[feat]`

- Status: **done** (PR #15, merged `c7c10ac`) · Implementer: chief-implementer
- Files: `settings.rs:279-300` (second default binding `speak_clipboard`, `option+shift+escape` / `alt+shift+escape`); `settings.rs:414-440` (`sync_bindings_with_defaults` leaves a new default **unbound** — empty `current_binding` — when its keys equal another stored binding's, compared parsed so `alt`/`option` match; test); `shortcut/mod.rs:86` (`change_binding` rejects keys equal to another binding's current keys, same parsed comparison; test); `actions.rs` (an `Input { Selection, Clipboard }` parameter threaded `request_speech` → `speak_selection_on_main`, which keeps its name; `SpeakClipboardAction` + `ACTION_MAP` entry; `pub fn speak_clipboard(app, source)` beside `speak_selection`); `clipboard.rs` (`pub fn read_text() -> Result<Option<String>, String>`: refuses with a message when the pasteboard carries `org.nspasteboard.ConcealedType` or `org.nspasteboard.TransientType` (A12; external convention), caps the text at `CLIPBOARD_MAX_CHARS` = 20,000 before `normalize` since it runs on the main thread; the non-macOS stub returns `Err` as `capture_selection` does); `overlay.rs` + `src/overlay/` (new `OverlayView::ClipboardEmpty`, string "Clipboard is empty", and the concealed notice reuses `Error { message }`); `cli.rs` + `lib.rs:374-380` (`--speak-clipboard` through the single-instance matcher, for agent testing; no signal); `GeneralSettings.tsx:24` (second `<ShortcutInput shortcutId="speak_clipboard">`); 26 locales (`settings.general.shortcut.bindings.speak_clipboard.{name,description}`, the overlay string, the group description now plural); README (usage, the terminal mouse-reporting note); CHANGELOG. No bindings regeneration (no command or type changes).
- Approach: the clipboard path skips `capture_selection` and `copied_while_held_at` (no ⌘C, so no copy echo) and feeds the string into the same normalise → split → `start_speech` → session tail. Toggle semantics unchanged (`should_toggle_stop`). No tray item.
- Verify: `cargo test` (binding/action pairing; sync collision; `change_binding` collision; cap); from the agent shell `./target/debug/mouthy --speak-clipboard` on a running dev instance speaks the clipboard (Tauri or handy-keys backend irrelevant); empty clipboard → "Clipboard is empty"; `check:translations`.
- Risks: ⌥⇧⎋ has no documented macOS binding (⌥⌘⎋ Force Quit, ⌥⎋ Spoken Content; external) — confirmed by the user at H22; a 0.3.0 downgrade drops the binding (acceptable).
- Implementation Notes:
  - **Decisions:** `actions::Input { Selection, Clipboard, Preview }` is threaded `request_speech` → `speak_selection_on_main` (name kept) and stored as `Session.input`. `clipboard::read_text() -> Result<ClipboardText, String>` with `ClipboardText { Text, Empty, Secret }`: plain `stringForType` in an autoreleasepool; `Secret` when any item carries `org.nspasteboard.ConcealedType`/`TransientType`. `CLIPBOARD_CHAR_CAP` (20,000 chars) applies before `normalize`. Overlays `ClipboardEmpty` ("Clipboard is empty", also when nothing speakable remains) and `ClipboardSecret` ("Clipboard holds a secret and was not read") hide after 2 s. Default `speak_clipboard` = `option+shift+escape` (`alt+…` off macOS). CLI `--speak-clipboard` through single-instance; no signal, no tray item.
  - **Collision guard:** `shortcut::keys_collide` = same key and either side's handy-keys `Modifiers::matches` the other (`option_left` clashes with `option`; left vs right do not), falling back to Tauri parse equality. `change_binding` refuses before unregistering. `settings::sync_bindings` adds a colliding new default unbound (`current_binding = ""`); unbound bindings register nothing on either backend, are skipped by the backend switch and the Secure Input fallback, and show "Not set". The backend-switch result reports `unbound_bindings` apart from `reset_bindings`. Frontend: `updateBinding` rolls back to `""`; `resetBinding` checks the status and toasts.
  - **Deviations:** `read_text` returns a three-way enum, not `Option<String>`, and a secret gets its own overlay state instead of `Error { message }` (calling a password manager's copy "empty" would mislead). The collision check goes through handy-keys `Modifiers::matches`, not parsed equality alone (review must-fix). `bindings.ts` did change (`ImplementationChangeResult.unbound_bindings`): hand-edited in specta format, since regenerated by the P3.4/P3.5 exports.
  - **Verification:** `cargo test` 261 pass at merge, 287 pass / 7 ignored after P3.5 and P3.4; the ignored live `read_text` test passes. Live hotkey checks not run (debug launch hands off to the release app); they fold into H22.
  - **Known limits:** left and right side-specific modifiers count as distinct; the clash message is English only; an older build reading an unbound `""` binding logs an error and continues.

Contents: LICENSE, NOTICE, handy-keys 0.3.4 (MIT), misaki-rs 0.6.0 (MIT), misaki by hexgrad (Apache-2.0), language-tokenizer 0.1.0 (WTFPL), waken_snowball 0.1.0 (BSD-3-Clause), ort/ort-sys rc.14 (MIT), rodio 0.23.0, cpal 0.18.2, Kokoro-82M (Apache-2.0, downloaded not bundled), ONNX Runtime v1.31.0 (MIT) with its upstream `ThirdPartyNotices.txt` verbatim. The ONNX Runtime tag comes from ort-sys's `dist.tsv`; re-fetch the notices when `ort` moves. Command `open_third_party_notices` (Rust `open_path`, no capability); the About row sits in the Acknowledgments group, keys `settings.about.thirdPartyNotices.*`.

No GPL/LGPL/AGPL; the npm production tree is permissive (lightningcss's MPL is build-time only).

### Stage P3.4 — voice preview command `[feat]`

- Status: **done** (PR #17, merged `8d211f1`) · Implementer: chief-implementer-deep
- Files: `actions.rs` — extract the session-registration tail of `speak_selection_on_main` (`:205-220`: store the `Session`, overlay, tray) into a helper shared with the preview; `speech_backend` (`:274-292`) and `local_model_files` (`:294-303`) take the voice as a parameter; `pub fn preview_voice(app, voice) -> Result<(), String>` builds the backend **in the command** (so a missing model returns `Err` to the UI) and then posts `preview_on_main` which stops any current session and registers a preview `Session` with `released_since_start = true` (so the first hotkey press stops it; this amends the T5 invariant: a hotkey stops speech after a counted release **or for a preview session**); `Speech{sentences: split_sentences(normalize(PREVIEW_TEXT)), captured_at: now}` with `PREVIEW_TEXT` = "Hi. This is how I sound when I read your text aloud."; when a preview session ends, `SessionGuard` calls `preload_local_voice` so the next hotkey does not pay the voice reload; `SessionGuard` emits a `speech-state` event (`{state: "started" | "ended", preview: bool}`) for the UI's playing state. `commands/mod.rs`: `#[tauri::command] preview_voice`, registered in `lib.rs`; `bindings.ts` regenerated; CHANGELOG. Overlay shows "speaking" as for any session (no new state).
- Verify: `cargo test` (`PREVIEW_TEXT` splits into ≥ 1 chunk at both `max_chunk_chars`; voice-parameterised `speech_backend` builds `Backend::Http{voice}` from the argument; preview session is stoppable on the first press); from the dev console `commands.previewVoice("bm_lewis")` speaks (dev app-data has `bm_lewis.bin`), `previewVoice("af_alloy")` returns the missing-file error; `speech-state` events arrive.
- Risks: a preview stops the user's own session (intended); the `speech-state` event must be emitted from the playback thread without holding any lock the main thread takes (T2 rule).
- Implementation Notes:
  - **Decisions:** `preview_voice(app, voice) -> Result<(), String>` is a sync command doing read-only checks (`VoiceSpec::parse` for Local, trim for HTTP, missing `ModelFiles` → `Err`), then posts `preview_on_main`: `stop_session_on_main` (emits `ended`) + `start_session_on_main`. `start_session_on_main` is shared with the hotkey path (starts the threads, stores the `Session`, overlay, tray, emits `started`) and returns `Err` if the threads fail; the result comes back over an mpsc channel, because `run_on_main_thread` runs inline when already on the main thread (tauri-runtime-wry 2.11.4). `Session::preview(stop)` sets `released_since_start = true` and `input: Input::Preview`. `PREVIEW_TEXT` = "Hi. This is how I sound when I read your text aloud." `speech-state` = `{state: started|ended, preview: bool}`; no specta event types, the UI types it by hand. `speech_backend`/`local_model_files` take the voice; `http_backend` and `sentences_for` are split out with hotkey behaviour unchanged.
  - **Deviations:** `speech-state` is emitted on the main thread where `TtsState` changes, not from `SessionGuard` on the playback thread, so a replaced session's `ended` always precedes its successor's `started`. `preload_local_voice` runs when any session ends (`stop_speaking_on_main`, `end_session_on_main`), except when one preview replaces another, not only after a preview.
  - **Verification:** `cargo test` 287 pass / 7 ignored. Not unit-tested: exactly one `ended` per session and the preload after a preview (both need an `AppHandle`). The live `commands.previewVoice` check was not run (H22).

### Stage P3.5 — voice blending, local and HTTP `[feat]`

- Status: **done** (PR #16 with P3.5b, merged `86c7362`) · Implementer: chief-implementer-deep
- Files: new `tts/voice.rs` (`VoiceSpec { parts: Vec<(String, f32)> }`; `parse(&str)`: reject `-` **before** the name check, split on `+`, items `name` or `name(weight)`, names by the `is_voice_name` rule (which stays in `local.rs` for `voice_path`/`installed_voices`), weights finite and > 0, default 1, at most 4 parts; `Display` writes the canonical form; `dialect()` = first part, non-`a`/`b` prefix → American); `local.rs` (`ModelFiles`: `voices: Option<Vec<(PathBuf, f32)>>`; an empty or unparseable stored value → missing voice, never an error; `missing_voice_ids()`; `ensure_loaded`/`open` build `Vec<(FileStamp, f32)>`; `Voice::blend(parts)` requires equal table lengths, output Σ wᵢ/Σw · tableᵢ; `LocalSynth::refresh` compares (stamp, weight) pairs sorted by path — test that a weight-only change reloads; `Dialect::for_voice` takes the spec); `shortcut/mod.rs:667-673` (`change_tts_voice_setting`: for **Local** parse and store the canonical form, returning the parser's message on failure; for HTTP store the trimmed string unparsed — server ids may contain `-`, `.` or `:`); `commands/models.rs:48-60, 85-127` (`ModelFileList.active: Vec<(String, f32)>` and `missing_voices: Vec<String>` replacing `missing_voice`); `ModelsSettings.tsx:24-39, 83-96, 164` (active parts marked; "Use" replaces a blend, explicitly); `settingsStore.ts:71` (`tts_voice` updater checks `status`, throws on error so rollback and a sonner toast happen, `refreshSettings` on success so the canonical string shows); `bindings.ts` regenerated; CHANGELOG; AGENTS.md "Local backend" gets one line on blends.
- Verify: `cargo test`: parse (`bm_lewis`, `bm_lewis+bm_george`, `bm_lewis(2)+bm_george(1)`, spaces, bad weight, `-`, lone `+`, five parts, a path-like name); blend on two synthetic 2-row tables at 2:1; dialect of `jf_alpha+bm_lewis` is American; mixed-length tables error; `missing_voices` names every absent part; weight-only refresh. Manual (dev store Local, restored after): `bm_lewis+af_alloy` with only `bm_lewis.bin` → Models page lists `af_alloy` missing; a rejected string toasts and rolls back. Listening is H22.
- Implementation Notes:
  - **Decisions:** `tts/voice.rs` `VoiceSpec`: `+` only, `-` rejected first, `name(weight)` with finite weights > 0, at most 4 parts, canonical `Display`, no TS mirror. Only Local validates and canonicalises in `change_tts_voice_setting` (which also emits `model-files-changed`); HTTP stores the trimmed string. `Voice::load` is all-or-nothing; a blend is Σ wᵢ/Σw · tableᵢ (shares in f64) with an equal-length check; `refresh` compares (path, weight) pairs sorted. Dialect comes from the first part: any prefix other than `b` reads American. `settingsStore`'s `tts_voice` updater throws on `{status:"error"}` so rollback and the toast work, and calls `refreshSettings` on success.
  - **Deviations:** names differ from the packet. `ModelFiles.voices` is `Vec<{id, path, weight}>` (not `Option<Vec<(PathBuf, f32)>>`); `missing_voices: Vec<String>` names each part once; an empty or unparseable value counts as missing, never an error. `ModelFileList` gains `voice_parts: Vec<VoicePart{id, share}>` and `language` instead of `active`. The Models page shows "In use · NN%" per part, offers Use on each part of a blend, and "Download missing" fetches every part. `installed_voices` omits `.bin` stems containing `-` (review).
  - **Verification:** `cargo test` 287 pass / 7 ignored after P3.4. Ignored live `local_voice_blends` (int8): Lewis 3.30 s, 70/30 Lewis/George 3.31 s, 30/70 3.36 s, George 3.40 s. Measured, not heard (H22).
  - **Known limits:** a hand-placed hyphenated voice file is unusable on Local; a weight written `1e-3` is rejected by the `-` rule.

With no voice installed every voice is listed and the switch is hidden; a running download stays listed when the switch is off. Checklist steps 14, 15 and 28 match. Not run live.

### Release R3 — v0.4.0, unsigned, with the Homebrew cask `[chore]`

- Status: superseded by Release R4 (the 0.4.0 draft was deleted unpublished; the tag stays) · Implementer: chief-implementer
- Part 1 as R2: one signed commit `chore(release): v0.4.0` on `chore/release-0.4.0` (versions in `package.json`, `tauri.conf.json`, `Cargo.toml`, `Cargo.lock` via `cargo update -p mouthy --offline`; `## Unreleased` → `## 0.4.0 — <date>` with an intro line; README dmg name), plus `BUILD.md` "Release" section (the exact commands, `SHA256SUMS`, the cask update) and `docs/phase3-checklist.md` (H22 items, below); PR, squash.
- Checklist contents (`docs/phase3-checklist.md`; P3.2–P3.5 and P3.7 had no live run, so these are their only end-to-end checks):
  - Menu-bar glyphs on light and dark, idle, speaking and warning.
  - ⌥⇧⎋ reads the clipboard in Terminal, iTerm2 and a browser and triggers nothing else; a password copied from 1Password gives "Clipboard holds a secret"; an empty clipboard gives "Clipboard is empty".
  - Settings refuses a shortcut the other binding already uses (toast).
  - About → Third-party licences opens the file.
  - Models page: all 54 voices with language labels; Preview/Stop on an installed voice; Download a non-English voice, then Preview it.
  - Settings picker: Preview, Use, blend slider → Use → the hotkey speaks the blend; a 70/30 Lewis/George blend sounds like a mix.
  - Preview in HTTP mode against Kokoro-FastAPI; the first hotkey press during a preview stops it.

### Stage P3.8 — --speak-file (2026-11-02) `[feat]`

- Status: **merged** (#31, `0241058`) · Implementer: chief-implementer-deep
- Goal: a Claude Code skill reads its replies aloud through Mouthy, "creating a more conversation-like back and forth"; each new reply cuts off the previous one.
- Design: `--speak-file <PATH>` (also `--speak-file=<PATH>`). The handoff hands the file to `actions::speak_file`, which reads it before posting; the main thread stops any session (selection, clipboard, preview or file), normalises and splits as the clipboard path does, and starts a file session that the first hotkey press stops, as a preview does. `--stop`, the tray and the overlay × stop it as any session.
- Implementation Notes:
  - **Decisions:** `cli.rs`: `speak_file: Option<PathBuf>`, with `conflicts_with_all` against `--speak-selection`, `--speak-clipboard` and `--stop`, so the launching process rejects a combination and neither the handoff nor startup has to pick one; `CliArgs::handed_over_speak_file(args, cwd)` re-parses the forwarded argv with clap (both spellings, and the second process already validated it) and joins a relative path onto the cwd the single-instance plugin passes (2.4.0 sends the caller's `current_dir`, or `""` when that is not UTF-8). `actions.rs`: `Input::File` ("file"); `Session::preview` became `Session::without_hotkey(stop, input)` for previews and files; `CLIPBOARD_CHAR_CAP` became `TEXT_CHAR_CAP` and `cap_clipboard_text` became `cap_text(text, input)`; `read_text_file` refuses a relative path (left only when the caller's working directory could not be determined; the message says so and asks for an absolute path) and anything but a regular file, reads at most `FILE_BYTE_CAP` (4 × 20,000) bytes, decodes lossily and caps at 20,000 characters (the byte cap always holds 20,000 whole characters, so a character it cuts in half is dropped by the character cap). `speak_file_on_main` stops the current session first, so a file that cannot be spoken still cuts off the speech and then shows why: a read failure is `Error { "Cannot read <file name>: <reason>" }` (full path in the log), an empty file is `Error { "The file had nothing to speak" }` (the preview's "nothing to speak" style, no new overlay state or strings), a missing model is `ModelMissing`. The speaking overlay is the normal one.
  - **Startup:** the existing startup path ignores `--speak-selection`, `--speak-clipboard` and `--stop` on purpose (README: they "just launch the app"); only `--speak-file` is handled, at the end of `setup`, resolved against the process's own cwd. Here the file is read on the main thread, bounded by the regular-file check and the byte cap; a handed-over file is read on the single-instance worker. `run_on_main_thread` runs inline there (tauri-runtime-wry 2.11.4), after `TtsState` is managed; the session's synth thread waits for the startup preload's `LOADED` lock.
  - **Script use:** when no Mouthy is running, the command is Mouthy itself and does not return until Mouthy quits. A script should start Mouthy first (`open -a Mouthy`, or the binary with `--start-hidden` in the background), give it a moment, and then call the binary for each message. A call made before the new Mouthy has bound its socket finds none, starts a second Mouthy and speaks there (the plugin's `notify_singleton` falls back to launching on `NotFound`/`ConnectionRefused`). A handed-over call returns before the running Mouthy reads the file, so the file must outlive it: write each message to a new file and delete old ones later.
  - **Deviations:** the packet asked for a preload when a file session ends "like the others"; only a preview preloads (`end_session_on_main`, `stop_speaking_on_main`; P3.4's note saying any session does is not what the code does), because a file, like the selection and clipboard, reads in the settings voice, which is then still loaded. A file that replaces a preview loads the settings voice itself; a failed file request that replaced a preview preloads. `Input::Text`/`speak_text(app, text)` are named `Input::File`/`speak_file(app, path, source)` beside `Input::Selection`/`Clipboard` and `speak_clipboard(app, source)`.
  - **Edge cases:** two handoffs in quick succession are spoken in order, since the plugin's accept loop runs the callback inline, one connection at a time, and posts reach the main thread in order; the replaced session sends exactly one `ended` before the new `started` (both from `speak_file_on_main`), and its playback thread's later `end_session_on_main` is ignored as it no longer owns the session. A FIFO would block `open` and with it every later handoff, including `--stop`, so it is refused before opening (unit test fails with a 2 s timeout without the check). Settings and the backend are read on the main thread, not in the callback: the single-instance listener starts during that plugin's setup, before the store plugin's, and `app.store()` panics without `StoreState`. Invalid UTF-8 becomes U+FFFD. A relative path from a caller whose cwd the plugin could not send is refused, not read from Mouthy's own cwd (`/` for a Finder launch).
  - **Verification:** `cargo test` 296 pass / 7 ignored (289 before; new: three CLI parse tests including the flag conflicts, file read and cap, refusals, FIFO, empty notice; the preview stop test covers files too); `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo build` (only the tray-translation notice), `bun run lint`, `bun run prettier --check .` clean. No bindings change. No live run: the user's release Mouthy.app holds `/tmp/com_azizi_mouthy_si.sock`, so a debug launch would hand off to it.
  - **Manual check** (built app from this branch, installed in `/Applications`): (1) `echo "Hello. This is the first reply, and it goes on for a while so there is time to cut it off." > /tmp/m1.txt && /Applications/Mouthy.app/Contents/MacOS/mouthy --speak-file /tmp/m1.txt` speaks with the "Speaking…" overlay. (2) While it speaks, `echo "Second reply." > /tmp/m2.txt && /Applications/Mouthy.app/Contents/MacOS/mouthy --speak-file /tmp/m2.txt`: the first stops mid-sentence and the second starts. (3) Speak m1 again and press ⌥⎋ once: it stops. (4) Speak m1, then `mouthy --stop`: it stops. (5) `cd /tmp && /Applications/Mouthy.app/Contents/MacOS/mouthy --speak-file m2.txt` speaks m2 (a relative path; use the space form, since the shell does not expand `~` after `=`). (6) `mouthy --speak-file /tmp/nope.txt` shows "Speech failed: Cannot read nope.txt: No such file or directory (os error 2)"; an empty file shows "Speech failed: The file had nothing to speak"; both stop any speech first. (7) Quit Mouthy, then `mouthy --start-hidden --speak-file /tmp/m1.txt` launches it and speaks once the voice has loaded; the command stays in the foreground until Mouthy quits. (8) `mouthy --speak-file /tmp/m1.txt --stop` exits with a clap error and nothing changes.
  - **Known limits:** at a first launch the overlay webview may still be loading when the session starts and miss "Speaking…" (speech, `--stop` and, once the frontend registers them, the hotkeys work). A non-UTF-8 path cannot be handed over (the plugin sends `std::env::args`). A path on a stalled network volume blocks the handoff loop like any read.

### Stage P3.9 — --voice for --speak-file (2026-11-02) `[feat]`

- Status: **merged** (#34, `580bf2e`) · Implementer: chief-implementer
- Goal: different Claude Code sessions speak in different voices ("is it possible to have different voices for each Claude session?" → "add that now"); each session passes its own voice on every call. Folded into 0.5.0, published 2026-11-04.
- Design: `--voice <SPEC>` (also `--voice=<SPEC>`), only with `--speak-file`: a voice id or blend in the voice setting's syntax. A voice that cannot read the file falls back to the Settings voice with a log warning; the overlay is unchanged.
- Implementation Notes:
  - **Decisions:** `cli.rs`: `voice: Option<String>` with `requires = "speak_file"` and the same `conflicts_with_all` as `--speak-file`, because clap waives a `requires` whose target conflicts with a flag given (`--voice x --speak-clipboard` parsed without it). `CliArgs::speak_file_in`/`handed_over_speak_file` return `SpeakFile { path, voice }`; `actions::speak_file(app, path, voice, source)` carries the raw string to `speak_file_on_main`, which resolves it on the main thread in `file_speech` (settings are read there, as in P3.8). Resolution, `actions::usable_voice(settings, models_dir, requested)`: Local, `VoiceSpec::parse` then the canonical `to_string()`, then `ModelFiles::new(…).missing_voices()` must be empty (the Models page's own check, reused); HTTP, the trimmed string if not empty, as the voice setting passes it. Any `Err` (parse error, `not downloaded: a, b`, no app-data dir, empty) logs `Cannot read the file in the voice '<requested>' (<reason>); using the settings voice <voice>` and uses `settings.tts_voice`. The backend is then `speech_backend(app, &settings, &voice)` as in `preview_voice`, so a missing model is still `ModelMissing`. The log line `Speaking the file in <voice>` names the voice used.
  - **Preload rules:** `Session::other_voice` replaces the `Input::Preview` checks: true for every preview (as before) and for a file whose resolved voice differs from `settings.tts_voice`; `stop_session_on_main` now returns the stopped `Session`, and `end_session_on_main` takes it with `Option::take_if`. So: a session in another voice that ends (`end_session_on_main`) or is stopped (hotkey, tray, `--stop`, overlay ×) preloads the settings voice; one replaced by a preview or a file does not, since the new session loads its own voice; a failed file request, or a preview or file whose threads fail to start (`start_session_on_main` returns `Err`), that replaced one does preload (`preload_after`). A file in the settings voice behaves exactly as in P3.8. Back-to-back files in another voice therefore keep that voice loaded between them; only the end of the last one reloads the settings voice (a voice switch reloads only the voice table and, for another dialect, the G2P, not the ONNX session).
  - **Deviations:** none from the packet. `speak_file_on_main` takes the voice as a fourth argument rather than a struct.
  - **Verification:** `cargo test` 301 pass / 7 ignored (296 before; new: `--voice` in both spellings, `--voice` refused without `--speak-file` and with the other speak flags, a downloaded voice or blend resolves canonical, a missing voice or part / unparseable spec / no models dir fails with a reason, HTTP trims and never looks for files; the handoff test covers the voice). `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo fmt --check`, `cargo build` (only the tray-translation notice), `bun run lint`, `bun run prettier --check .` clean. `mouthy --voice bm_lewis` and `mouthy --speak-file x --voice bm_lewis --stop` exit 2 with a clap error (debug binary). Not unit-tested: `file_voice`'s fallback and the preload calls (they need an `AppHandle`). No live run: the user's release Mouthy.app holds the single-instance socket.
  - **Manual check** (built app from this branch in `/Applications`; dev app-data has `af_alloy`, `bf_emma`, `bm_lewis`; let `M=/Applications/Mouthy.app/Contents/MacOS/mouthy`): (1) `echo "This is the first session speaking." > /tmp/v1.txt && echo "And this is the second session." > /tmp/v2.txt`; `$M --speak-file /tmp/v1.txt --voice bm_lewis`, then after it ends `$M --speak-file /tmp/v2.txt --voice bf_emma`: two different voices. (2) `$M --speak-file /tmp/v1.txt --voice 'bm_lewis(70)+bf_emma(30)'` speaks a blend, closer to bm_lewis. (3) `$M --speak-file /tmp/v1.txt --voice af_nicole` (not downloaded) speaks in the Settings voice, and the log has `Cannot read the file in the voice 'af_nicole' (not downloaded: af_nicole); using the settings voice …`; `--voice 'nope-voice'` does the same with the parse error. (4) `$M --voice bm_lewis` and `$M --speak-file /tmp/v1.txt --voice bm_lewis --stop` exit with a clap error and nothing is spoken. (5) After (1), press ⌥⎋ on a selection: it reads in the Settings voice without a reload delay.

## Phase 4

Linux on Wayland and non-English pronunciation, for 0.6.0. Planned 2026-11-17, reviewed by four seats the same day and rewritten to the arbiter's rulings. The review files stay outside the repo, in `~/.claude/projects/-Users-azizi-adeyemo-Developer/plans/`: `phase4-arbiter-rulings.md` (binding), `phase4-review-{correctness,integration,simplicity,adversary}.md`, `phase4-asks-briefing.md`, scout reports `phase4-scout.md` and `phase4-scout-linux.md`. Ask numbers in this section (H1, H3–H10) are Phase 4's own, separate from the Human Asks table at the top.

Success looks like: on the Bazzite laptop a desktop shortcut reads the highlighted text aloud and a second press stops it; the clipboard shortcut works; a missing `wl-clipboard` or `espeak-ng` is explained in Settings, not silent; each of the seven language voices reads a paragraph of its own language intelligibly; the macOS app reads English exactly as 0.5.0 does.

### Phase 4 decisions

- **D1 Wayland only.** No X11 work (no xdotool, xclip, arboard). An X11 session keeps what compiles today; nothing is promised for it.
- **D2 Hotkeys on Wayland.** No new `KeyboardImplementation` variant and nothing persisted. A pure helper `utils::is_wayland_session(wayland_display, xdg_session_type)` decides at run time; when it is true the Tauri backend registers nothing (`tauri_impl` init, register and unregister return early), because `global-hotkey` is an X11 API. Settings shows a notice with the three commands to bind in the desktop's shortcut settings (`mouthy --speak-selection`, `--speak-clipboard`, `--stop`, with the real path: `$APPIMAGE` when set, else `current_exe`) whenever the effective backend registers nothing. handy-keys stays selectable: it reads evdev and works on Wayland once the user is in the `input` group; documented as opt-in, with the caveat that `input` membership lets any process of that user read every keystroke, and the Fedora Atomic step (copy the `input` line from `/usr/lib/group` to `/etc/group` before `usermod`). A failed handy-keys start already falls back to Tauri (`shortcut/mod.rs:41-54`), which on Wayland lands on the notice. Toggle semantics need no change: every CLI request is `Trigger::Other` (`actions.rs:228-235`) and `should_toggle_stop` (`actions.rs:193-195`) is true for it.
- **D3 Selection and clipboard on Wayland go through `wl-paste`**, never keystroke injection. Selection = the PRIMARY selection (`wl-paste --primary --no-newline --type text`); clipboard = `wl-paste --no-newline --type text`. The resolved absolute path is spawned (menu-launched apps lack Linuxbrew on `PATH`); output is decoded lossily; stdout is drained on a reader thread while a 500 ms deadline is polled (a pipe holds 64 KiB, so reading after exit deadlocks on a long selection); stderr is mapped: only "No selection", "Nothing is copied" and "No suitable type" mean empty, a timeout or anything else is an error notice. `wl-paste --list-types` naming `x-kde-passwordManagerHint` maps to `ClipboardText::Secret`. The read never runs on the main thread: when Mouthy itself owns the selection (text highlighted in Settings, or the notice's Copy button) `wl-paste` waits on the blocked owner. Nothing is bundled; a missing `wl-paste` is an error notice and a Settings status line. `input::send_copy`'s Linux and Windows branches (`input.rs:177-199`) are dead under this decision and are deleted.
- **D4 Repeat guard.** On Wayland a `--speak-selection` / `--speak-clipboard` request is ignored until 300 ms have passed with no such request (each ignored request restarts the wait), so a desktop that repeats a held shortcut cannot stop what it started. `--stop` and `--speak-file` are never ignored. macOS is unchanged.
- **D5 Overlay on Linux.** Default `Minimal` when the GTK layer shell is active (KDE Plasma), else `None` (on GNOME the overlay is an ordinary window that takes focus). The stored default is a static serde default written at first read (`settings.rs:230-237`, `:380-381`), before the overlay window exists (`lib.rs:250`, `overlay.rs:106-126`), so the choice is made once, at the launch that creates the store, after `create_overlay` set `LAYER_SHELL_ACTIVE`. Because notices are invisible with style `None` (`overlay.rs:510-512`), Settings carries found / not-found status lines for `wl-paste` and `espeak-ng` on every desktop.
- **D6 AppImage and `GDK_BACKEND`.** Tauri's AppImage hook exports `GDK_BACKEND=x11`. Mouthy overrides it only when `APPIMAGE` is set (a deb/rpm user's own `GDK_BACKEND` survives), and `MOUTHY_GDK_BACKEND` always wins as the escape hatch. Whether the AppImage defaults to native Wayland is decided by smoke tier 2, not assumed (Handy keeps x11): P4.1 ships the mechanism with x11 as the default, P4.2 flips it if tier 2 passes. The WebKitGTK renderer port and `webkit2gtk-sys` are deferred until a smoke run shows ghosting.
- **D7 Packaging and CI.** One workflow `linux.yml` on `ubuntu-24.04` (planned on 22.04; A22 failed, see P4.0), x86_64 only: a check job on pull requests and pushes to `main` (`cargo test`, `clippy -D warnings`), and a bundle job on a `v*` tag or manual dispatch that builds deb, rpm and AppImage and attaches them to the draft release the user created. Not a required check (a docs-only PR never reports it). ORT is the CPU `download-binaries` build. Nothing beyond the release page: no Flatpak, AUR or COPR; the Homebrew cask stays macOS-only.
- **D8 The voice decides the language, as one resolved value.** `ModelFiles` carries a `language: catalog::VoiceLanguage`, resolved once when the files are built: the first voice's language (`VoiceLanguage::of`, made case-insensitive and `pub(crate)`), American English for an unknown prefix (custom voices and the `BM_Lewis` test keep working), and American English for any language this build has no phonemiser for (0.5.0's behaviour, so each language switches on in the stage that adds its phonemiser). Everything language-dependent reads that one value: phonemiser, text preparation, sentence marks, chunk size, preview sentence, the espeak-ng requirement. `ModelFiles::reading_as(language)` replaces it, which is the only hook H8's answer needs. No new `Language` enum; `Dialect` stays English-only.
- **D9 Phonemisers.** English: misaki-rs, unchanged, with the optional espeak-ng word fallback. Spanish, French, Italian, Brazilian Portuguese, Hindi: espeak-ng, one call per whole sentence (Kokoro's own pipeline uses espeak-ng for exactly these five: `kokoro/pipeline.py` `LANG_CODES` `e→es f→fr-fr h→hi i→it p→pt-br`). Japanese: an in-process port of misaki's cutlet kana table (`misaki/cutlet.py`; Kokoro's `JAG2P()` defaults to `version='cutlet'`, `misaki/ja.py:251`), no espeak-ng. Mandarin: in process, jieba-rs + `pinyin` + a port of misaki's modified `transcription.py` tables (H4). espeak-ng's `cmn` is rejected: tones 1 and 4 both print `5` under `--ipa`.
- **D10 espeak-ng stays a subprocess** (GPL-3, never linked or bundled). It is looked up for every voice whose language needs it, whatever the English toggle says: `ModelFiles.espeak: Option<PathBuf>` plus a separate `english_fallback: bool` that only the English arm honours (`tts_espeak_fallback` defaults to `false`, `settings.rs:341`, and must not switch Spanish off). The requirement is checked in `actions::speech_backend`, so the hotkey and the preview show the notice; a `--voice` (or `--lang`) request that needs espeak-ng on a machine without it falls back to the Settings voice with a log line, as an unknown voice does (`usable_voice` does the lookup; see the Resolutions).
- **D11 English text preparation never runs on other languages.** `expand_abbreviations`, `expand_numbers`, `spell_out` and the accent folding in `plain_text` (`FOLDS`, the combining-mark drop) are English-only. Per language:

  | Step                                                               | English | es fr it pt hi                  | ja                          | zh              |
  | ------------------------------------------------------------------ | ------- | ------------------------------- | --------------------------- | --------------- |
  | `normalize` (reflow, URLs, markdown)                               | yes     | yes                             | yes                         | yes             |
  | `plain_text` shared rules (brackets, quotes, dashes, soft hyphens) | yes     | yes                             | yes                         | yes             |
  | `plain_text` accent folding                                        | yes     | no                              | no                          | no              |
  | abbreviations, numbers, spell-out                                  | yes     | no (espeak-ng reads the digits) | no (own number reading, L3) | no (own, L4)    |
  | NFC on input                                                       | no      | yes, once H3 is approved (L1a)  | no                          | no              |
  | sentence marks                                                     | default | default; Hindi adds `।` `॥`     | fullwidth, no space needed  | fullwidth, same |

- **D12 HTTP backend untouched.** `normalize` + `split_sentences` output is what 0.5.0 sent; the language-aware splitting is Local-only. `normalize`'s `reflow` (shared, `text.rs:639-643`) is not taught CJK marks, so hard-wrapped CJK text may join lines with a space: a known limit.
- **D13 Single instance on Linux is D-Bus** (`com.azizi.mouthy.SingleInstance`, shared by AppImage, rpm and dev builds; needs a session bus), not the `/tmp/com_azizi_mouthy_si.sock` socket, which is macOS's. The handed-over call waits for the callback there, so a second process returns only after the request was taken. The single-instance plugin is registered before the log plugin (`lib.rs:332-364` vs `:376`), so a key press does not open, or rotate away, the running app's log file.
- **D14 Signals, tray, autostart:** unchanged. SIGUSR2 only on Linux; the tray and `tauri-plugin-autostart` already cover Linux and are checked on the laptop.
- **D15 macOS untouched** apart from the shared pronunciation path, whose English output is pinned by a golden test of phoneme strings.
- **D16 Source hygiene:** no upstream commit hashes in comments or test names; no Linux-only `cfg` on an exported command or type (`bindings.ts` is generated on macOS), so new commands exist everywhere and return a neutral value off Linux.

**Resolutions (2026-11-17, via the liaison):**

- **H8 resolved — option (2):** the voice decides by default; an override (a Settings choice and a `--lang en` flag) lets a non-English voice keep reading English. The user's Claude voice skill will pass the flag. L1b is unblocked once L1 merges.
- **H5 resolved — (b):** `jpreprocess` + `jpreprocess-naist-jdic` approved; L3b measures the app-size change and reports it to the user before merging.

- **H3 resolved:** `unicode-normalization` 0.1 approved as a direct dependency (L1a after L2).
- **H1 approved, with a repo constraint:** Actions is enabled but the allowlist is "selected" (GitHub-owned and verified creators only, no custom patterns) and default workflow permissions are read-only. Arbiter decision: P4.0's workflow uses only `actions/checkout` and `actions/upload-artifact`, installs Rust (`rustup`, pinned) and bun with plain run steps instead of `dtolnay/rust-toolchain`, `oven-sh/setup-bun` and `swatinem/rust-cache`, and declares `permissions: contents: write` on the bundle job only. Caching waits for a yes on `actions/cache` (asked). **Amended the same day:** the user allowlisted `dtolnay/rust-toolchain`, `oven-sh/setup-bun` and `swatinem/rust-cache`, so the workflow uses them as first planned; the `actions/cache` ask (H11) is withdrawn.
- **H9 partly resolved:** the Bazzite laptop runs **KDE Plasma**; P4.1 designs for KDE (layer-shell, Klipper, data-control available). Desktop and tier 0 step 1 received (Stage S, Bazzite smoke results); steps 2, 4 and 5 pending.
- **H10 sent to the user** after P4.0's first bundle run (run 44113156905, artifact `mouthy-linux`); results pending.
