H2 (`webkit2gtk-sys`) is not asked: it went to Deferred with the WebKitGTK renderer port. No credentials, accounts or paid tiers are needed: the workflow uses `GITHUB_TOKEN`, and Linux builds are unsigned like the Mac ones.

### Phase 4 common rules

- One branch and one PR per stage, squash-merged, conventional commit, signed (if signing fails, retry once, then stop and report; never commit unsigned). Agents never push tags or publish.
- Before the PR: `cd src-tauri && cargo test && cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings && cargo fmt --check`, `bun run lint`, `bun run format:check`, `bun run check:translations`, `bun run build`. `src/bindings.ts` is regenerated and committed when a command or exported type changes (recipe in Current State).
- `CHANGELOG.md`: lines go under `## Unreleased`, in the `### Linux` or `### Languages` subsection P4.0 creates (a stage that lands before P4.0 creates `## Unreleased` and its own subsection). After a rebase that touches `Cargo.lock`, `cargo update -p mouthy --offline`.
- Verification has three places, and each stage says which applies: **Mac** (this machine; agents), **Linux CI** (the `linux.yml` check job; for a stage merged before P4.0 exists, the first run after P4.0 covers it), **laptop** (the user, through the smoke run or the R6 checklist). An agent never claims a laptop result.
- No dependency outside H3, H4 and H5(b), each only once approved. Delete, don't disable.
- Never start, stop or signal the user's Kokoro-FastAPI; its venv is read-only (run its Python with `-B`). Never touch the running release Mouthy.app. Restore the dev settings store byte for byte.
- An implementer who downloads a non-English voice for a listening test deletes it afterwards: the voices folder is the one the user's Claude voice skill rotates over, and until H8 is settled a non-English voice there changes how replies sound.
- Implementer: `chief-implementer` (default) or `chief-implementer-deep`, as each stage says.

### Phase 4 stages

Two lanes that share no files after P4.0. **Pronunciation** starts now and needs no approval: L1 → L2 and L3 (parallel) → L4 once H4 lands; L1a (H3), L1b (H8) and L3b (H5) are small follow-ups that nothing else waits for. **Linux** starts when H1 lands: P4.0 → P4.1 → P4.2, with the Bazzite smoke run beside it. Release R6 is last.

| Stage | Title                                         | Blocked by                         | Implementer |
| ----- | --------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------- | ----------- |
| L1    | One resolved reading language; English pinned | nothing                            | deep        |
| L1a   | NFC on input for espeak-ng languages          | L2, H3                             | default     |
| L1b   | English text in a non-English voice           | L1, H8                             | default     |
| L2    | espeak-ng sentence G2P: es, fr, it, pt-br, hi | L1                                 | deep        |
| L3    | Japanese kana in process                      | L1                                 | default     |
| L3b   | Japanese kanji readings                       | L3, H5 = (b)                       | deep        |
| L4    | Mandarin in process                           | L1, H4                             | deep        |
| P4.0  | Linux CI, compile fixes, packaging, doc stubs | H1                                 | default     |
| S     | Bazzite smoke run                             | tier 0: H9; tiers 1–2: P4.0, H10   | the user    |
| P4.1  | Wayland speak path                            | P4.0 (reads tier 0 if in)          | deep        |
| P4.2  | AppImage native-Wayland default               | P4.1, smoke tier 2                 | default     |
| R6    | Release 0.6.0 with the checklist              | all merged stages, H8, H6, H7, H10 | default     |

### Stage L1 — one resolved reading language; English pinned `[refactor]`

- Status: **complete** (#44) · Implementer: chief-implementer-deep
- Blocked by: nothing (not H3, H4, H5 or H8)
- Files: `src-tauri/src/tts/catalog.rs` (`VoiceLanguage::of`, `:119-135`: `pub(crate)`, first letter compared case-insensitively; the module comment at the top still holds after this stage); `src-tauri/src/tts/local.rs` (`Dialect::for_voice` `:122-129` replaced by a `VoiceLanguage` → `Dialect` mapping; `ModelFiles` `:147-229` gains `language` and `english_fallback`, `dialect()` goes; `Reading` `:438-441` becomes `{ language, english_fallback, espeak }`; `ensure_loaded` `:387-392`; `build_g2p` `:583-596`; `text_to_ids` and `reading` `:737-749`; `plain_text` `:839-872`; tests `:1356-1372`); `src-tauri/src/tts/mod.rs` (`Backend::max_chunk_chars` `:20-25` asks the files; a new `Backend::marks()`); `src-tauri/src/tts/text.rs` (`TERMINATORS`/`CLAUSE_BREAKS` `:9-11`, `split_first_clause` `:660`, `split_sentences` `:733`, `raw_sentences` `:769-793`, `split_long` `:819-843`); `src-tauri/src/tts/espeak.rs` (`g2p` `:117`, `args` `:236-241`: callers pass the `Dialect` as before); `src-tauri/src/actions.rs` (`PREVIEW_TEXT` `:47`, `:352`, `:451`; `sentences_for` `:703-710`; `local_model_files` `:741-751`); `src-tauri/src/tts/fixtures/english-phonemes.txt` (new); `.prettierignore`; `AGENTS.md` (local-backend bullets); `CHANGELOG.md` only if something user-visible changed (nothing should).
- What:
  1. **Golden test first, on untouched code.** A test in `actions.rs` runs `docs/listening-test.md` plus about 30 lines that exercise `FOLDS`, numbers, abbreviations, initialisms and curly quotes through `normalize` → `sentences_for` → the local phoneme string (a `#[cfg(test)] pub(crate)` helper in `local.rs` that stops before `token_ids`), for `bm_lewis` and `af_alloy`, with `espeak: None` so the Mac and CI agree. Output is compared with `include_str!("tts/fixtures/english-phonemes.txt")`, one `voice<TAB>phonemes` line per chunk; the fixture is regenerated only on purpose, by an `#[ignore]` test that writes it. Commit the fixture from the unmodified tree before any other edit in the branch.
  2. **Resolved language (D8).** `ModelFiles::new` sets `language` from the first voice: `VoiceLanguage::of(id)`, `AmericanEnglish` when `None`, and `AmericanEnglish` when `!local::native(language)`. `local::native` is the one list of languages this build phonemises; here it is the two English ones, and L2, L3 and L4 each add theirs. `ModelFiles::reading_as(language)` replaces the value; `ModelFiles::language()` reads it. `ensure_loaded` no longer fails on the language (only on voices), and `Reading` equality still decides when the G2P is rebuilt.
  3. **espeak split (D10).** `local_model_files` sets `english_fallback: settings.tts_espeak_fallback` and `espeak`: `espeak::find()` when the language needs espeak-ng (none yet) or the fallback is on. `build_g2p` uses espeak-ng for English only when `english_fallback` is set. `ModelFiles::unmet_requirement() -> Option<String>` (always `None` here) is called from `speech_backend` and turned into `OverlayView::Error`; L2 fills it in.
  4. **Text preparation gate (D11).** `plain_text(text, language)`: the `FOLDS` lookup and the combining-mark drop run for English only; other languages keep their letters; every other rule is shared. `reading()` runs abbreviations, numbers and spell-out for English only. No NFC here.
  5. **Local-only splitting (D12).** `text::Marks { terminators, clause_breaks, need_space }` with a `Marks::DEFAULT` equal to today's constants; `split_sentences(text, max)` keeps its signature and calls a new `split_sentences_with(text, max, &Marks)`, as do `split_first_clause` and `split_long`. `Backend::marks()` returns the default for HTTP and by language for Local: Japanese and Mandarin add `。！？` and `、，；：` and break without trailing whitespace; Hindi adds `।` `॥` as terminators; the rest use the default. `Backend::max_chunk_chars` asks the files' language (450 for all in this stage).
  6. **Preview sentence per language.** `actions::preview_text(language)` replaces `PREVIEW_TEXT`, with a sentence of the same meaning in all nine languages (Japanese in kana only). It takes the backend's resolved language, so until a language is native its voices preview in English as today; HTTP always gets the English sentence.
- Why this shape: one value, one place to change it. H8's answer, whichever it is, becomes a call to `reading_as` (L1b); each later language is one entry in `native` plus one phonemiser arm.
- Verification: **Mac:** the golden test is green before and after the refactor with a byte-identical fixture; `cargo test` (new: every prefix and `BM_Lewis`, `xx_custom` → American; a blend follows its first voice; non-native languages resolve to American; `plain_text` keeps `é ñ ç` for Spanish and folds them for English; CJK text splits at fullwidth marks with no spaces and a 300-character Chinese sentence is cut at `，`; Hindi splits at `।`; `split_sentences` on the existing HTTP fixtures is unchanged; `preview_text` covers every language); clippy, fmt. Live: preview `af_alloy`, `bm_lewis` and one downloaded non-English voice in the dev app and confirm all three sound as before. **Linux CI:** first run after P4.0. **Laptop:** nothing.
- DoD: fixture unchanged by the refactor; no `Dialect::for_voice`; non-English voices behave exactly as in 0.5.0; a later stage can switch a language on by editing `native` and adding an arm.

**Implementation Notes:**

- **PR #44** (`feat/reading-language`, 2026-11-17), small signed commits; the golden came first (`23d6633`, on 0.5.0's code) and its phonemes are byte-identical after every later commit. Review round 1 (correctness 92%, integration 85%, nothing must-fix) added the `ends_sentence` column, regenerated on unchanged splitting logic, and the follow-ups below.
- **Golden:** `actions::tests::english_reads_as_the_fixture_says` reads `src-tauri/src/tts/fixtures/english-text.txt` (a frozen copy of `docs/listening-test.md`, so L2's language paragraphs there do not move the pin) and 30 more lines, each its own input (folds and ligatures, decomposed accents, curly quotes, titles and abbreviations, initialisms, camel case, money, versions, decades, times, ranges, the minus sign, dashes, brackets, invisible characters, a 506-character sentence), through `normalize` → `sentences_for` → `tts::local::phonemes_without_espeak` (`#[cfg(test)]`; builds the G2P from the files' `Reading` with `espeak: None`). 194 `voice<TAB>ends_sentence<TAB>phonemes` lines, `bm_lewis` then `af_alloy`, so a splitter change that moves a length cut fails it too. 0 failures in 60 runs (review) and 30 more here, each a fresh process: it could flake only if one of misaki's tagger near-ties flipped, since `misaki-rs-0.6.0/src/tagger.rs:18-27` sums feature weights in `HashMap` order. Rewrite on purpose with `cargo test -- --ignored write_the_english_phonemes`. `.prettierignore` covers `src-tauri/src/tts/fixtures/`, and `.gitattributes` keeps those files' line endings (`-text`). `a_voice_in_a_language_this_build_cannot_read_reads_as_american_english` holds `zf_xiaobei`, `ef_dora`, `jm_kumo` and `hf_alpha` to `af_alloy`'s chunks, ends and phonemes on the same text; `the_server_gets_chinese_and_hindi_cut_as_before` holds the HTTP arm of `sentences_for` to `split_sentences(text, 300)`.
- **Language:** `native` = American and British English. `ModelFiles::new` takes the first voice's `VoiceLanguage::of` (now `pub(crate)`, ASCII case-insensitive), American English when the prefix is unknown, and passes it through `reading_as` from American English. `reading_as` takes a native language and otherwise keeps the language the files had, so an override this build cannot read leaves a British voice British. `Dialect::of(language)`: British for British English, American otherwise (the same result for every id as the old `b`/`B` test). `Reading { language, english_fallback, espeak }`; `build_g2p` uses espeak-ng only with `english_fallback`. `local_model_files` sets `english_fallback` from `tts_espeak_fallback` and `espeak` from `find_if_enabled` of the same setting (no language needs espeak-ng yet). `unmet_requirement()` returns `None`; `speech_backend` turns it into `OverlayView::Error` after the missing-files check, and `preload_local_voice` loads nothing (with a log line) while it is `Some`. The espeak-ng choice is `Reading::english_espeak`, tested over the four combinations without running a program.
- **Text preparation:** `plain_text(text, language)` folds accents and drops combining marks for English only; `reading` runs abbreviations, numbers and `spell_out` for English only.
- **Splitting:** `text::Marks { terminators, clause_breaks, unspaced }` with `DEFAULT`, `CJK` and `HINDI`; `split_sentences` = `split_sentences_with(…, &Marks::DEFAULT)`; `raw_sentences`, `split_long` and `split_first_clause(chunks, marks)` take the marks; `split_long` now steps by the mark's UTF-8 length. `ModelFiles::marks()` maps the language; `sentences_for` calls `split_sentences` for HTTP and `split_sentences_with` + `split_first_clause` for Local.
- **Preview:** `actions::preview_text(language)` holds the sentence in nine languages (Japanese in kana, with no は or へ particle); `preview_sentence(backend)` picks it by the resolved language, English for HTTP.
- **Deviations from the stage text:** (1) no `Backend::marks()` (`tts/mod.rs` is unchanged): `sentences_for` matches on the backend, so the HTTP arm visibly calls the unchanged `split_sentences`, which a `marks()` accessor would have left used only by tests. (2) `Backend::max_chunk_chars` is unchanged: 450 for every language here; L3/L4 add their sizes with their arms. (3) `Marks.unspaced` is a list, not `need_space: bool`: one bool for the CJK set would also let `.` and `,` break without a space and cut "3.14" or "1,500" inside Chinese text. (4) The golden reads a frozen copy of the listening test, not the live file. (5) `CHANGELOG.md` untouched: nothing user-visible.
- **Verification (Mac):** `cargo test` 319 pass / 8 ignored after review round 1 (301 / 7 before the stage); `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo fmt --check`, `cargo build` (only the tray-translation notice), `bun run lint`, `bun run format:check`, `bun run check:translations`, `bun run build` clean. `bindings.ts` not regenerated: no command or exported type changed. Two throwaway spikes, not committed: the old and new splitters compared on 200,000 random strings at four limits (`split_sentences`, `split_sentences_with(DEFAULT)`, `split_first_clause(DEFAULT)`; all identical); and Kokoro audio from 0.5.0's code and this branch, using the app-data model files read-only, gave identical sample hashes for `af_alloy`, `bm_lewis`, `ef_dora`, `jm_kumo` and `ff_siwis`, a `bm_lewis(70)+ef_dora(30)` blend, and three voices with espeak-ng on. Not run: previews in the dev app (the release app holds the single-instance socket). **Linux CI:** green on PR #44 after merging P4.0 (#43): check job run 44113570115 on ubuntu-24.04, `cargo test` 310 pass / 7 ignored (the macOS-only tests are compiled out), the English golden, non-English and HTTP pins among them, so misaki reads the same on Linux; `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings` clean.
- **For L1b–L4:** call `reading_as` before `local_model_files` decides on espeak-ng, or the lookup follows the voice's language rather than the override. `split_first_clause` gates on more than 8 whitespace-separated words, so a Chinese or Japanese first sentence (no spaces) is never cut at its first clause. `merge_short` joins short fragments with a space, also between CJK sentences. L1b changes `Session::other_voice` to "other voice or other language", so a session read under an override preloads the Settings reading when it ends. L3 adds `」』）】` to the closers used with CJK marks: `CLOSERS` has none, so in `。」` the `」` starts the next sentence. The golden shows English behaviour this stage kept: "Œuvre", "Straße", "Muñoz" and "Paulo" are spelled letter by letter, and "日本語" reads as `❓`.

### Stage L1b — English text in a non-English voice `[feat]`

- Status: **complete, PR #46 open** (option 2) · Implementer: chief-implementer
- Blocked by: L1, H8 (the user's choice among the three options)
- Files, by option. **(1) strict:** no code; `README.md` and the R6 notes say a voice reads its own language only, and the user limits the skill's rotation to `af_*`, `am_*`, `bf_*`, `bm_*` (the skill lives outside this repo, `~/.claude/skills/voice/`; changing it is the user's call). **(2) override:** `src-tauri/src/settings.rs` (a `tts_language` setting: "voice" or a language), `src-tauri/src/shortcut/mod.rs` (its `change_*` command), `src-tauri/src/lib.rs`, `src-tauri/src/cli.rs` (`--lang <code>` with `--speak-file`), `src-tauri/src/actions.rs` (`local_model_files` calls `reading_as`), `src/bindings.ts`, `src/stores/settingsStore.ts`, a dropdown in `src/components/settings/general/GeneralSettings.tsx`'s Speech group, 26 locales, `README.md`, `AGENTS.md` (CLI table), `CHANGELOG.md`. **(3) script switch:** `src-tauri/src/actions.rs` only: after the text is captured, a Japanese, Mandarin or Hindi voice whose text has no letter of its own script is read as American English (`reading_as`, then `unmet_requirement` again); `README.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- What: apply the chosen option at the single resolution point L1 made. Option (2) needs a one-control design note in the PR (a dropdown in the existing Speech group; no mockup).
- Verification: **Mac:** `cargo test` for the chosen rule; live in the dev app: an English sentence with `ef_dora` (or `jf_alpha`) reads as the option says. **Linux CI:** check job.
- DoD: the Claude voice skill's English replies are intelligible under the chosen option, or the option's limit is documented where the user will see it.

**Implementation Notes:**

- **Option (2), branch `feat/reading-language-override`,** three signed commits (backend + bindings, Settings control + 26 locales, docs).
- **Setting:** `tts_language: ReadingLanguage` (`settings.rs`), stored as `"voice"` (default) or `"en"`, the same codes `--lang` takes (a test holds the two in step). `#[serde(default)]`, so a store without the key loads `voice` with no write-back; an unknown code (say `"es"` from a later build) is salvaged to `voice` with the other fields kept; an older build ignores the key. Command `change_tts_language_setting(language: String)` (`shortcut/mod.rs`, the `change_tts_backend_setting` pattern: unknown value → warn, `voice`), then a preload. UI: `ReadingLanguageSelector.tsx`, a dropdown ("The voice's language" / "English") under the voice picker, hidden with the HTTP server as the espeak-ng toggle is; strings under `settings.speech.language`, English in all 26 locales.
- **CLI:** `--lang <en|voice>` (also `--lang=en`), only with `--speak-file`: `requires = "speak_file"` plus the same `conflicts_with_all` as `--voice` (clap's `requires` quirk, P3.9). `ValueEnum` is implemented for `ReadingLanguage` in `cli.rs`, so any other code is a clap error listing `en, voice` (exit 2). `SpeakFile` gains `language`; the handoff and startup pass it to `actions::speak_file(app, path, voice, language, source)`. **The voice skill's command:** `/Applications/Mouthy.app/Contents/MacOS/mouthy --speak-file "$FILE" --voice "$VOICE" --lang en`.
- **Dispatch (`actions.rs`):** `speech_backend` and `local_model_files` take a `ReadingLanguage`; hotkey, clipboard, tray, preview and preload pass `settings.tts_language`, a file passes `file_language(settings, --lang)` (`--lang` wins). `local_model_files` builds `ModelFiles::new`, then `read_in(files, choice)` = `files.reading_as(reading_language(files.language(), choice))`, before `with_espeak`. `reading_language`: `voice` → the voice's language; `en` → British English for a British voice, American English for every other (blends follow their first voice). The preview needs no code: `preview_sentence` follows the files' language, so an override previews the English sentence. HTTP ignores the choice.
- **Preload:** `Session::other_voice` is renamed `other_reading`: true for every preview and for a file whose voice or `ReadingLanguage` differs from the settings (`reads_unlike_settings`). Compared as choices, not resolved languages, so it stays right when L2/L3 make more languages native; while a choice resolves to the same language (`--lang en` with `af_alloy`), the extra preload finds the model loaded and does nothing.
- **Tests** (written to hold when L2–L4 extend `native`): `reading_language` over all nine languages for both choices; `file_language` precedence both ways; `read_in` through `ModelFiles` (`bf_emma`, `bm_lewis`, a `bm_` blend → British; `af_alloy`, the seven other languages, an `ef_` blend, `xx_custom` → American; `voice` leaves `ModelFiles::new`'s language alone); a `jf_alpha` read as English previews the English sentence; `reads_unlike_settings` true when only the language differs; CLI grammar (both spellings, `es`/`english`/no value refused, refused without `--speak-file` or with the other speak flags, handoff carries it); store default, round-trip and unknown-code salvage.
- **Deviations:** (1) The setting's values are `voice` | `en` rather than `voice` | a language name, so the store and `--lang` share one vocabulary; native codes can be added to both later. (2) Only `en` and `voice` are accepted, as the packet allowed. (3) `Session::other_voice` renamed `other_reading` (`actions.rs:94`) since it now covers the language. (4) The control is hidden for the HTTP server rather than shown with a note. (5) No live check in the dev app: the release app holds the single-instance socket, and on `main` no non-English language is native yet, so the override is observable only in the tests until L2/L3 merge.
- **For L2:** `usable_voice` is called before the reading language is known (`file_speech`). When it gains the espeak-ng lookup, pass it `file_language(...)` and apply `read_in` there too, or `--voice ef_dora --lang en` on a machine without espeak-ng falls back to the Settings voice for nothing. `unmet_requirement` already sees the overridden language, since `read_in` runs before `speech_backend` asks.
- **Verification (Mac):** `cargo test` 328 pass / 8 ignored (319 / 8 before); `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo fmt --check`, `bun run lint`, `bun run format:check`, `bun run check:translations`, `bun run build` clean; `bindings.ts` regenerated (new command and `ReadingLanguage` type). `mouthy --speak-file /tmp/x --lang es` exits 2 with `invalid value 'es' for '--lang <LANG>' [possible values: en, voice]`. **Linux CI:** check job green on PR #46 (run 44119054800).
- **Review round 1** (92%, nothing must-fix), applied: `CliArgs::handed_over_speak_file` logs the clap error kind when a handed-over `--speak-file` does not parse (a newer caller's flag), then the callback raises the window as before; `reads_unlike_settings` ignores the language for the HTTP backend, and the file's " as English" log suffix is Local only; `change_tts_language_setting` sends `files_changed` before the preload, as the voice command does; `tts_language` has a doc comment; README and CHANGELOG say the choice matters only where Mouthy can read the voice's language, so they hold whichever of L1b and L2 merges first; CLI tests refuse `--lang en --stop`, `--lang en --voice x` and `--lang EN`.
- **Manual check, once L2 or L3 has merged** (built app; `ef_dora` downloaded): (1) Settings voice `ef_dora`, Reading Language "The voice's language": ⌥⎋ on a Spanish sentence reads it as Spanish. (2) Switch to "English": ⌥⎋ on an English sentence reads it as English with a Spanish accent; Preview on `ef_dora` reads the English sentence. (3) Back to the voice's language; `mouthy --speak-file /tmp/v1.txt --voice ef_dora --lang en` reads English; then ⌥⎋ reads Spanish again without a reload delay. (4) With the setting on English, `--lang voice` reads Spanish.

### Stage L2 — espeak-ng sentence G2P for Spanish, French, Italian, Portuguese, Hindi `[feat]`

- Status: pending · Implementer: chief-implementer-deep
- Blocked by: L1
- Files: `src-tauri/src/tts/espeak.rs` (a sentence phonemiser beside the English word `Fallback`, which is untouched; `run` `:249` gains a reader thread and a timeout parameter); `src-tauri/src/tts/local.rs` (`native`; a `Phonemizer` enum with `Misaki` and `Espeak` arms replacing the bare `G2P` in `LocalSynth`; `unmet_requirement`); `src-tauri/src/tts/catalog.rs` (module comment); `src-tauri/src/actions.rs` (`local_model_files`: espeak-ng looked up for these languages; `usable_voice`: the same lookup); `src/components/settings/VoicePicker.tsx:232-236` and `settings.speech.voice.englishOnly` (en `translation.json:96`, 26 locales): deleted; `src/components/settings/EspeakFallback.tsx` (the found / not-found line shows whether or not the toggle is on); en `settings.speech.espeak.description` ("accent" → "language", says which languages need espeak-ng) and `settings.models.voices.description`, 25 locales; `docs/listening-test.md` (a short paragraph per language); `README.md` (under the `## Languages` heading from P4.0, or creating it); `AGENTS.md`; `CHANGELOG.md` (`### Languages`, and a **Changed** line: Spanish, French, Italian, Portuguese and Hindi voices now read their own language and no longer read English).
- What:
  - **Call:** one process per sentence, `espeak-ng -q --ipa=3 -v <es|fr-fr|it|pt-br|hi> -- <sentence>`; the text is one argument after `--` ("-5 grados" must not parse as an option), with NUL and control characters removed and digits left in (espeak-ng reads numbers per language; pre-splitting at punctuation turns "3,5" into "three, five"). Timeout 2 s; stdout drained while waiting; no cache.
  - **Punctuation:** espeak-ng prints one line per clause and no punctuation. Collect the sentence's clause marks in order (`, ; : . ! ? … — –` and `।`, each followed by a space or the end; not `¿ ¡ ( ) « » "`, not inside `3,5` or `10:30`); when the line count matches, attach each mark to its line (`।`/`॥` as `.`); on a mismatch join the lines with `, ` and end with the sentence's last mark. Quotes and brackets are not carried into the phonemes: a known limit.
  - **Symbols**, ported from `misaki/espeak.py` `EspeakG2P` (v1.0 branch), not from `to_kokoro` or `SYMBOLS` (`espeak.rs:36-49`, the English fallback table, which would turn `ɾ` into `T` and drop nasals): first remove language-switch flags with a ZWJ-aware pattern (real output is `(en)…(hi)` with a U+200D before each letter, so a plain `\([a-z]+\)` pattern matches nothing); then misaki's eleven tie replacements, where its `^` is U+200D between the two letters in espeak-ng's output (`a^ɪ→I a^ʊ→W d^z→ʣ d^ʒ→ʤ e^ɪ→A o^ʊ→O ə^ʊ→Q s^s→S t^s→ʦ t^ʃ→ʧ ɔ^ɪ→Y`); then remove remaining U+200D and `-` (French liaison); keep U+0303, the nasal tilde (`VOCAB` id 17); then the `VOCAB` filter, counting drops. Never NFC the output.
  - **Dispatch:** `native` gains the five; `unmet_requirement` returns "<Language> voices need espeak-ng. Install it with: brew install espeak-ng" when it is not found (HTTP is never asked); the hotkey and the preview pass through `speech_backend` and show the notice. A `--voice` (or L1b's `--lang`) request that needs espeak-ng on a machine without it falls back to the Settings voice with a log line, as an unknown voice does (the arbiter decision under Resolutions): `usable_voice` gains the lookup. Chunk size stays 450 for all five.
- Verification: **Mac** (espeak-ng 1.52 at `/opt/homebrew/bin`): unit tests of the pure mapping on strings captured from real espeak-ng output and pasted into the tests, asserting exact results: `ɑ̃` survives; the flags in `मैं iPhone use करता हूँ` leave no `(`; `le-z ɑ̃fˈɑ̃` loses the hyphen; `Hola, ¿cómo estás?` gets its comma and `?` back; "El 3,5 % de 1.000 personas llegó a las 10:30" keeps its numbers whole; a Hindi line with `।`; a count mismatch joins with `, `; every output symbol is in `VOCAB`. `#[ignore]` live tests per language (zero drops on the listening paragraph), and an `#[ignore]` test writing `/tmp/mouthy-<lang>.wav` with `ef_dora`, `ff_siwis`, `if_sara`, `pf_dora`, `hf_alpha` for the user to hear. The English golden fixture is unchanged. Live in the dev app: preview `ef_dora` (Spanish sentence), and with espeak-ng renamed away the preview reports the requirement. **Linux CI:** check job (pure tests only; no espeak-ng on the runner). **Laptop:** R6 checklist, with the laptop's espeak-ng version recorded (Ubuntu 22.04 ships 1.50; the fixtures are from 1.52).
- DoD: the five voices read their paragraphs with no `❓` and at most 2% dropped symbols; with the English toggle off they still read; without espeak-ng the hotkey and preview show the notice and `--voice` falls back to the Settings voice with a log line; English unchanged.

**Implementation Notes:**

### Stage L3 — Japanese kana in process `[feat]`

- Status: **in review** (#47, not merged) · Implementer: chief-implementer
- Blocked by: L1 (shares `local.rs` with L2: whichever merges second rebases and, if L2 is not in yet, introduces the `Phonemizer` enum itself)
- Files: `src-tauri/src/tts/ja.rs` (new), `src-tauri/src/tts/mod.rs`, `src-tauri/src/tts/local.rs` (`native`, the `Japanese` arm, chunk size), `src-tauri/src/actions.rs` (the refusal, after capture), `src-tauri/resources/THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.txt` (cutlet, MIT, for the ported table), `docs/listening-test.md`, `README.md`, `AGENTS.md`, `CHANGELOG.md` (`### Languages`, and the **Changed** line for Japanese voices).
- What: a port of `misaki/cutlet.py`: the `HEPBURN` table (`:14-209`, kana and its punctuation map), katakana folded to hiragana by code-point offset, and `_get_single_mapping` (`:370-423`): digraphs, small kana, `っ` → `ʔ`, `ー` → `ː`, `ん` by the next sound (`m ŋ ɲ n ɴ`), odoriji. Fullwidth ASCII is folded by offset; integers are read through a port of `misaki/num2kana.py`'s `Convert`; Latin runs go through the English path (the `Misaki` arm, American). Han characters are dropped and counted; `ModelFiles::refuses(text) -> Option<String>`, called on the main thread after capture and before the session starts, returns "Japanese text with kanji is not supported yet" when more than 20% of the text's letters are Han. `max_chunk_chars` for Japanese is set by measurement (ids per character about 2.5: start at 180 and confirm a 180-character kana sentence stays under 510 ids).
- Known limits to record: with no tagger there are no word spaces, particles `は`/`へ` read as written (`ha`, `he`), and long vowels follow the spelling; Kokoro was trained on cutlet output that had all three from UniDic. H5(b) (L3b) supplies them.
- Verification: **Mac:** `cargo test` (table rows checked against `cutlet.py`: `し→ɕi`, `じ→ʥi`, `す→sɨ`, `きゃ`, `がっこう` with `ʔ`, `ラーメン` with `ː`, `ん` before `p`, `k`, `t` and at the end; every output symbol in `VOCAB`; a kanji-heavy sentence is refused and a kana sentence with one kanji is read with one drop; Latin inside kana; numbers); the English golden fixture is unchanged; an `#[ignore]` test writes `/tmp/mouthy-ja.wav` with `jf_alpha`. misaki's cutlet cannot produce goldens here (its UniDic dictionary is not installed), so the table is checked by reading, and the sound by the user. **Linux CI:** check job. **Laptop:** R6 checklist.
- DoD: a kana paragraph is read intelligibly by `jf_alpha`; kanji-heavy text gets the notice; no espeak-ng involved.

**Implementation Notes:**

- **PR #47** (`feat/japanese-kana`, 2026-11-17), two signed commits: the CJK closers with the splitter fuzz tests (`tts/text_fuzz_tests.rs`, from the L1 tester), then the reader. No new dependency.
- **`tts/ja.rs`:** `KANA` is misaki's `HEPBURN` kana part (164 entries: 88 single kana incl. `ヷヸヹヺ`, 76 pairs), generated from the installed dict, not retyped; `SYMBOLS` is its punctuation map (17 reachable entries). Then ports of `_normalize_text` (wave dash before a digit → から, the Ainu small katakana, fullwidth ASCII and U+3000 folded by offset in place of NFKC), `_romaji_tokens` (spacing, including Python's substring `in`, so an empty reading acts as an opening bracket), `_romaji_word`, `_get_single_mapping` (pairs, small kana, `っ`→`ʔ`, `ー`→`ː`, `ん` → `m ŋ ɲ n ɴ` by the next kana), the `ʔ` space regex, and `num2kana.Convert` (hiragana). Katakana folds to hiragana as jaconv does (`ァ`–`ヶ`, `ヽヾ`; `ヷ`–`ヺ` stay).
- **Wiring:** `native` gains Japanese. `local::language_for(language, text)` reads a Japanese voice's text as American English when the text has no Japanese character (`ja::has_japanese`: kana, kanji, U+3000–30FF, U+31F0–31FF, U+FF00–FFEF), so English in a Japanese voice is exactly an American voice's reading, with no G2P reload. `ModelFiles::marks(text)`, `ModelFiles::max_chunk_chars(text)` and `Backend::max_chunk_chars(text)` now take the text and go through it (whole text: 450 and `DEFAULT` with no Japanese, else 180 and `CJK`), and so does `text_to_phonemes` per chunk. Its Japanese arm: `plain_text` (shared rules only), then `ja::phonemes` with English read by `text_to_phonemes(…, AmericanEnglish, …)` on the same misaki G2P (`Dialect::of(Japanese)` is American, so the loaded G2P already fits). `local::MAX_CHUNK_CHARS` is private now. `ModelFiles::refuses` / `Backend::refuses` (HTTP: never) are called in `speak_selection_on_main` (selection and clipboard) and `file_speech` after `normalize`, before splitting. No `Phonemizer` enum: Japanese needs only the misaki G2P that is already loaded, so the dispatch is one `if` in `text_to_phonemes`; L2's enum can absorb it.
- **Refusal:** `ja::refusal` refuses when kanji are more than 20% of the letters (`char::is_alphabetic`: kana, kanji, Latin); kanji = CJK Unified + Ext A + Ext B–F + compatibility + `々〆〇`. Message "Japanese text with kanji is not supported yet", shown through `OverlayView::Error` like every backend error (the overlay prefixes the translated "Error:"; the message itself is untranslated English, as L2's espeak-ng notice will be). No i18n key: the error path carries no keys, a translated notice needs a new overlay variant, and L3b deletes the notice. Below the threshold kanji are skipped and counted (`debug!` "Skipped N kanji").
- **English inside Japanese:** `ja::english_end` takes the whole run of non-Japanese text up to the next Japanese character (trailing spaces trimmed) as one English word when it holds a letter (any script but Japanese) or `$ £ €`, so the English path sees "£4.99", "10:30 a.m.", "(1) Install it", "naïve café" or "\"quoted\" words" whole and prepares them as English. A run that starts with `.,:;!?` leaves that mark as Japanese punctuation. A run with no letter stays Japanese: digits (with `1,500` grouping, exactly three digits after a comma, and a `.5` fraction) read by `Convert`, `%` as パーセント (`paːsento`, as UniDic would read it), other symbols through the table. Japanese brackets become misaki's `«»` before the English's own `()` are seen, so English keeps its brackets.
- **Chunk size:** measured 902 ids over 506 characters of kana prose (1.8 ids a character); the longest chunk of a 380-character sentence cut at its commas is 177 characters and fits 510 ids (`a_japanese_chunk_fits_one_inference`). Other text can pass 510 within 180 characters (review measurements: `き` × 180 = 540 ids, alternating scripts 719, number-heavy text up to 1,187); `split_tokens` then cuts the chunk, mid-word where it has no pause or space. 180 stays.
- **How expected strings were derived:** a harness (scratch only, not committed) imports `misaki.cutlet` read-only (`-B`, `TMPDIR` in scratch; UniDic is not installed, so no `Tagger`), builds `Cutlet` via `__new__`, sets `JA_WORDS` empty, and feeds `_romaji_tokens` the words a Python copy of `ja::words` makes, Latin runs mapped to `<run>` through `Cutlet.exceptions`; then `__call__`'s last steps. The unit tests' strings come from it; numbers from `num2kana.Convert` directly. A throwaway Rust test compared `ja::phonemes` with the harness on 5,000 random strings (kana, katakana, small kana, っ, ん, ー, punctuation, digits, Latin, kanji, fullwidth, ellipses, brackets, `$5`): 0 differences, after one fix (misaki's two lone sound marks are the combining U+3099/U+309A, not the spacing ゛゜); re-run with the review round's English spans, ellipses and grouping (918 strings with an English span): still 0. The mixed kana + English strings are this build's own misaki-rs output, and `english_inside_japanese_reads_as_an_american_voice_reads_it` holds each English span to `text_to_phonemes(AmericanEnglish)` of the same words.
- **Deviations from misaki, each deliberate:** (1) `ゝ`/`ゞ` repeat the previous kana's sound; cutlet emits the kana character itself, which Kokoro drops. (2) "んゃ" and other non-table kana before a small kana read normally; cutlet raises `KeyError`. (3) Numbers over nine digits are read digit by digit, then てん and the fraction (`Convert` returns an English error string), and "0.5" reads ゼロてんご (cutlet crashes); grouping commas and decimals are read as one number (cutlet splits at every non-digit). (4) Japanese brackets are dropped and the double space they leave is collapsed; misaki emits `«»[]`, which Kokoro's tokenizer drops. (5) Fullwidth `？！：；（），～` are folded to ASCII before lookup, and `…`/`‥` to `...`/`..`, as cutlet's NFKC does, so those table entries are omitted as unreachable. (6) `%` with no English around it reads パーセント (cutlet passes it through and Kokoro drops it; UniDic would give the reading). (7) English is a whole span read by misaki-rs, where cutlet passes ASCII through as letters.
- **Known limits (A27):** no tagger, so a word is a run of one script (hiragana, katakana; `ー` goes with either) and kana words run together; は/へ as particles read `ha`/`he`, を reads `o`; long vowels follow the spelling (`こう` → `koɯ`, where UniDic's pron gives `koː`); half-width katakana and decomposed dakuten (NFD, e.g. macOS file names) are not normalised, since NFKC needs `unicode-normalization` (approved for L1a, H3): L1a can give Japanese NFKC, cutlet's own first step, and delete the offset and ellipsis folds; symbols with no Kokoro token, other than `%`, are dropped; a time or number with no English around it ("10:30" between kana) reads in Japanese digit by group ("じゅう: さんじゅう"). misaki's `JA_WORDS` re-grouping (2 MB of words) is not ported: it only matters with a tagger.
- **For L3b:** replace `ja::words` only. It is the tagger's stand-in and returns `(Vec<Word>, skipped)`; give each dictionary word `Word { surface, kind: Kind::Kana(reading) }` with the reading in hiragana (`to_hiragana` over jpreprocess's katakana pron, `ー` kept), keep `Kind::English` (from `english_end`) and `Kind::Symbol` as they are; everything after (`read_kana`, spacing, `ʔ`, `num2kana`) stays. Then delete `refusal`, `KANJI_REFUSAL`, `MAX_KANJI_PERCENT`, `ModelFiles::refuses`, `Backend::refuses` and its two calls in `actions.rs`, `ja::tests::text_mostly_in_kanji_is_refused`, `local::tests::only_japanese_refuses_kanji`, and the README/CHANGELOG/listening-test/`settings.models.voices.description` wording. Measure the chunk size again: a kanji reads as about two kana, so ids per character roughly double; `MAX_CHUNK_CHARS` (180) likely drops to about 100. Consider porting `JA_WORDS` grouping if jpreprocess over-splits.
- **For L2:** the `englishOnly` picker note now skips Japanese through `READS_OWN_LANGUAGE` in `VoicePicker.tsx` (L2 deletes the note and the list with it). en `settings.models.voices.description` is one sentence written for L2 to extend by a clause: "English voices read English (British voices with British pronunciation, the rest American), Japanese voices read Japanese kana but not kanji yet, and the other voices, Chinese among them, read English for now." L2 adds its five languages (needing espeak-ng) before the last clause and turns it into "and Chinese voices read English for now"; the other 25 locales still carry the old wording that L2 rewrites. `ModelFiles::marks` and `max_chunk_chars` take the text now. `actions::tests::READ_AS_ENGLISH` is the one list of voices still read as English (`zf_xiaobei`, `ef_dora`, `hf_alpha`): L2 drops `ef_dora`/`hf_alpha` and leaves Mandarin for L4.

Fixed by `language_for` (whole chunks with no Japanese) and English spans; `english_text_in_a_japanese_voice_reads_as_an_american_voice_does` now holds `jm_kumo` and `jf_alpha` to `af_alloy` byte for byte on every chunk with no Japanese character (96 of 97) and asserts the differing chunks are exactly those with Japanese: the one line "日本語 and emoji 🎉 pass through untouched.", which is not refused and whose phonemes equal `af_alloy`'s for "and emoji 🎉 pass through untouched.". `a_japanese_voice_set_to_read_english_takes_the_whole_english_path` (L1b's `read_in` with `ReadingLanguage::English`): American, 450 characters, never refused, chunks and phonemes equal `af_alloy`'s. The `file_speech` refusal stays after `speech_backend(…, language)`, so `--lang en` is seen first.

- What:
  - **`linux.yml`**, both jobs on `ubuntu-22.04`, x86_64. Shared steps: checkout, setup-bun, `bun install`, `bun run build` (`generate_context!` needs `dist`), `dtolnay/rust-toolchain` pinned to the exact version this Mac's `rustc --version` prints on the day (1.99.1 on 2026-11-17; bumped by hand), `swatinem/rust-cache` (`workspaces: src-tauri`), apt: `libwebkit2gtk-4.1-dev libappindicator3-dev librsvg2-dev patchelf libasound2-dev libgtk-layer-shell-dev libx11-dev libxtst-dev libxrandr-dev libssl-dev pkg-config`. **check** (pull requests and pushes to `main`; concurrency per ref, cancel in progress): `cargo test`, `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings`. **bundle** (`push: tags: ['v*']` and `workflow_dispatch` with a `ref` input defaulting to `main`): `bun run tauri build --bundles deb,rpm,appimage`; print `rpm -qpR`, `dpkg -I` and the AppImage's bundled libraries (`--appimage-extract`, list `usr/lib`; shows whether `libasound.so.2` and which WebKitGTK are inside) and the `.desktop` file; `sha256sum` into `SHA256SUMS-linux`; always `actions/upload-artifact`; on a tag ref only, with `permissions: contents: write` and `GH_TOKEN: ${{ github.token }}`, poll `gh release view "$TAG"` every minute for up to 30 minutes, then `gh release upload "$TAG" … --clobber`, failing clearly if no release appears. If H1's answer is "artifacts only", the upload step is left out. The implementer checks that each action's pinned major tag exists (`gh api repos/<owner>/<repo>/releases/latest`) and that the YAML passes `prettier --check`.
  - **Compile fixes:** minimal and cfg-local; each one recorded in Implementation Notes.
  - **Packaging:** read the bundle job's `rpm -qpR` / `dpkg -I` output; add `webkit2gtk4.1` and `libayatana-appindicator-gtk3` to `rpm.depends` (and the deb equivalents) only if Tauri's bundler did not (the tray's `.build(app_handle).unwrap()`, `lib.rs:232-233`, panics without an appindicator library).
- Why first in the Linux lane: there is no Linux compiler on this Mac; every later Linux stage is proven to compile by this job. Putting the espeak-ng locations and wording here keeps the two lanes off each other's files.
- Verification: **Mac:** the full per-stage check list; `find_in` test with the new locations; the Settings line still reads the macOS wording. **Linux CI:** the PR's own check job green; after merge, one `workflow_dispatch` of the bundle job on `main` produces the three bundles as artifacts; sizes and the printed dependency lists go into Implementation Notes. **Laptop:** smoke tier 2 uses that AppImage.
