**Implementation Notes:**

P4.0 (2026-11-17, PR #43, branch `chore/linux-ci`, merged as `95957b26`). Status: check job green; first bundle run recorded below; follow-up `fix/linux-glibc-floor` raised the floor to 2.39.

- **Workflow:** `.github/workflows/linux.yml`, every job on **ubuntu-24.04** x86_64 (not 22.04, see the glibc floor). Third-party actions are pinned by commit SHA with a version comment: `dtolnay/rust-toolchain@df2a88e…` (master, `toolchain: 1.99.1` + clippy; this Mac's rustc, bumped by hand), `swatinem/rust-cache@d1527c8…` (v2.9.3, `workspaces: src-tauri`), `oven-sh/setup-bun@0c5077e…` (v2.2.0, `bun-version: 1.4.4`, this Mac's); `actions/checkout@v7` and `actions/upload-artifact@v7` stay on tags. `.github/dependabot.yml` updates the `github-actions` ecosystem weekly (cargo and npm keep security updates only, as before). Shared steps are YAML anchors on the check job, aliased in the bundle job. `permissions: contents: read` at the top. Three jobs:
  - **check:** pull requests and pushes to `main`; concurrency per ref, cancelling only for pull requests; rust-cache.
  - **bundle:** `v*` tag pushes and `workflow_dispatch` (`ref`, default `main`); read-only token; 90-minute timeout; **no cargo cache**, so a cache saved by another ref (a PR or `main`) never reaches a release binary. It builds, prints the highest `GLIBC_` symbol the binary needs, `rpm -qpR`, `dpkg -I`, the AppImage's `usr/lib` and `.desktop` file, writes `SHA256SUMS-linux` and uploads the artifact `mouthy-linux` (`overwrite: true`). It installs clippy too, since it reuses the check job's toolchain step.
  - **attach:** `needs: bundle`, only on a tag **push** (a manual dispatch never uploads, whatever ref it builds); `actions: read` + `contents: write`; no checkout, build or third-party action; 40-minute timeout. It fetches the artifact with the runner's `gh run download "$GITHUB_RUN_ID"` (no `actions/download-artifact`), then polls `gh release view "$TAG" --json isDraft` once a minute for up to 30 minutes while the answer is "not found"; it uploads (`--clobber`) only when `isDraft` is `true`, fails with an error and uploads nothing for a published release, and fails on any other `gh` error.
- **Allowlist:** the first allowlist entries were bare `owner/repo` patterns, which match no ref: GitHub refused the run at startup ("not allowed … or match one of the patterns: dtolnay/rust-toolchain, …"). The user changed them to `owner/repo@*`. The branch carries a rustup fallback commit and its revert from that window.
- **Run times (check job):** plain rustup, no cache: 4 m 14 s (run 44106126090). With the actions, empty cache: 4 m 43 s (run 44108351423, attempt 1; Test 2 m 01 s, Clippy 1 m 08 s). Cached: 2 m 34 s (attempt 2; restoring the cache 35 s, Test 22 s, Clippy 4 s); apt is about a minute of every run. With SHA-pinned actions: 1 m 43 s (run 44112047590, cache from the PR ref).
- **glibc floor (assumption A22 failed):** on ubuntu-22.04 the test binary failed to link against pyke's prebuilt ONNX Runtime (`ort-sys` rc.14, `download-binaries`): `rust-lld: error: undefined symbol: __isoc23_strtol` (also `__isoc23_strtoull`, `__isoc23_strtoll`; glibc 2.38) and `std::__cxx11::basic_string<…>::_M_replace_cold` (char and wchar_t; GCC 13's libstdc++). So the build moved to ubuntu-24.04. PR #43 declared a 2.38 floor from those symbols; the first bundle run showed the binary needs **GLIBC_2.39**, the build host's glibc, so the shipped floor is **2.39** (Ubuntu 24.04, Fedora 40+, Debian 13+, Bazzite; not Ubuntu 22.04, Debian 12 or Fedora 39), declared in `tauri.conf.json`, README, BUILD.md and AGENTS.md. Pyke's archive rules out 22.04, so a later `ort` bump can revisit the build host; building ORT from source was not tried.
- **apt list (final):** `libwebkit2gtk-4.1-dev libayatana-appindicator3-dev librsvg2-dev patchelf libasound2-dev libgtk-layer-shell-dev libx11-dev libxtst-dev libxrandr-dev libssl-dev pkg-config`. `libayatana-appindicator3-dev` replaces the plan's `libappindicator3-dev`: Tauri's CLI picks the rpm's appindicator `Requires` from whichever dev package pkg-config finds, and the Ayatana one is what Fedora ships (`libayatana-appindicator-gtk3`, the package smoke tier 0 queries). Nothing else was missing (`libdbus-1-dev` comes in with WebKitGTK's dev packages).
- **Linux-only fixes** (all cfg-local): `clipboard.rs:28,30` `COPY_TIMEOUT`/`COPY_POLL_INTERVAL` macOS-only; `clipboard.rs:123` `ClipboardText` and `:159` `poll` get the file's existing `cfg_attr(not(macos), allow(dead_code))` (nothing builds a `ClipboardText` off macOS until P4.1's `wayland` module, which should drop that attribute; `poll` is unit-tested on every platform); `input.rs:2-3` `Key`/`Keyboard` imported on macOS only, `input.rs:179` `send_copy` macOS-only with its Linux and Windows branches deleted (D3); `secure_input.rs:21,118` `Emitter` imported inside the macOS module only. The first Linux compile showed only these two warnings beyond the known ones.
- **Packaging:** `tauri.conf.json` declares the glibc floor: deb `libc6 (>= 2.39)`, rpm `libc.so.6(GLIBC_2.39)(64bit)` (2.38 in PR #43, corrected after the first bundle run). Tauri CLI 2.11 adds `libwebkit2gtk-4.1-0`, `libgtk-3-0` and the appindicator package to the deb's `Depends`, and `libwebkit2gtk-4.1.so.0()(64bit)`, `libgtk-3.so.0()(64bit)` and the appindicator `.so` to the rpm's `Requires` on its own (`crates/tauri-cli/src/interface/rust.rs`), and copies the appindicator library into the AppImage; the first bundle run confirmed all three, so no depends were added.
- **First bundle run (2026-11-17, run 44113156905, `workflow_dispatch` on `main` `95957b26`, success, ~7 min):** artifact `mouthy-linux` with `Mouthy_0.5.0_amd64.deb` (23,413,888 B), `Mouthy-0.5.0-1.x86_64.rpm` (74,191,825 B, uncompressed payload), `Mouthy_0.5.0_amd64.AppImage` (98,970,104 B) and `SHA256SUMS-linux`. Highest glibc symbol: `GLIBC_2.39`. deb `Depends`: `libgtk-layer-shell0, libc6, libayatana-appindicator3-1, libwebkit2gtk-4.1-0, libgtk-3-0`; rpm `Requires`: the matching five. The AppImage's `usr/lib` has `libwebkit2gtk-4.1.so.0`, `libayatana-appindicator3.so.1`, `libgtk-layer-shell.so.0` and `libwayland-client.so.0`, and **not** `libasound.so.2` (sound goes through the host's ALSA to PipeWire) or `libonnxruntime` (linked statically). `attach` and `check` were skipped, as they should be on a dispatch. linuxdeploy ran without FUSE problems on the runner. Still untested: `attach` (first exercised by the 0.6.0 tag; whether a re-run of `attach` alone still finds the bundle job's artifact is unproven). BUILD.md's Release section lists the Linux order: push the tag, create the one draft, wait for `attach`, check the four files, publish.
- **Desktop entry category:** the run's `.desktop` file had an empty `Categories=`. `bundle.category` would fix it but also add `LSApplicationCategoryType` to the macOS `Info.plist`, which has none today, so the deb and rpm use `desktopTemplate: linux/mouthy.desktop` instead (Tauri's default template with `Categories=Utility;`; the AppImage takes the deb's desktop file). Checked on the Mac: `tauri build --bundles app` from the follow-up branch gives an `Info.plist` identical to the 0.5.0 release's (`plutil -p` diff). Not yet seen in a Linux bundle: the next bundle run should print `Categories=Utility;`.
- **espeak-ng:** `KNOWN_LOCATIONS` is Homebrew (`/opt/homebrew`, `/usr/local`), then Linuxbrew `/home/linuxbrew/.linuxbrew/bin`, then `/usr/bin`, then `PATH`; the `find_in` test now checks the order across two known locations. The Linux not-found line (`settings.speech.espeak.notFoundLinux`, English in all 26 locales; the macOS key `notFound` is unchanged) says to use the distribution's package manager, or `brew install espeak-ng` on an immutable distribution such as Bazzite; per the user, Mouthy never suggests `rpm-ostree` (the stage text's wording is superseded, and P4.1's `wl-clipboard` line should follow the same rule). **P4.1's `wl-paste` lookup must scan the same Linuxbrew directory**, since desktop-launched apps lack it on `PATH`. The Settings line was not checked live on the Mac (the release app holds the single instance); `platform()` returns `"macos"` there, so the `notFound` key is used.
- **Docs:** CHANGELOG `## Unreleased` with `### Linux` (two lines) and an empty `### Languages`; README `## Languages` (one line) and `## Linux` (glibc floor, espeak-ng install); BUILD.md `## Linux` (floor, apt list, bundle command, dispatch and download of the artifact, the tag upload); AGENTS.md prerequisites, `espeak.rs` and `shortcut/` lines, Linux single instance (D13), Platform Notes; SECURITY.md `SHA256SUMS-linux`; bug template field `os` (was `macos`), Linux desktop and package dropdowns.
- **Concern for smoke tier 2:** Handy pins WebKitGTK 2.44.0 on 24.04 for its AppImage; this workflow takes the archive's current version. If the AppImage's overlay ghosts, that pin is one thing to try before the deferred renderer port.
- **Refactor verification:** `rg "send_copy"` → the macOS call at `clipboard.rs:85` and the definition only; `rg "Key::Control|Key::Unicode\('c'\)|0x43" src-tauri/src` → none; `rg KNOWN_LOCATIONS` → `espeak.rs` only; `rg "rpm-ostree"` outside docs/PLAN.md → none.
- **Local validation:** `cargo test` (301 pass, 7 ignored), `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo fmt --check`, `bun run lint`, `bun run format:check`, `bun run check:translations`, `bun run build`: all pass. Linux CI: 292 pass, 6 ignored (macOS-only tests are cfg'd out).

### Stage S — Bazzite smoke run `[verification, the user]`

- Status: tier 0 step 1 in (below); the rest pending · Runs on: the user's laptop; an agent only records the results here and adjusts P4.1/P4.2
- Blocked by: tier 0: H9 (nothing else; it can run today). Tiers 1–2: P4.0's bundle artifact, H10.
- **Tier 0 (no build, about 20 minutes).** (1) `echo $XDG_SESSION_TYPE $XDG_CURRENT_DESKTOP; rpm -q wl-clipboard espeak-ng fuse-libs fuse3 webkit2gtk4.1 libayatana-appindicator-gtk3 gtk-layer-shell; which -a wl-paste espeak-ng brew; espeak-ng --version`. (2) `~/probe.sh` = `#!/bin/sh` and `{ date +%T.%N; echo "PATH=$PATH"; echo "TOKEN=$XDG_ACTIVATION_TOKEN"; wl-paste --primary -n; echo " rc=$?"; } >> /tmp/probe.log 2>&1`, bound to a key in the desktop's shortcut settings; press once with text highlighted in the terminal, a text editor, Firefox and one more app, then hold the key for 2 s; paste `/tmp/probe.log`. (3) `setsid sh -c 'while sleep 2; do timeout 0.5 wl-paste --primary -n >>/tmp/bg.log; echo " rc=$?" >>/tmp/bg.log; done' &`, close the terminal, highlight text in two or three apps for 20 s, note flicker or focus loss, `pkill -f bg.log`, paste `/tmp/bg.log`. (4) copy a password from the password manager, `wl-paste --list-types` (type names only). (5) `espeak-ng -q --ipa=3 -v es -- "Hola, ¿cómo estás?"` and `-v hi -- "नमस्ते दुनिया"`.
- **Tier 1 (Handy 0.10.1 AppImage, no Mouthy code).** Does it start (FUSE)? With `HANDY_GDK_BACKEND=wayland`: is the overlay at the screen edge without taking focus? Is there a tray icon? `time ./Handy*.AppImage --cancel` while Handy runs (hand-off time per key press).
- **Tier 2 (P4.0's AppImage of Mouthy).** Download the model and `af_heart`; `--speak-file /tmp/x.txt` speaks (ONNX Runtime on this glibc; sound through PipeWire); `time ./Mouthy*.AppImage --stop`; tray; launch at login; overlay style Minimal. Native Wayland without code: `--appimage-extract`, delete the `GDK_BACKEND=x11` line in `squashfs-root/apprun-hooks/*gtk*.sh`, run `squashfs-root/AppRun`: does it start, is the overlay a layer-shell surface, any ghosting on the transparent card? Optional: `rpm-ostree install` the rpm, reboot, `ldd /usr/bin/mouthy | grep 'not found'`.
- What each result decides:

  | Result                                        | Decides                                                                                                          |
  | --------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
  | Desktop (KDE or GNOME)                        | Which notice text and checklist steps are primary; whether the overlay default can be `Minimal` on this laptop   |
  | `PATH` seen by a desktop shortcut, `which -a` | `KNOWN_LOCATIONS` for `wl-paste` and `espeak-ng` (P4.1, P4.0)                                                    |
  | `rc` per app in `probe.log`                   | README wording on what "selection" means per app; whether stale PRIMARY needs a warning                          |
  | Lines logged while the key was held           | Confirms D4's repeat guard is needed and that 300 ms is enough                                                   |
  | `bg.log` on GNOME                             | Whether a background process can read PRIMARY at all there; if not, the notice recommends the clipboard shortcut |
  | `--list-types` for a password                 | The secret marker P4.1 checks                                                                                    |
  | espeak-ng version and output                  | L2's fixtures hold on the laptop's version, or the version floor is raised in the README                         |
  | Tier 1                                        | FUSE requirement in the README; layer shell on this desktop; per-press latency                                   |
  | Tier 2 sound, `--stop` time, tray, autostart  | Packaging depends and README notes before R6                                                                     |
  | Tier 2 native Wayland run                     | P4.2: flip the AppImage default or leave x11; ghosting revives the deferred WebKitGTK port                       |

- DoD: results pasted under Implementation Notes; P4.1 and P4.2 amended where a result contradicts their assumptions.

**Implementation Notes:**

#### Bazzite smoke results

Tier 0 step 1 (2026-11-17, relayed by the liaison, dictated from memory so versions are approximate):

- Session: Wayland, KDE Plasma.
- In the base image: `wl-clipboard` 2.3.0 and `espeak-ng` 1.52.0 (both in `/usr/bin`; espeak data in `/usr/share/espeak-ng-data`), `fuse-libs` 2.9.9, `fuse3` 3.18.3, `webkit2gtk4.1` 2.54.x, `gtk-layer-shell` 0.10.1. Linuxbrew at `/home/linuxbrew/.linuxbrew/bin/brew`.
- **Not installed: `libayatana-appindicator-gtk3`.**

Consequences:

- P4.1: tray creation must not panic when no appindicator library loads (added to P4.1's What). The rpm already requires the appindicator library; the AppImage is the recommended install on Bazzite (rpm layering is discouraged), and it bundles `libayatana-appindicator3.so.1` (P4.0's first bundle run); tier 2 checks whether the tray shows.
- WebKitGTK 2.54.x on the laptop matters for the rpm only; the AppImage bundles the build host's. The deferred renderer port stays deferred until tier 2 shows ghosting.
- espeak-ng 1.52.0 is the version of the Mac probes, so L2's outputs carry over.
- FUSE 2 is present, so the AppImage should start without `--appimage-extract-and-run`.

Tier 0 step 2 (2026-11-17, the user's `/tmp/probe.log`, KDE Plasma on Wayland):

- `wl-paste --primary -n` run by a KDE command shortcut returned the highlighted text with rc 0, 5 of 5: terminal, text editor, browser, file manager, browser again.
- `XDG_ACTIVATION_TOKEN` is set in shortcut-launched processes (`kwin-79` … `kwin-85`, new for each press).
- The shortcut's `PATH`: `/home/azizi/.local/bin:/home/azizi/bin:/usr/local/bin:/usr/bin:/bin:/usr/local/sbin:/home/linuxbrew/.linuxbrew/bin:/home/linuxbrew/.linuxbrew/sbin`, so Linuxbrew comes after `/usr/bin`. Home is `/var/home/azizi`, with `/home/azizi` a symlink to it, so `/home/<user>/Applications/Mouthy.AppImage` works in a shortcut.
- Four presses and one 2 s hold logged five lines: KDE seems not to repeat a command shortcut while it is held (the liaison is confirming the count).
- Not measured: step 3 (`wl-paste` from a long-running background process). KDE has the data-control protocol, so it should work; the P4.1 build is the test.

Tier 0 step 4 (2026-11-17): after the user copied a password, `wl-paste --list-types` listed only plain-text types (`text/plain;charset=utf-8`, `UTF8_STRING`, `COMPOUND_TEXT`, `TEXT`, `text/plain`, `STRING`, `SAVE_TARGETS`): no `x-kde-passwordManagerHint` and no concealed marker. The X11-style names suggest the app copied through XWayland.

Consequences:

- D2 and D3 stand: the selection is PRIMARY through `wl-paste --primary`, the clipboard comes from `wl-paste`, and no keys are sent.
- D4's repeat guard stays (cheap, and other compositors may repeat), though key repeat is not a problem on KDE.
- A29 failed for the user's password manager: on Linux the clipboard-secret refusal is best-effort. P4.1 keeps the `x-kde-passwordManagerHint` check (native KDE and Qt password managers set it) and adds no heuristics on the text; README, CHANGELOG, the Settings notice and the checklist say that a copied password may be read aloud.
- GNOME stays unverified (no data-control); the README calls it untested, which does not block 0.6.0.

Pending: tier 0 steps 3 and 5; tiers 1 and 2 (H10).

### Stage P4.1 — Wayland speak path `[feat]`

- Status: PR #49 open, not merged · Implementer: chief-implementer-deep
- Blocked by: P4.0. Reads smoke tier 0 first; if H9 is still open when P4.0 merges, proceeds on these assumptions and records that it did: KDE Plasma, `wl-paste` under Linuxbrew or `/usr/bin`, PRIMARY readable from a background process, the desktop repeats a held shortcut.
- Files: `src-tauri/src/utils.rs` (`is_wayland_session`, pure, plus the thin `is_wayland()` that reads the environment); `src-tauri/src/shortcut/tauri_impl.rs` and `src-tauri/src/shortcut/mod.rs:33-74` (D2: the Tauri backend registers nothing on Wayland; `shortcuts_registered_by_app()` for the frontend); `src-tauri/src/commands/mod.rs` and `src-tauri/src/lib.rs` (commands `shortcut_help() -> Option<{ commandPath, desktop }>`, `None` unless the effective backend registers nothing; `find_wl_paste() -> Option<String>`, `None` off Linux; both exist on every platform, D16); `src/bindings.ts`; `src-tauri/src/clipboard.rs` (`:114-115`, `:135-136`: Linux implementations in a `wayland` module replace the stubs; other non-macOS targets keep the `Err`); `src-tauri/src/actions.rs` (`request_speech` `:376`, `speak_selection_on_main` `:407-449`: on Wayland the text is read on a capture thread before the post; the repeat guard); `src-tauri/src/settings.rs:230-237` and `src-tauri/src/lib.rs` near `:250` (D5); `src-tauri/src/lib.rs:332-396` (single-instance before the log plugin; main window `.enable_clipboard_access()` off macOS so the Copy button works in WebKitGTK); `src-tauri/src/main.rs:8-13` (the `GDK_BACKEND` rule beside the existing DMABUF line, through a pure `gdk_backend(appimage, wayland_display, override) -> Option<String>` in `utils.rs`); `src/components/settings/general/GeneralSettings.tsx:23-29` and a new `src/components/settings/DesktopShortcuts.tsx`; `src/components/settings/EspeakFallback.tsx` (unchanged) and a `wl-paste` status line in the notice; `src/components/settings/debug/KeyboardImplementationSelector.tsx:9-12` and `advanced/AdvancedSettings.tsx:21-28` (labels through i18n; a line on Linux that Event Tap needs the `input` group); 26 locales; `README.md` (`## Linux`); `AGENTS.md`; `CHANGELOG.md` (`### Linux`).
- What:
  1. **Shortcut gate (D2).** `tauri_impl::{init_shortcuts, register_shortcut, unregister_shortcut}` return early when `utils::is_wayland()`, logging once "Shortcuts come from the desktop on Wayland; bind the mouthy command". Bindings are still stored. Nothing else in `shortcut/` changes.
  2. **Settings notice.** When `shortcut_help()` returns a value, the Shortcuts group shows, in place of the two `ShortcutInput`s, one `SettingContainer` block: a sentence saying shortcuts are set in the desktop's settings, the three commands as selectable text with a Copy button each, one line on where to bind them for the detected desktop (KDE: System Settings → Shortcuts → Add New → Command; GNOME: Settings → Keyboard → View and Customize Shortcuts → Custom Shortcuts), a line that a shortcut pressed while Mouthy is not running only starts it, a line about the opt-in Event Tap backend, and the `wl-paste` status line ("Using wl-paste at …" / "wl-clipboard is not installed. Install it with: brew install wl-clipboard, or rpm-ostree install wl-clipboard"). Existing components and text styles only; no design mockup (one informational block in the existing Settings language).
  3. **Selection and clipboard (D3).** `clipboard::wayland::{read_primary, read_clipboard}` built on one `run_wl_paste(program, args, timeout)`; a `find_wl_paste()` scanning known locations then `PATH`, as `espeak::find_in` does. Not a Wayland session → `Err("Reading the selection needs a Wayland session")`.
  4. **Off the main thread.** On Wayland `request_speech` hands the request to one long-lived capture thread (a channel keeps presses in order); it reads `wl-paste`, then posts to the main thread with the result, where the existing logic runs unchanged: if a session is speaking the press stops it and the text is discarded, else it speaks. The tray's "Speak selection" and SIGUSR2 take the same route. The invariants hold: only the main thread locks `TtsState`, and `*_on_main` functions call no posting entry point.
  5. **Repeat guard (D4).** A pure `RepeatGuard::allow(now)` in `actions.rs`, used by the capture thread for Wayland requests only.
  6. **Overlay default (D5).** At the launch that creates the settings store on Linux, after `create_overlay`, `overlay_style` is written as `Minimal` if the layer shell initialised, else left `None`. `default_overlay_style` itself is unchanged.
  7. **`GDK_BACKEND` (D6).** `MOUTHY_GDK_BACKEND` set → that value. Else, when `APPIMAGE` is set, the build's default: x11, which is what the AppImage hook already exported, so this stage changes nothing for a user who sets nothing (P4.2 flips it). Outside an AppImage the variable is never touched. The effective value is logged at startup.
  8. **Single instance (D13).** Register the plugin before the log plugin; README and AGENTS.md say the Linux hand-off waits for the callback and that AppImage, rpm and dev builds share one instance name.
  9. **README `## Linux`:** install (AppImage `chmod +x`, kept at a stable path such as `~/Applications/Mouthy.AppImage`, since desktop shortcuts and autostart store the path; rpm via `rpm-ostree install`; deb), needs `wl-clipboard`, binding the three commands, what "selection" means (the text last highlighted; some apps clear it on deselect, some keep it), the opt-in Event Tap backend with the `input` group steps and caveat, `MOUTHY_GDK_BACKEND`, sound through ALSA on PipeWire.
  10. **No tray without appindicator.** On Linux, tray creation must not panic when no appindicator library can be loaded (the Bazzite base image has none; `lib.rs:232-233` `.build(app_handle).unwrap()`): log it and run without a tray (`tray.rs:505` uses `state::<TrayIcon>()` and must cope too). Settings stays reachable by launching the app again, which raises it through single instance.
- Verification: **Mac:** `cargo test` for the pure parts, which compile on every Unix: `is_wayland_session` table; `gdk_backend` table; `RepeatGuard` (a held key's 30 ms repeats are all ignored, a press 300 ms after the last one passes); `run_wl_paste` against stand-in programs (`/bin/sleep` is killed at the deadline; a script printing 200 KiB is read whole; stderr texts map to empty or error; invalid UTF-8 is read lossily); `--list-types` output with the hint → `Secret`; `shortcut_help` path choice (`APPIMAGE` wins). Live in the dev app: the Shortcuts group is unchanged on macOS; force the notice with a temporary test override to check the layout, copy buttons and translations, then remove the override. `bun run lint`, `check:translations`, bindings regenerated. **Linux CI:** check job compiles the `wayland` module and the GTK calls and runs the same tests. **Laptop:** tier 2 if the build exists, else the R6 checklist: bind, speak, stop, hold the key, Copy then speak-clipboard, password notice, `wl-paste` moved away.
- DoD: check job green; macOS behaviour and bindings semantics unchanged; on Wayland nothing is registered by the Tauri backend and the notice appears; no `wl-paste` call on the main thread.

**Implementation Notes:**

P4.1 (2026-11-17, PR #49, branch `feat/wayland-speak-path`). Designed for KDE Plasma from smoke tier 0 steps 1, 2 and 4 (Stage S); step 3 was not run. Revised the same day after the correctness (84%) and integration (88%) reviews; the integration seat traced macOS path by path and found it unchanged.

- **Reads (D3):** `clipboard::wayland` runs `wl-paste --list-types` (with `--primary` for the selection), then `wl-paste --no-newline --type <type>` with the best plain-text type offered (`text/plain;charset=utf-8`, `UTF8_STRING`, `text/plain`, `STRING`, `TEXT`), so a browser's `text/html` is never read; with none of them the read is empty. It spawns the absolute path found in `/usr/bin`, then Linuxbrew, then `PATH` (`program::find`). `program::run` reads stdout and stderr on their own threads while it waits, with a 500 ms deadline; output past 80,000 bytes (`TEXT_CHAR_CAP` × 4) stops the program and keeps the first 80,000 without waiting for stderr, which a helper may hold open; stderr keeps 4 KiB and drains the rest. At the deadline only the program itself is killed (no process-group kill: that would need `libc`); a helper holding the pipe may outlive it. Output is decoded lossily. Empty: stderr containing `Nothing is copied`, `No selection`, `No suitable type` or `Clipboard content is not available as`; anything else is the notice `wl-paste failed: <first stderr line>`, a timeout `wl-paste: no answer within 500 ms`. No `wl-paste`: "Install wl-clipboard (package manager, or brew install wl-clipboard)"; no Wayland session: "Reading the selection needs a Wayland session". A clipboard whose types name `x-kde-passwordManagerHint` is `ClipboardText::Secret`; nothing else marks a secret (tier 0 step 4). The Linux selection is capped at `TEXT_CHAR_CAP` like the clipboard.
- **AppImage environment:** Tauri's AppImage runs the classic AppImageKit `AppRun`, which prepends `$APPDIR` folders to `PATH`, `LD_LIBRARY_PATH`, `XDG_DATA_DIRS` and other paths and `chdir`s to `$APPDIR/usr`; its gtk hook sets `GTK_*`, `GDK_PIXBUF_MODULE_FILE`, `GI_TYPELIB_PATH`, `GIO_MODULE_DIR` and `GTK_THEME`, and the image bundles `libwayland-client.so.0`. When `APPDIR` is set, `program::run` gives the child the host's environment (`program::host_environment`): each listed path variable loses its entries under `$APPDIR` (component-wise, so `/tmp/.mount_X10` survives `/tmp/.mount_X1`; empty entries dropped; removed when nothing is left), and `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE`, `GTK_THEME` and `GDK_BACKEND` are removed. `WAYLAND_DISPLAY`, `XDG_RUNTIME_DIR`, `DISPLAY`, `HOME` and `LANG*` are not touched.
- **AppImage working directory:** Tauri's gtk plugin rewrites `/usr` to `././` inside `libwebkit*`, so WebKit finds `WebKitWebProcess` and `WebKitNetworkProcess` relative to the working directory `$APPDIR/usr`; the process must stay there whenever WebKit starts a helper. Tauri starts plugins inside `Builder::build()` and opens windows later, in `setup`. So on Linux `run` sets the caller's directory (`OWD`, left by the AppImage runtime; `utils::launch_dir`) just before `build()` and puts `$APPDIR/usr` back right after it: the single-instance plugin, which hands over `current_dir()`, sends the caller's directory, and no WebKit helper starts meanwhile. A first-launch `--speak-file` path is resolved against `launch_dir` too. Outside an AppImage nothing changes.
- **Threads:** on Linux (`actions::READ_OFF_MAIN_THREAD`, `cfg!(target_os = "linux")`) speak requests, hotkey releases and handed-over files go to one long-lived request thread (`send_in_order`: a `OnceLock` channel, jobs run in order, each under `catch_unwind`). A speak job reads with `wl-paste`, then posts `speak_selection_on_main(…, Some(read))`; the main thread runs the unchanged toggle: a session speaking is stopped and the text dropped, else it speaks. Releases and files never overtake a press still being read. `stop_speaking` does not queue: it counts itself in `STOPS`, resets the repeat guard and posts at once, so a stop never waits behind a 500 ms read; a speak request or file records `STOPS` when it is made and is dropped on the main thread if a stop has run since. `preview_voice` and the launch file (`speak_launch_file`, inline from `setup`, so an early handed-over `--stop` finds it speaking) post directly. A failed read is logged as an error only where its notice is shown; one dropped because the press stops speech logs at debug. Skipping the read for a press that will stop speech would need the main thread's `TtsState` off the main thread, so the read still happens. macOS posts directly as before and captures on the main thread (`capture_on_main`, the old inline match).
- **Repeat guard (D4):** `RepeatGuard::allow(now)` lets a request through only 700 ms after the last one that went ahead and after 100 ms with no request at all; ignored requests extend the wait only while a burst lasts, and a stop resets it. A keyboard waits 500–660 ms before repeating a held key, then repeats every ~33 ms, so a held shortcut starts speech once and never stops it; taps 800 ms apart toggle; a tap within 700 ms of the last is ignored. It applies on Linux to requests that are not hotkey presses (CLI, tray, SIGUSR2), checked when the request arrives; Event Tap presses keep the held-key logic, and stops, files and previews never consult it. The entry points need an `AppHandle`, so the tests cover the guard and the stop count, not the entry points themselves.
- **Shortcuts (D2):** `tauri_impl::registers_nothing()` is `utils::is_wayland()` (pure `is_wayland_session`, false off Linux); init, register and unregister return early then, and init logs it. `shortcut::desktop_binds_shortcuts` is that plus the Tauri backend selected. Commands `shortcut_help() -> Option<ShortcutHelp { command }>` and `find_wl_paste() -> Option<String>` exist on every platform (neutral `None` off Linux).
- **Settings notice:** `DesktopShortcuts.tsx` replaces the two shortcut inputs when `shortcutHelp` returns a value (asked again when the keyboard implementation changes; on Linux neither the inputs nor the notice shows until the first answer, elsewhere the inputs show at once as before): the three commands as `select-all` monospace text, KDE's path (System Settings → Keyboard → Shortcuts → Add New → Command or Script; other desktops untested), that a press while Mouthy is not running only starts it, that a desktop dropping the arguments needs a two-line script, the Event Tap option and its `input`-group caveat, the password limitation, and the `wl-paste` found / not-found line. 11 keys under `settings.general.shortcut.desktop`, English in all 26 locales. "On Linux 'None' is recommended." is gone from the overlay description in all 26 locales.
- **Overlay default (D5):** `settings::is_stored` is read before the first `get_settings`; after `create_overlay`, a new store gets `overlay::first_overlay_style(layer shell active)`: `Minimal` under native Wayland with the layer shell (the rpm and deb on KDE, or the AppImage with `MOUTHY_GDK_BACKEND=wayland`), `None` elsewhere, including the AppImage's default XWayland. Existing stores are not touched.
- **Tray:** the tray build runs under `catch_unwind` (libappindicator-sys panics when no library loads); failure logs "Running without a tray icon: …", the window shows even with Start Hidden, `set_tray_visibility` copes with no tray, and off macOS closing the main window then quits, since the tray is the only other way to quit.
- **Single instance (D13):** the log plugin is registered right after single-instance (`log_plugin`), so a second process exits before it opens or rotates the log. On Linux the D-Bus callback returns as soon as the request is queued.
- **`GDK_BACKEND` (D6):** `utils::gdk_backend(in_appimage, MOUTHY_GDK_BACKEND)` sets it only inside the AppImage and only when asked; otherwise the hook's value stays (P4.2 decides). Linux logs the effective value at startup.
- **Deviations from the stage text:** (1) no Copy buttons and no `enable_clipboard_access`, as the orchestrator asked: the commands are selectable text. (2) `ShortcutHelp` has no `desktop`: one static line names KDE's path. (3) The command is `$APPIMAGE`, else the binary's name (not `current_exe`), double-quoted when it holds spaces or shell characters. (4) wl-clipboard 2.2 and later print `Nothing is copied` for an empty selection too, and `Clipboard content is not available as …` where 2.0 and 2.1 printed `No suitable type of content copied` (checked in the wl-clipboard sources for 2.0.0–2.3.0), so that wording also means empty. (5) Releases and files also go through the request thread; with only `request_speech` there, an Event Tap release could reach the main thread before its own press's session existed, and the next press would count as a held repeat and never stop the speech. (6) The guard and the request thread use `cfg!(target_os = "linux")`, not `is_wayland()`: on X11 the read fails at once anyway (D1). (7) `KeyboardImplementationSelector` labels stay as they were; switching to Event Tap without the `input` group already shows handy-keys' own error in a toast. (8) The tray `catch_unwind` and the plugin order apply on macOS too: the first only turns a crash on a failed tray build into a log line, the second only stops a second process opening the log. (9) `program` stays compiled on Linux and in tests only: on macOS nothing calls `run` yet, so compiling it everywhere would need an `allow(dead_code)`; the follow-up that moves `espeak::run` and `find_in` onto it deletes the `cfg` on `mod program`. (10) A voice preview still posts directly, so one started while a press is being read can be stopped by that press when it arrives (rare: the read takes milliseconds).
- **Bindings:** the debug binary's export ran with a throwaway `process::exit(0)` right after it, not committed, because the release Mouthy.app was running and the usual 20 s launch would have handed off to it and raised its window. The export reproduced the merged `bindings.ts` after each merge of `main`.
- **macOS unchanged:** `request_speech`, `hotkey_released`, `stop_speaking`, `speak_file` and the launch file post with `run_on_main_thread` exactly as before (same log lines); `STOPS` never changes there, so no request is dropped; `capture_on_main` is the old match (secret notice without an error line, an error logged then shown); the selection stays uncapped; `is_wayland()` is false, so the Tauri backend and the Settings inputs are as before, and Settings shows the inputs on its first render. The working-directory step and `host_environment` need `APPIMAGE`/`APPDIR`. Checked by reading the diff and by the full test suite; not run live (the release app holds the single instance).
- **Tests (run on the Mac too):** `program` (200 KiB read whole, past the cap stops the program, output cut short while a background helper holds both pipes, exact cap, `/bin/sleep` killed at the deadline, stderr kept and long stderr drained, missing program, `find` order and executable bit, `host_environment` with a fake `APPDIR`), `clipboard::wayland` against a stand-in `wl-paste` script written by a child process (so no exec hits "Text file busy"): arguments, plain-text type choice and HTML alone, the four empty wordings, other errors, hang, invalid UTF-8, the 80,000-byte cap, clipboard text after its types, the KDE hint as secret, empty and image-only clipboards; `RepeatGuard` (500 ms delay then 33 ms repeats, taps 800 ms apart, a quick tap, reset); the stop count; `is_wayland_session`; `gdk_backend`; `launch_dir`; `launch_command` and quoting; `first_overlay_style`.
- **Laptop checklist (Bazzite; for R6's checklist, and tier 2 if built from this branch).** Record the Plasma version first.
  1. Settings → General on Wayland shows the notice with the AppImage's absolute path and "Using wl-paste at /usr/bin/wl-paste".
  2. Bind the three commands in KDE; if a shortcut does nothing, bind the two-line scripts from the README instead and note it.
  3. Speak highlighted text in Konsole, Kate, Firefox and Dolphin, then press again to stop; note whether a press after clicking elsewhere reads the old highlight. Highlight a formatted passage in Firefox: no HTML tags are read.
  4. Copy text, speak-clipboard.
  5. Copy a password from the password manager and speak-clipboard: expect it read aloud unless the manager sets KDE's hint (a limitation to confirm, not pass or fail).
  6. Select all on a page over 64 KiB and speak: the first 20,000 characters, no hang.
  7. Highlight text in Mouthy's Settings, then speak-selection; copy a command from Settings with Ctrl+C, then speak-clipboard: both read within half a second, no freeze.
  8. Overlay default: move the settings store aside (`~/.local/share/com.azizi.mouthy/settings_store.json`, or the app-data folder Settings → About opens) and start the AppImage: the overlay setting is None. Move it aside again and start with `MOUTHY_GDK_BACKEND=wayland`: Minimal, at the screen's edge, without taking focus. Put the original store back.
  9. Tray present in the AppImage. No-tray start: `--appimage-extract`, delete `squashfs-root/usr/lib/libayatana-appindicator3.so.1`, run `squashfs-root/AppRun`: the window shows, the log says "Running without a tray icon", and closing the window quits Mouthy.
  10. `time` a `--stop`, and the time from key press to first audio.
  11. A second press stops; hold the key for 2 s: speech starts once and keeps going; taps about a second apart start and stop; SIGUSR2 (`pkill -USR2 -x mouthy`) speaks the selection, and a second one within 0.7 s is ignored.
  12. Rename `/usr/bin/wl-paste` away (or use a system without it): the notice and the Settings line say to install wl-clipboard; restore it.
  13. `MOUTHY_GDK_BACKEND=wayland` reaches GTK in the AppImage (the startup log line).
  14. With Mouthy running, press a shortcut a few times: the log file's inode is unchanged (`stat -c %i` before and after) and no start-up lines appear per press.
  15. AppImage child environment: `ldd /usr/bin/wl-paste` and `ldd /usr/bin/espeak-ng` with the AppImage's `LD_LIBRARY_PATH` (`squashfs-root/usr/lib`) to see what an unscrubbed child would load; while Mouthy reads the clipboard, if practical, `cat /proc/<wl-paste pid>/environ | tr '\0' '\n' | grep -E 'PATH|GTK|APPDIR'` shows no `/tmp/.mount_` entries.
  16. Relative `--speak-file`: from `~`, `~/Applications/Mouthy.AppImage --speak-file notes.txt` with Mouthy running, and again with it not running: both read `~/notes.txt`.
  17. Switch to Event Tap (Advanced → Experimental Features) and back to the Tauri backend: the notice goes and comes back; without the `input` group the toast shows handy-keys' error.
- **macOS regression (the user's Mac):** ⌥⎋ speak, stop, and hold on both shortcut backends; `--speak-selection`, `--speak-clipboard`, `--stop`; three `--speak-file` calls within half a second, each replacing the last; SIGUSR2 speaks and SIGUSR1 stops; tray Speak Selection and Stop; a voice preview; a password-manager secret on the clipboard shows the secret notice; Settings → General shows the two shortcut inputs at once; the log's inode is unchanged across twenty `--stop` calls.
- **Validation:** after the review fixes and merging `main` at `9019c960` (L3): on the Mac `cargo test` (388 pass, 9 ignored), `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo fmt --check`, `bun run lint`, `bun run format:check`, `bun run check:translations`, `bun run build` all pass; the bindings did not change in the review round. Linux check job green on `c9f910e7` (run 44136330405: 379 pass, 8 ignored, every new test included). An earlier Mac run had two `wl-paste` tests fail under load because they shared the 500 ms production deadline; they take their own since.

### Stage P4.2 — AppImage native-Wayland default `[fix]`

- Status: pending · Implementer: chief-implementer
- Blocked by: P4.1, smoke tier 2's native-Wayland result (H10)
- Files: `src-tauri/src/utils.rs` (the default inside `gdk_backend`), `README.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- What: if tier 2's hand-edited AppRun ran natively on Wayland with a working overlay, the AppImage default becomes `wayland` when `WAYLAND_DISPLAY` is set (`MOUTHY_GDK_BACKEND=x11` remains the way back). If it did not, this stage closes with no code and the README says the AppImage runs under XWayland, where the overlay default is `None`. Ghosting on the overlay card reopens the deferred WebKitGTK renderer port (and its H2 ask) instead.
- Verification: **Mac:** the `gdk_backend` table test. **Linux CI:** check job. **Laptop:** R6 checklist (startup log line; overlay at the edge on KDE).
- DoD: the default matches what the laptop showed, and the README says which.

**Implementation Notes:**

### Release R6 — 0.6.0, with `docs/phase4-checklist.md` `[chore]`

### Stage P2.3b — fix-ups from the P2.3 panel (after P2.4 commits) `[fix]`

Panel: simplicity 62%, integration 86%, correctness 86%, adversary 70%. Arbiter decisions:

**Do:** done 2026-10-14; see the stage's Implementation Notes (the ranked list is at `948c68d`).

**Rejected (with reason):** num2words swap — its output ("one hundred five", "nineteen oh-five") is worse for British voices and would need a new dep; withdrawn. Removing the cancel watcher — it is what makes stop→restart 321 ms, and keeps ORT off the main thread. Rebuild-all on change — P2.4 re-downloads rely on the file-stamp check. **H11 (new ask):** memory. Default applied: 150-token piece cap + README correction. Option for later: unload after N idle minutes (Handy had `model_unload_timeout`); unloading may not return memory to the OS (3 of 4 runs did not). Does the user want the idle unload too?

### Stage T1b — fix-ups from the adversary review of R1–R5 + T1 (runs after T2 commits) `[fix]`

Arbiter-accepted, one commit `fix(tts): harden http client and text normaliser; drop orphans` (two commits if the orphan sweep is cleaner separate):

- `tts/http.rs`: `client()` adds `.no_proxy()` (system proxies swallow loopback traffic; reproduced); non-2xx errors include the server's `detail.message` when the body is JSON (Kokoro returns it for wrong voice / "no speakable text"); `trim_base_url` also strips a trailing `/v1`; reqwest features → `["json"]` only.
- `tts/text.rs`: `strip_links` bounded — destination may not contain whitespace and `(` must immediately follow `]` (quadratic + text loss reproduced on JSON/code); text with no alphanumeric chars after normalize → "" (so `...`, `—`, emoji runs become nothing-selected); split on line breaks FIRST (a line without terminal punctuation is a sentence break — headings/bullets) then on `.!?…`, then collapse whitespace within each sentence; tiny abbreviation list (Dr., Mr., Mrs., Ms., St., vs., e.g., i.e., etc., U.S., U.K., a.m., p.m., month abbreviations) that does not end a sentence; 300-char fallback cuts only at `,;:` followed by whitespace, then re-merges fragments <20 chars; `unreachable_server_reports_base_url` binds port 0 and drops the listener instead of assuming port 9 is closed. Tests for each.
- T2 seam: a 400 whose message contains "no speakable text" skips that sentence instead of aborting the session.
- Orphans (brief: delete deps too): npm `react-select` + `@types/react-select`, `src/components/ui/Select.tsx`, `ui/Alert.tsx`, the `ui/Alert.tsx` mention in `src/styles/theme.css:18`, locale key `common.close` (26 locales), Cargo `rdev` (zero uses; pulls 29 crates incl. the `block` future-incompat warning), `windows` features `Win32_System_Com_StructuredStorage`, `Win32_System_Variant`, `Win32_System_Threading` (grep-verified unused; Windows not compiled — note it), Cargo comment `# Feedback sounds; output only.`, BUILD.md CMake requirement. Leave the fork-era unused npm packages (`@tauri-apps/plugin-autostart/-clipboard-manager/-global-shortcut/-store`, `zod`) and `store:default`/`global-shortcut:allow-*` capabilities for the T4 sweep (list them there).
- T3 wording list (from the adversary): onboarding permission descriptions ("type transcribed text", "a couple of permissions"), `accessibility.permissionsDescription`, `settings.general.shortcut.description`, `bindings.cancel.description`→speak, `settings.advanced.groups.transcription`, `overlay.position.description`, `settings.debug.onboardingPreview.description`, `overlay.transcribing`/`overlay.processing`, `about.acknowledgments.ggml.*`, 25 locales still saying "Live"/"feedback sounds", `src/overlay/index.html` title + overlay window title `overlay.rs:382,427`.
- 2026-10-12 — Adversary review of R1–R5+T1: 78%; all items above accepted; rejected none. Prosody suggestions beyond the abbreviation list and line-break rule (merge symmetry) deferred to Phase 3 polish.
- Implementation Notes:
  - **Decisions:** `normalize` collapses whitespace per line and keeps `\n` between non-empty lines; `split_sentences` runs `lines()` → `raw_sentences` → per-sentence whitespace collapse → `merge_short` → `split_long` per sentence → `merge_short` again on that sentence's pieces only (so a short "Well," head joins the next piece, never the previous sentence). Pieces can therefore reach about `MAX + MIN` (≈ 320) chars. Abbreviation check: the last whitespace-separated word of the candidate sentence, leading non-alphanumerics trimmed, compared case-insensitively. A link `[` (and an image `!`) must not follow an alphanumeric, `_`, `]` or `)`: `handlers[0](event)` and `grid[0][1](x)` stay; `Great![1](url)` → `Great!1` (the `!` is punctuation there). The label ends at the first `[`, `]` or newline; the destination ends at `)` or whitespace (whitespace → not a link). `replace_urls` gained an `h`/`w` first-letter gate: it was 18 ms of a 40 KB debug run. The unspeakable check lives in `tts::http::is_unspeakable` (400 + "no speakable text", Kokoro's `check_speakable` message is "Input contains no speakable text") so `actions.rs` holds no server strings; a skipped sentence logs at `debug`. Error format: `TTS server returned {status}: {detail.message}` when the body has `detail.message`, else as before (FastAPI 422s have a list `detail` and keep the plain form).
  - **Deviations:** two commits (fix, then chore). Kept `strip_links`' text-loss fix to the bounded parser (no extra stop at `[` inside a destination, so IPv6 URLs still parse); a no-whitespace run of `[a](b[a](b…` without `)` is still quadratic, which normal text never produces.
  - **Learnings:** the old normaliser took 65 ms and dropped 28 chars on the test blob (1,000 JSON objects + one link); the new one takes ~5 ms. Line-break splitting splits hard-wrapped prose (email, PDF copy) mid-sentence when both halves are ≥ 20 chars — accepted per the arbiter, worth a look in Phase 3 prosody polish. `windows` features verified unused by grepping every `windows::` import (`overlay.rs`, `lib.rs`, `paste_tx/windows.rs`); Windows is not compiled here (only `aarch64-apple-darwin` installed). Tests 72 → 83 (+11). Warnings unchanged: 31, all R6. Crates 736 → 708 after dropping `rdev`.
  - **Live test:** Kokoro came back during the stage (hung at first, not touched). `cargo test --lib -- --ignored synthesize_against_local_server` passes through the `no_proxy` client: 77,693 samples (3.24 s) in 0.36 s. Probed `...`, `—`, `🎉🎉` and `" "`: each returns 400 `{"detail":{"error":"validation_error","message":"Input contains no speakable text",…}}`, the shape `error_message`/`is_unspeakable` expect.
  - **Commit note:** the `ui/Select.tsx`/`ui/Alert.tsx` deletions are in the chore commit `7d04119`.
  - **Grep verification:** zero hits outside PLAN.md for `\brdev\b`, `react-select`, `ui/Select`, `ui/Alert`, `Feedback sounds`, `Win32_System_Variant`, `Win32_System_Threading`, `Com_StructuredStorage`, `CMake`/`cmake`; no `common.close`/`"close":` in `src`; `cargo tree --target all -i rdev|cmake|block` → no match.

### Stage T2b — speak-action simplification + panel fixes (after T1b) `[refactor]`

Arbiter rulings on the T2 panel (correctness 85% / integration 90% / simplicity 64%; findings in the agents' reports, summarised here):

- ACCEPT (simplicity M1): all `TtsState` transitions run on the main thread. Public `speak_selection`/`stop_speaking`/`SessionGuard::drop` only post a closure via `run_on_main_thread` (inner `*_on_main` fns so nothing re-posts; the lock is only ever taken on main, so nothing it waits on can hold it). Capture is synchronous on main; `Session` is created only after capture succeeds. Delete `Phase`, `ui_seq`/`ui_applied`/`post_ui`/`apply_ui`, `generation`/`last_generation`; identity = `Arc::ptr_eq` on the stop flag. Consequence (accepted): a press during the ≤250 ms capture queues behind it and then stops the speech (toggle) — this also fixes correctness S2 (press-during-capture dropped under handy-keys). D10/D12 wording amended accordingly.
- ACCEPT (simplicity M2): one serde-tagged `OverlayView { Speaking, NothingSelected, Error{message} }` emitted on the existing `show-overlay` event; delete `OverlayNotice` (Rust+TS), the second listener, `show_overlay`'s label/closure params; auto-hide derived from the variant.
- ACCEPT (correctness S1): on a synth `Err`, wait for `player.empty()` (or stop) before breaking so already-synthesised sentences play out.
- ACCEPT: synth-thread panic → send `Err("synthesis failed")` via an unwind guard so the user sees a notice instead of silent truncation; clamp `tts_speed`/`tts_volume` on read in the pipeline (hand-edited stores); non-macOS `capture_selection` becomes a stub returning `Err("selection capture is macOS-only")` (compile only); `input.rs` `command_key(ch, ansi_fallback)` and delete `ansi_keycode` + the `None` arm; delete the `queued` counter/logs; `signal_handle.rs` fixed log string; `sync_channel(READ_AHEAD)`; AGENTS.md lines 55/81/132/136 corrected; CHANGELOG "Removed: macOS SIGUSR1 trigger" line reconciled with SIGUSR1→stop.
- ACCEPT if verified: `audio_out` uses `.with_buffer_size(BufferSize::Default).open_stream()` instead of `open_sink_or_fallback` ONLY if a probe on the Bluetooth device on this Mac succeeds; otherwise keep the fallback.
- REJECT: collapsing the `ShortcutAction` trait / `ACTION_MAP` (brief: keep Handy's conventions; Phase 3 may add actions); moving the `change_tts_*`/`fetch_tts_voices` commands to T3 (T3 is next; churn for nothing); replacing the OS-thread auto-hide with an async timer (cosmetic).
- DEFER: device-stall detection (no rodio error callback) → Phase 3; overlay `present` race after `hide-overlay` → T3 rewrite; `alt+escape` conflicts on Windows/Linux → README note at T4; settings UI showing unclamped values until refresh → T3 (emit or re-read after `change_tts_*`).
- Tests (chief-tester after T2b): pasteboard restore-only-if-unchanged gating (extract the decision into a pure fn), drain-on-error ordering (fake chunk source), `OverlayView` serde tag shape, `idle_and_busy_menus_differ` first assertion is tautological (fix).
- Re-verification required after T2b (needs Kokoro up): CLI speak → completion, `--stop`, toggle, SIGUSR1/2, dead-port notice, stop-during-capture.
- Implementation Notes:
  - **Decisions:** Public `actions::speak_selection(app, source)` / `stop_speaking(app, source)` only post `speak_selection_on_main` / `stop_speaking_on_main` (inline when already on main: Tauri-backend hotkey, tray, sync commands). `TtsState` is locked only inside the `*_on_main` fns, and each lock is released before any overlay/tray call, so a hypothetical run-loop re-entry could not self-deadlock either. Session order: capture → normalise/split → spawn playback then synth (`start_speech`) → insert `Session { stop }` → `Speaking` overlay + tray. A spawn failure sets the flag and shows `Error`; the already-spawned playback thread's guard is then a no-op. `SessionGuard` is built inside the playback thread (never on main), sets the flag and posts `end_session_on_main(app, &stop, error)`, which acts only if `Arc::ptr_eq` matches the current session. The loop is `play_chunks(&Player, &Receiver, &AtomicBool) -> Option<String>`; on `Err` and on `Disconnected` it calls `play_out` (wait for `player.empty()` or stop) before returning. `ReportPanic(&tx)` sends `Err("synthesis failed")` from its `Drop` when `thread::panicking()`; the send blocks only until playback consumes or drops the receiver. `tts_speed`/`tts_volume` clamped in `start_speech`. `OverlayView` (`#[serde(tag = "kind", rename_all = "kebab-case")]`) serialises to `{"kind":"speaking"}`, `{"kind":"nothing-selected"}`, `{"kind":"error","message":…}`; `show_overlay(app, view)` and `show_overlay_state_on_main` are back to the pre-T2 Handy body shape plus the show-generation snapshot and `OverlayView::auto_hide` (2 s / 6 s). Kept the `source` argument on the public fns (log lines). `collect_commands!` unchanged, so no bindings export.
  - **Deviations:** (1) Tray test: idle and busy menus have the same item ids (Stop's enabled flag differs), so the tautological `inputs(false) != inputs(true)` became `!Idle.is_busy() && Speaking.is_busy()` — the Stop-enabled mapping. (2) Wrote the drain-on-error (`synthesis_error_waits_for_queued_audio_to_play`, `stop_ends_the_wait_for_queued_audio`, against `Player::new()` with the test pulling samples as the device), `synth_panic_reaches_playback_as_an_error` and `overlay_view_is_tagged_by_kind` tests here; chief-tester still owns the pasteboard gating test. Mutation check: replacing `play_out` with `player.stop()` on `Err` fails both drain tests. (3) `update_overlay_position`'s comment named the T2-renamed `show_overlay_state`; it now names `show_overlay`.
  - **`BufferSize::Default` probe (before the change):** scratch crate on rodio 0.23.0 (offline). Default output "OpenRun Pro 2 by Shokz" (Bluetooth, 44.1 kHz, buffer range 14–1024): fixed 50 ms buffer → "Error opening the stream with the OS"; `with_buffer_size(BufferSize::Default).open_stream()` → opens, 0.5 s of silence drained in 510 ms. All six outputs open with `Default` (GF400B, Shokz, Yeti, MacBook Pro Speakers, Microsoft Teams Audio, ZoomAudioDevice); the fixed buffer fails on Shokz and ZoomAudioDevice. `open_sink_or_fallback` removed.
  - **Edge cases:** press while speaking → stop (verified). Second press during capture → queued, starts, then stops 6 ms later (verified, SIGUSR2 ×2 50 ms apart). Stop during capture → runs right after the session starts, no chunk appended, overlay flashes Speaking then fades (accepted) (verified ×3, SIGUSR1 40 ms after SIGUSR2). Stop with no session → hides the overlay (dismisses a notice), tray stays idle (verified). Nothing copied → `NothingSelected`, `changeCount` untouched (verified). Server unreachable → `Error` (verified). Synth 500 after sentence 1 → sentence 1 plays to the end, then `Error` (verified). Stop during that play-out → immediate, error dropped because the session already ended (verified). Synth panic → same as a synth error with "synthesis failed" (verified). Old guard posting after stop + immediate restart → its `end_session_on_main` ran 3 ms after the new session started and did nothing; the new session played on (verified). Capture error, thread-spawn failure, device-open failure, playback-thread panic (ends as Idle, as in T2), hand-edited out-of-range speed/volume → code-read only. A backend delivering one press twice would now start then stop (the T2 "ignore while capturing" debounce is gone, accepted by the ruling); not observed — signals merge and both hotkey backends deliver once.
  - **Failure and recovery:** no persisted state. The clipboard is restored inside capture, before any session exists, so a crash mid-speech leaves nothing to clean up. If the event loop is gone (shutdown), posts fail and are logged; nothing waits on them.
  - **Verification:** `cargo build` 31 warnings (`input.rs` 4, `clipboard.rs` 8, `paste_tx/` 19); `cargo test` 87 pass / 1 ignored (83 → 87), actions tests stable over 8 reruns; `cargo clippy --all-targets` 36 warnings before and after, all dead code; `bun run lint`, `build`, `check:translations`, `format:check` clean. Runtime: debug binary from `src-tauri`, temporary env-gated spike (capture's ⌘C replaced by `pbcopy` of a file's text 120 ms later, so the real `NSPasteboard` snapshot/poll/restore ran on main; ms-timestamp logs; file-gated synth panic), reverted with checksums matching and the binary rebuilt without it. CLI speak of 3 sentences (11,129 ms of audio) → first append to playback exit 11,133 ms, so it played to completion in real time on the Shokz via `BufferSize::Default`; `--stop` → playback thread out in 16 ms; toggle → 3 ms; SIGUSR2 → speak, SIGUSR1 → stop in 21 ms; dead port, synth-500 and synth-panic runs used a throwaway Python proxy in front of Kokoro (store pointed at it, then restored byte-identical). The user's clipboard digest (both types) matched the pre-run backup after every capture and at the end. **Not verified:** real ⌘C capture, the ⌥⎋ keypress on either backend, the 2 s hold, tray clicks (shell not Accessibility-trusted); overlay rendering (no Vite dev server; payload shape covered by the serde test and `tsc`); notice auto-hide timing (no log line); Windows/Linux compile — the stub leaves `COPY_*`, non-test `poll` and `input::send_copy` unused there (about five `dead_code` warnings expected).
  - **Grep verification:** zero hits in `src`, `src-tauri/src`, AGENTS.md, CHANGELOG.md for `show-overlay-notice`, `OverlayNotice`, `show_overlay_notice`, `show_speaking_overlay`, `post_ui`, `apply_ui`, `ui_seq`, `ui_applied`, `last_generation`, `Phase::`, `capture_and_speak`, `ansi_keycode`, `open_sink_or_fallback`, `SPIKE`. `block_on` only at `actions.rs` (synth thread) and `tts/http.rs` tests.

### Stage T3b — capture hardening from the T2/T2b adversary review (after T3) `[fix]`

Arbiter rulings (adversary 70%; findings in its report):

- ACCEPT (item 1, ⌥ held during ⌘C): before injecting ⌘C, wait up to 300 ms for the hardware modifier state to show no ⌥/⌃/⇧ (poll `NSEvent::modifierFlags()` class method from `objc2-app-kit` — already a dep — every 10 ms), then send regardless. Rationale: handy-keys' session tap sees our synthetic C/⌘-up without Alternate, drops OPT and treats the hotkey as released, so a held ⌥⎋ can re-fire on autorepeat (start→stop); and if WindowServer merges held ⌥ the apps receive ⌥⌘C. The user's Hammerspoon PoC used the same chord, which suggests no merge, but the tap interaction is new. Add a 2 s hold + re-tap test to the T4 manual matrix on BOTH backends.
- ACCEPT (item 2, partial): re-read `changeCount` after the snapshot and retry the snapshot once if it moved; cap total snapshot bytes (16 MB) — above the cap, skip restore and log a warning; log snapshot duration at debug. DEFER skipping derived types (TIFF/icns) to Phase 3.
- ACCEPT (item 3): if Mouthy itself is the active app (`NSApp.isActive`), do not inject ⌘C — show NothingSelected.
- ACCEPT (item 4): auto-hide performs a single main-thread `hide_if_current(generation)` (compare-and-hide atomically on main) instead of check-then-post.
- ACCEPT (item 5a): if every sentence was skipped as unspeakable (zero chunks sent), show NothingSelected instead of ending silently.
- DEFER: 60 s timeout edge at speed 0.5 on slow servers (Phase 2 makes it moot); `AXSelectedText` fast path (Phase 3). README (T4): `--speak-selection` with no running instance just launches the app.
- Tests: `restore_decision` unchanged; add a unit test for the modifier-wait helper's timeout bound if factored purely; snapshot byte-cap decision as a pure fn + test.
- Verification-matrix corrections recorded: A7 is code-read only; A3 proves registration not delivery; A11 bound excludes snapshot/hold/restore; overlay never rendered before T3.
- Implementation Notes:
  - **Decisions:** macOS `clipboard::capture_selection` (`clipboard.rs:42`) runs, in order: the main-thread check (moved up from `pasteboard::capture`; it needs the marker for `NSApplication`), `NSApplication::sharedApplication(mtm).isActive()` → `Ok(None)` + info log (`:51`), the modifier wait, then `pasteboard::capture`. The wait (`wait_for_modifier_release`, `:85`) reuses `poll` with `NSEvent::modifierFlags_class` as the injected probe: 10 ms interval, `MODIFIER_RELEASE_TIMEOUT` 300 ms (`:34`), elapsed logged at debug (warn on timeout). `modifiers_clear` (`:76`) looks only at ⌥/⌃/⇧, so ⌘ and Caps Lock (a toggle) never delay the copy. It runs before the snapshot so the snapshot→⌘C gap stays as short as before. `+[NSEvent modifierFlags]` returns device state combined with synthesized events, independent of the event stream (Apple docs), so it updates while our main thread is blocked. `pasteboard::save` (`:204`): read `changeCount`, snapshot, re-read; if it moved, snapshot again from the new count (`SNAPSHOT_ATTEMPTS = 2`); the copy is measured from the last count read. `snapshot` (`:182`) stops as soon as the running byte total passes `SNAPSHOT_CAP_BYTES` (16 MiB) via the pure `add_within_cap` (`:175`, checked add). Either failure yields `Err(reason)`: the copy still runs, and where a restore would have happened it warns `Not restoring the clipboard: {reason}. The copied selection stays on it.` (`:283`). `restore_decision` unchanged. Each snapshot logs items, bytes and duration at debug. `overlay::hide_if_current_after(app, generation, delay, hide)` (`overlay.rs:676`): the sleeping thread only posts; the generation compare and the hide both run on main, where every show bumps the generation. `play_chunks` returns `PlaybackEnd { Done, NothingSpoken, Failed(String) }` (`actions.rs:235`) instead of `Option<String>`; `NothingSpoken` = sender gone, no chunk appended, not stopped; `end_session_on_main` shows `NothingSelected` for it (info "No sentence had speakable text"). No Cargo change: `objc2-app-kit` is declared with default features, which include `NSEvent` and `NSApplication`.
  - **Deviations:** (1) `hide_overlay`'s 300 ms fade-end `window.hide()` also goes through `hide_if_current_after`. The notice auto-hide calls `hide_overlay`, whose old off-main check-then-hide had the same race one step later, so item 4 was not closed without it. `hide_overlay` is documented main-thread-only (all callers already were). (2) If `changeCount` also moves during the second snapshot, that snapshot may be torn: it is not restored (reason "it kept changing while being saved") rather than writing a mix back. (3) The cap stops reading at the type that crosses 16 MiB instead of reading everything first; nothing over the cap is restored anyway. (4) The modifier-wait test injects the flags reader and a short timeout instead of a clock, reusing the already-tested `poll`.
  - **Edge cases:** no modifier held → one read, 2–9 µs (verified). ⌥ held past 300 ms → ⌘C sent anyway with a warn (unit test only; needs a human). Caps Lock on → no wait (unit test). Mouthy frontmost → no ⌘C, NothingSelected, `changeCount` untouched (verified). Tray Speak Selection → status-item menus do not activate the app and `tray-icon` never calls `activate`, so the check does not fire (code-read). `isActive` lags an app switch by one event-loop turn; a hotkey within that turn gets the stale answer (accepted, human-scale gap). Clipboard changed mid-snapshot → retaken, copy measured from the new count, retaken data restored (verified). Changed again during the retake → no restore (code-read). Over 16 MiB → gives up after 2.7 ms on 17 MB, copy runs, no restore, warn, selection left on the clipboard (verified). Over cap but nothing selected → clipboard untouched, no warn (code-read via `NoCopy`). Empty clipboard → empty snapshot, restore writes nothing (unchanged). Every sentence unspeakable → Speaking flash for the requests, then NothingSelected (verified). Some skipped, some spoken → `Done` (unit test via chunk count). Stopped with nothing queued → `Done`, and the session is already gone (unit test). Notice then speak within 2 s → auto-hide skipped, speaking overlay stays (verified).
  - **Failure and recovery:** no persisted state. Over the cap or torn, the user's clipboard ends holding the selection (accepted by the ruling for the cap). Posted hides fail silently once the event loop is gone, as before.
  - **Verification:** `cargo build` 0 compiler warnings; `cargo test` 92 pass / 1 ignored (86 → 92: +5 clipboard, +1 actions; clipboard+actions stable over 6 reruns); `cargo clippy --all-targets` clean; `bun run format:check` clean. No bindings change. Runtime: debug binary from `src-tauri`, temporary env-gated spike (⌘C replaced by `pbcopy` of a file 120 ms later; a second flag runs `pbcopy` between the first snapshot and the `changeCount` re-read), reverted with checksums matching, rebuilt clean. **Kokoro hung on a direct `中文` probe before the app runs** (see the known-issue line under T3c), so a throwaway local server stood in (silent PCM, or 400 "no speakable text"); store pointed at it, restored byte-identical (`983b9ad4…`). Window visibility from `CGWindowListCopyWindowInfo` polled every 20 ms. Verified: modifier wait logs `Modifier keys up after` 2.4–9.4 µs; `Saved the clipboard: 1 items, 47 bytes in 0.14–0.68 ms`; CLI speak → 3 sentences requested, overlay on, `--stop` → window starts closing ~0.3 s later through the new main-thread hide (gone from the list by ~0.6 s, after the system's close animation); SIGUSR2/SIGUSR1 same; notice + speak 1 s later → `Skipping stale overlay hide: the overlay was shown again`, overlay stayed 5 s until stop; all-unspeakable → NothingSelected, window starts closing ~2.4 s later (2 s auto-hide + 0.3 s fade); 17 MB clipboard → warn, selection left; torn write → retake (10 B), copy detected, retaken data restored; Mouthy frontmost (`System Events` set frontmost; a plain relaunch shows the window but macOS does not activate it) → `Mouthy is the active app…`, no copy, no request. User clipboard digest (`81a03aa2…`) matched after every restoring run and was put back from a full backup after the cap and torn runs. **Not verified:** a physically held modifier (needs a human; T4 hold + re-tap test), real ⌘C injection and the hotkey on either backend (shell not Accessibility-trusted), tray click while another app is front, real Kokoro speech after this change (server hung), overlay rendering (no Vite), Windows/Linux compile.
  - **Learnings:** a UserNotificationCenter alert (running since 10 Oct, 260×302) was frontmost during the runs; left alone. `date +%N` is GNU-only on macOS. `+[NSApplication isActive]` stays false for a hidden debug launch.
  - **Grep verification:** zero hits in `src-tauri/src` for `SPIKE`, `guard.error`, `self.error`, `window_clone`, `MainThreadMarker::new().is_none`; `block_on` still only in `actions.rs` (synth thread) and `tts/http.rs` tests.

### Stage T5 — held-hotkey fix, privacy wording, checklist (final adversary, 72%) `[fix]`

- ACCEPT (finding 1, held ⌥⎋ under handy-keys): track `released_since_start` on the session. The hotkey backends deliver key-release events to `SpeakAction::stop`; a release that arrives within 150 ms after our ⌘C injection is the synthetic ⌘-up echo and is ignored; a HOTKEY press toggles stop only when `released_since_start` is true. Tray / CLI / signal / overlay stops stay unconditional. Rationale: handy-keys suppresses autorepeat only while it believes the hotkey is held; our synthetic ⌘-up makes it believe it was released, so each autorepeat becomes a new Pressed. Works for both backends (Tauri delivers Pressed/Released too). No dependency change.
- ACCEPT (finding 2): README + `settings.speech.backendUrl.description` state that selected text is sent to the configured server and plain http is unencrypted; drop `{sentence:?}` from the skip log (`actions.rs:~209`) — log the sentence length instead.
- ACCEPT (finding 3): relabel the keyboard-implementation option "Handy Keys" → "Event tap" (the crate name stays in code; label is user-facing).
- ACCEPT (finding 4): fix the stale commit ref in PLAN.md (Select/Alert deletions are in `7d04119`); checklist additions: overlay hides after a natural finish, changing the hotkey, voice/speed/volume take effect, CLI flags + signals against the bundled binary, launch at login; step 7 also in VS Code (⌥⌘C would read a file path); step 1 label reads "Option + Escape"; CHANGELOG: "OpenAI-compatible server that supports `pcm` output" instead of "any".
- DECISION: version → `0.1.0` in `tauri.conf.json`/`Cargo.toml`/`package.json` (new app, Handy's 0.9.8 is meaningless here).
- Noted, not changed: `2d1e275` and `7ac6f15` each bundle several small removals; `cfg_attr(not(macos), allow(dead_code))` on the two testable helpers is accepted.
- Implementation Notes:
  - **Decisions:** `SpeakAction::start` → `request_speech(app, shortcut, Trigger::Hotkey)`; tray, CLI and SIGUSR2 keep `speak_selection` (`Trigger::Other`). `SpeakAction::stop` → `hotkey_released(app)`, which reads `Instant::now()` on the calling thread (the handy-keys manager thread, or main inline for Tauri) before posting `hotkey_released_on_main`, so a capture holding up the main thread does not shift the stamp. `Session { stop, released_since_start, copied_while_held_at }`; `Session::note_release` ignores a release within `COPY_ECHO_WINDOW` (150 ms, inclusive) after `copied_while_held_at` (`is_copy_echo`), else sets `released_since_start`. A press while a session exists stops only if `should_toggle_stop(released_since_start, trigger)`; an ignored press logs debug `Ignoring a repeat while held (<shortcut>)`, an ignored release `Ignoring the hotkey release echoed by our ⌘C`. `clipboard::capture_selection` returns `Selection { text, copied_while_held_at }`: the instant is read just before `send_copy` and kept only when the modifier wait timed out. Skip log: `Skipping a sentence with no speakable text (N chars)`. Delivery confirmed by reading the sources: handy-keys 0.3.4 `ManagerState::process_event` emits `Released` on the hotkey key's key-up, or on a modifier-only event whose modifiers no longer match; enigo 0.6.1 sets each synthetic event's flags from its own state (⌘ only), so the tap's `reconcile_modifiers` drops ⌥ at the C key-down and the ⌘-up then releases a held ⌥⎋; handy-keys does not filter autorepeat, so the next ⎋ repeat (flags with ⌥) is a fresh `Pressed` (code-read, not observed). Tauri: global-hotkey 0.8 installs Carbon handlers for `kEventHotKeyPressed` and `kEventHotKeyReleased`, the plugin calls the handler with `ShortcutState::Released`, and `tauri_impl.rs` passes `is_pressed = false` → `action.stop`. Both backends deliver releases, so the gate is the same for both.
  - **Deviations:** (1) The echo window opens only for a ⌘C sent with ⌥, ⌃ or ⇧ still down, not after every copy. With every copy, real quick-tap releases fall inside it: on the Tauri backend almost always (the Carbon release is handled on main after the capture, typically 20–80 ms after the copy), and on handy-keys whenever the ⎋ or ⌥ release lands within the 10 ms polling jitter of the copy (the modifier wait copies right after ⌥ comes up). The next tap would then be ignored. If ⌥/⌃/⇧ are up when ⌘C goes out, handy-keys has already released the hotkey, so there is no echo to ignore. (2) The label is "Event Tap", title case like its sibling "Tauri Global Shortcut"; still a hard-coded technical name (eslint's `markupOnly` rule does not flag option labels). (3) README's requirement line also says `pcm` output. (4) AGENTS.md's speak-loop line mentions the release gate. (5) `bun.lock` carries no version, so only `Cargo.lock` changed. (6) The `backendUrl.description` change overwrote the 25 other locales with the new English text; they all held the English value already.
  - **Edge cases:** held ⌥⎋ on handy-keys → wait times out at 300 ms, ⌘C goes out with ⌥ down, its ⌘-up echo (~30–45 ms later) is ignored, the ⎋ repeat it lets through is ignored, the real release counts, a later tap stops (spike A). Quick tap → ⌥ up before the copy, no window, the release counts even 50 ms after the copy (spike B); under Tauri the late-handled release counts the same way. Step 7 (⌥ held, ⎋ tapped) → ⎋'s key-up comes before the 300 ms timeout, so it is stamped before the copy and counts (unit test). Hold of about 300–450 ms → the real release falls in the window and is ignored, so stopping takes one extra tap (accepted; bounded). ⌘- or fn-based bindings (the wait ignores ⌘ and fn) → no window; a quick tap works, but holding past the key-repeat delay can still start then stop, as before T5. Tray Speak Selection, `--speak-selection`, SIGUSR2 while speaking → stop regardless; `--stop`, SIGUSR1, tray Stop, overlay × → unchanged (spike C for the CLI and signals). Release with no session → no-op. Speech ends while the key is held → session gone; nothing to gate. Two real taps during one capture → release 1 counts, press 2 stops after the capture (T2b behaviour kept). Secure Input fallback switching between a press and its release → the release may never arrive; one extra tap.
  - **Failure and recovery:** no persisted state. The gate only ever ignores a hotkey press; the worst case is one extra tap, and tray Stop, overlay ×, `--stop` and SIGUSR1 always stop.
  - **Verification:** `cargo build` 0 compiler warnings; `cargo test` 96 pass / 1 ignored (92 → 96: `should_toggle_stop` table, echo window bounds incl. 150/151 ms and release-before-copy, the held sequence through `Session`, quick tap with no window), actions tests stable over 6 reruns; `cargo clippy --all-targets` clean; `bun run lint`, `build`, `check:translations`, `format:check` green. No bindings export: `collect_commands!` unchanged. Runtime: Kokoro hung (`curl -m 3 …/v1/audio/voices` timed out), so a throwaway server on 127.0.0.1:8899 served 2 s of silent PCM per sentence; store pointed at it, restored byte-identical (`189f1fec…`); clipboard digest unchanged after every run. Temporary env-gated spike in the debug binary (reverted, checksums matched, rebuilt): `MOUTHY_SPIKE_COPY` replaced ⌘C with `pbcopy` of a file 120 ms later, `MOUTHY_SPIKE_HELD` made the modifier wait report keys still down, `MOUTHY_SPIKE_SEQ` made a CLI speak start a thread that called `ACTION_MAP["speak"].stop/start` (the handy-keys thread's calls) at fixed offsets; stdout timestamped with Perl `Time::HiRes`. Spike A (held): release 54 ms after the copy, posted while main was still capturing and handled 188 ms after the copy → ignored as the echo; press at +450 ms → `Ignoring a repeat while held`, speech went on (audio appended at +700 ms, after the Bluetooth device opened); release at +1004 ms counted; press at +1205 ms → `Stopping speech`, playback thread out 51 ms later. Spike B (modifiers up): release 52 ms after the copy counted, press at +450 ms stopped. Spike C (held flag on, no hotkey events): `--stop` 1.4 s in, a second `--speak-selection`, and SIGUSR2 then SIGUSR1 each spoke or stopped at once. Bundle: `bun run tauri build --bundles app` 1m17s, `CFBundleShortVersionString` 0.1.0, 9.6 MB, release binary 9,905,968 B; alive at 5 s, quit via osascript, process gone. **Not verified:** a physical held ⌥⎋ on either backend and the real echo timing (shell not Accessibility-trusted; DoD steps 6 and 7), Carbon's behaviour on our synthetic ⌘-up, the label in the real Settings window.
  - **Learnings:** `if let Some(x) = state.lock().as_mut()` as a fn's tail expression fails to borrow-check (E0597: the guard temporary outlives the `State` local); bind the result in a `let` first, which also drops the lock before logging. `6a717b8` exists only as an unreachable object; main carries `2467822` for the T2b docs commit.
  - **Grep verification:** zero hits in `src-tauri/src` for `SPIKE`, `{sentence:?}`; zero `Handy Keys` in `src`, `docs`, README; `0.9.8` only in PLAN.md.
